# Vibe — code map

Where things live and which file to open for a given change. Written for an
AI agent (or a person) who has to change something quickly without reading
the whole code base. Keep it short and true: when a file is split, renamed
or gets a new job, update the matching line here in the same commit.

## Shape of the project

```
src/main.cpp                 command line, log, creates the engine
src/game/                    gameplay that knows nothing about Vulkan
  level.cpp                    the castle: walls, towers, keep, stairs, colliders
  player.cpp/.h                movement, crouch, eye position
  collision.cpp, physics.cpp   AABB collision for the player
src/anim/                    character animation library (no Vulkan): see docs/animation/guide.md
src/engine/                  engine modules that are NOT the VulkanEngine class
  terrain.cpp, terrain_clod.cpp  height map, CPU twins of the terrain shader
  world_props.cpp              tree / bush / rock archetypes + where they stand
  world_props_meshes.inl       ... their mesh builders (conifer, broadleaf, shrub, rock)
  scatter.cpp                  small plants + loose stones: placement, buffers
  scatter_plant_meshes.inl     ... their meshes (fern, flowers, reed, water lily, ...)
  veg_paint.h                  the editor's paint map + presets
  road.cpp, grass.cpp, audio.cpp, physics_world.cpp (Jolt), texture.cpp, window.cpp
src/engine/vk_engine.h       the one big class: every member + method declaration
src/engine/vk_engine.cpp     init(), run() (the frame loop), shutdown()
src/engine/vk_engine/game_loop.cpp   one turn of run(), the game half: loop_test_input, loop_handle_keys,
                             loop_weapons_and_edit, loop_simulate, loop_upkeep (called in that order)
src/engine/vk_engine/state/*.h       private state of 25 subsystems (see "Private state" below)
src/engine/vk_engine/        the class's methods, one topic per file (see below)
shaders/                     GLSL 4.60; *.glsl = includes, compiled into the others
tools/                       scripts (character build, rock bake, split_shader.pl, split_cpp.pl, perf/bench.sh)
docs/                        this file, design notes, screenshots
```

Everything is in `namespace vibe`. Runtime files live in the project root:
`vibe_settings.cfg` (all settings + player pose, restored at start),
`vibe.log`, `screenshots/` (F12).

| Topic | Files |
|---|---|
| Frame: order of passes, submit, present | `frame.cpp`, `frame_upload.cpp` |
| Drawing the world (depth pre-pass + colour pass) | `world_render.cpp` |

| Ray tracing (BLAS / TLAS, instance masks) | `rt.cpp` |
| Deferred lighting, GI caches, denoise | `deferred_lighting.cpp`, `radiance_cache.cpp`, `restir.cpp`, `svgf.cpp`, `rr_guides.cpp` |
| Upscaling / frame generation | `dlss.cpp`, `fsr2.cpp`, `fsr3.cpp`, `taa.cpp` |
| Post: bloom, exposure, compose | `bloom.cpp`, `post.cpp`; metering, AgX look, local tone mapping, glare, motion blur, depth of field, colour-blind view, smoke from the fog volume, tracks in snow: `post_fx.cpp` ("Post effects" below) |
| Sky, clouds, froxel fog (incl. lake mist) | `atmosphere.cpp` |
| Day / night, weather, rain, lightning | `environment.cpp` |

## Shaders

| Shader | What it draws | Its includes |
|---|---|---|
| `cube.frag` (+ `cube.vert`) | every mesh: castle walls, towers, props, trees, mesh terrain. Built 3x: forward, `cube_gbuf` (G-buffer only, the normal case), `cube_gbuf_terrain` | `cube_surface_helpers.glsl`, `cube_noise_rays.glsl`, `cube_wall_relief.glsl`, `cube_main_terrain.glsl`, `cube_main_surfaces.glsl`, `cube_main_forward_lighting.glsl` |
| `terrain_raymarch.frag` | the raymarched ground AND the water (built twice, `VIBE_WATER`) | `terrain_rm_noise_road.glsl`, `terrain_rm_pebbles.glsl`, `terrain_rm_heightfield.glsl`, `terrain_rm_shading_helpers.glsl`, `terrain_rm_water.glsl` |
| `deferred_lighting.frag` | sun, shadows, AO, GI for everything in the G-buffer | |
| `grass_raymarch.frag` | the lawn (raymarched blades) | `grass_blades.glsl` |
| `scatter.vert/.frag` | small plants, flowers, loose stones, water lilies | `rock_sdf.glsl` |
| `veg_cull.comp` | tree culling + LOD on the GPU | |
| `froxel_fog.comp` | volumetric fog, lake mist | |
| `compose.frag` | sky fill, fog apply, tonemap, final image | |
| `scene_ubo.glsl` | the scene uniform block shared by all | |

`*_main_*.glsl` files are pieces of one `main()` included in place: they share
its local variables and are not self-contained.

## "I want to change ..." -> open this

| Change | File(s) |
|---|---|
| Castle layout, wall sizes, stairs, tower colliders | `src/game/level.cpp` |
| The chapel (shape) | `tools/church/make_church.py` (run in Blender, writes `assets/church/church.mesh`); its materials + colliders: `append_church` in `level.cpp`; its site + ground pad: `world.cpp` (search `[church]`) |
| Stone relief on straight walls (depth, corners, edges) | `shaders/cube_wall_relief.glsl` (helpers), `shaders/cube_main_surfaces.glsl` (the march); which faces count as open: `compute_brush_face_masks` in `world_render.cpp` |
| Round towers' stonework | `shaders/cube_main_surfaces.glsl` (static-mesh branch) |
| Tree look (crown shading, leaf cards) | `shaders/cube.frag` (foliage block after the includes); tree shapes: `world_props_meshes.inl`; where trees stand: `world_props.cpp` |
| A flower / plant / water lily shape | `scatter_plant_meshes.inl`; its colours: `shaders/scatter.frag`; where it grows: `scatter.cpp` |
| Ground colour, pebbles, puddles, shore | `shaders/terrain_rm_shading_helpers.glsl`, `terrain_rm_pebbles.glsl`, tail of `terrain_raymarch.frag` |
| Water surface, foam, lake bed | `shaders/terrain_rm_water.glsl` + `terrain_rm_main_water.glsl` (the water part of main()) |
| Lawn grass | `shaders/grass_raymarch.frag` |
| Fog / mist | `shaders/froxel_fog.comp`, `atmosphere.cpp` |
| Rain on the lens, heat shimmer, the moon's disc | `shaders/lens_fx.comp`, `lens_fx.cpp`, `compose.frag` (`atmo_moon`) |
| Weather, rain, thunder timing | `environment.cpp`; lightning bolt drawing: `ui_hud.cpp` |
| A new setting | `settings_types.h` (field + default), `settings.cpp` (`f("name", ...)` line + clamp), `ui_menu.cpp` (slider) |
| A HUD element | `ui_hud.cpp` |
| Editor items | `veg_zones.cpp` (`kItems`) |
| Gun, bullets, fire rate (laser and rifle) | `combat.cpp`; bolt glow: `fx.cpp`; gun models and where they sit: `props.cpp` `init_viewmodel` |
| Weapon spread: how accurate a shot is from the hip / aimed / in a held burst / moving, and how far the crosshair opens | Model and one table of numbers per weapon: `src/game/aim_spread.h` (`kRifleSpread`, `kLaserSpread`). `combat.cpp` `spread_tick` (each frame: the cone's angle, the cooling) and `spread_shot_dir` (the local player's shot, anywhere in the cone's disc; the shooter decides the direction and sends it as before, so nothing changed on the network). The crosshair's arms stand apart by that angle: `ui_hud.cpp`. `VIBE_LOG_SPREAD=1` logs the angle at every shot and, at the start, the model's figures for a few set bursts; `VIBE_TEST_AIM=1` holds the aim |
| The soldier's meshes: body without head, head with helmet, three levels of detail each | built by `tools/character/make_soldier.py`, exported by `tools/character/export_fp.py` into `assets/character/soldier_fp*.qchar`, `soldier_head*.qchar`, `soldier_atlas.jpg`; loaded and chosen in `player_body.cpp` (`load_skin_mesh`, `body_mesh_lod`) |
| The soldiers' shadows | Ray traced for the nearest six: `vk_engine/body_rt.cpp` (`record_body_rt`, called from `rebuild_tlas`), `shaders/skin_rt.comp`. On the ground for all: `clutter_shadow.cpp` (`draw_skinned_shadows`), `shaders/skin_shadow.vert` |
| Order of a frame's first steps (camera, poses, TLAS) | `frame.cpp`: `pose_characters_for_frame` runs before `submit_tlas_build` because the TLAS build skins the soldiers' ray-tracing copies |
| What the animator is told about the local player; the bone palette; the animation debug overlay | `player_body.cpp`; the skinning itself: `shaders/cube_skin.vert` (pipelines in `pipelines_setup.cpp`, palette buffer = scene set binding 33 in `descriptors.cpp`) |
| How a pose is made (order of layers, warps, feet, arms, recoil, reload) | `src/anim/character_animator.cpp`; tuning: `AnimTuning` in its header |
| Which captured frame is played next | `src/anim/motion_matcher.cpp` (ground), `src/anim/airborne.cpp` (jumps, falls, landings) |
| Where the hands hold the weapon, recoil and reload timing | `WeaponDef` in `src/anim/character_animator.h` |
| What is replicated about a player | `src/engine/net/net_state.h` (bump `kProtocolVersion`) |
| What is replicated about a car, who simulates it | `net_state.h` "Cars", `jeep.cpp` `jeep_net_send` / `jeep_net_receive` |
| Cars striking cars; the NPC traffic's following and overtaking; the police cars and their siren | `jeep.cpp` `cars_collide`, `police_place`, `siren_audio_tick`; `npc/npc.cpp` "THE TRAFFIC"; `engine/siren_synth.h`; state `vk_engine/state/car_traffic.h` |
| The colour far things fade into | `shaders/ground_haze.glsl` |
| Stored ground anywhere on the planet (height tiles), the address of a piece of the planet | `engine/sector.h`, `engine/region_tile.h`, `vk_engine/regions.cpp`, `shaders/region_tiles.glsl` - section "Regions" |
| Where the ground is shaded coarsely | `shaders/terrain_vrs.frag`, `world_render.cpp` `tr_vrs_near_m` |
| Player speed, jump, crouch | `src/game/player.cpp` |
| Anti-aircraft vehicles | `aa.cpp` |
| Loading screen | `loader_screen.cpp`, art in `assets/loader/loading_bg.jpg` |
| Key bindings and the controller | `src/engine/window.cpp` (`Action`, `InputBindings`: reads keys, mouse and gamepad into one `InputFrame`), `vk_engine.cpp` `run()` (acts on it), `ui_menu.cpp` Controls tab (rebinding; saved as `bind_key_*` / `bind_pad_*`) |

## Working on it

- Build: `./build.ps1 -Config Release`. Run from the project root: `build/vibe.exe`.
- Capture one frame: `build/vibe.exe --frames 260 --screenshot out.png --screenshot-after 230`
  (PNG if the path ends in `.png`). The pose comes from `vibe_settings.cfg`
  (`player_pos_*`, `player_yaw` and `player_pitch` in radians, `time_of_day`).
- Test hooks (environment variables): `VIBE_TEST_HOLD=1` pin the camera,
  `VIBE_TEST_CRAWL=1` crouch, `VIBE_TEST_SIZE=3440x1400` window size,
  `VIBE_TEST_EDIT=1` open the editor, `VIBE_TEST_ZONE=<preset>` paint a test
  patch, `VIBE_TEST_LIGHTNING=1`, `VIBE_TEST_FIRE=1`, `VIBE_TEST_MENU=1`.
- GPU cost per pass: the `gpu-passes ...` line in `vibe.log` (once a second).
- Jitter rule: a pass BEFORE the temporal resolve / upscaler (the sky fill, `SKY_FILL` in
  `compose.frag`) builds its view rays from the jittered projection and writes motion; a pass
  AFTER it (compose: the stars `star_field`, haze, far field) uses the projection WITHOUT the
  jitter (`frame.cpp`, where `pc_data.inv_view_proj` is set) - the resolved picture stands
  still, and stars placed with the jittered matrix hopped a render pixel from frame to frame.
  `VIBE_TEST_SKY_JITTER=1` puts the jitter back to compare (two screenshots a frame apart).
  Measure at the played resolution (`VIBE_TEST_SIZE`), not the 1280x720 default.
- Anything added to the full terrain height function (`rm_ground_detail`) is
  paid on every terrain shadow / reflection step: add detail in the normal or
  the shading instead.
- Splitting a file that has grown too big: `tools/split_shader.pl` (GLSL line
  ranges -> includes) and `tools/split_cpp.pl` (a .cpp -> several).

## Conventions

- One topic per file; a file over ~1200 lines is a candidate for a split.
- New `VulkanEngine` methods go in the topic file under `vk_engine/`; declare
  them in `vk_engine.h` next to the related members.
- Settings names are `snake_case` and identical in the cfg file, the struct
  field and the `f("...")` line.
- Shader includes are named `<owner>_<topic>.glsl`.
- Internal names keep the old `vibe` / `VIBE_` prefix (namespace, test
  hooks, the `vibe_engine` library); the product name is Vibe.

## Multiplayer (LAN)

Model and rules are described at the top of `src/engine/net/net.h`. In short: every instance
broadcasts a beacon (UDP 47777); the lowest id hosts (ENet, UDP 47778+), the rest join; when the
host leaves, the others elect again. Each client simulates its own player and sends its state 30x
a second; the host relays snapshots at 60 Hz; other players are drawn 100 ms in the past,
interpolated. Shots / hits / deaths are reliable events; a hit is decided on the shooter's machine
against the avatar as drawn there (`mp_bolt_hits`).

Other players each have an animator of their own (`MpAvatar::animator`): `mp_pose_avatars` turns
their interpolated state into its input (`net::remote_animation_input`), picks a level of detail
by their size on screen, and writes their bone palette (the GPU skins). No pose data is sent: position,
velocity, view, movement keys, stance, weapon and a shot counter are (35 bytes, 30 times a second).
Shots are stamped events, de-duplicated and played when the delayed picture reaches their moment.
`draw_remote_players` draws body + what they carry through `draw_viewmodel` with their camera
matrix (`mp_draw_`). Whether a player can be hit never depends on whether or how they are drawn.

Off for automated runs (`--frames`, `--screenshot`) unless `VIBE_NET=1`; `VIBE_NO_NET=1` turns it
off entirely. Two instances on one PC work (the second takes the next game port).

Cars are replicated too: see "Cars: drawn between physics steps, and on the network" below.

## Levels of detail and shader builds

- **Trees / bushes / rocks** (`vegetation.cpp`, `veg_cull.comp`): GPU-driven - every instance is frustum- and Hi-Z-culled and given one of 4 mesh levels (or an impostor) on the GPU; drawn with indirect draws, near levels first. The colour pass tests depth EQUAL against the pre-pass.
- **Trees beyond the home tile** (`vk_engine/veg_stream.cpp`): the world is cut into 128 m cells; a cell's trees and bushes are a pure function of its coordinates and the world's forest rule (`wm_forest` in `shaders/world_map.glsl` / `world_forest` in `terrain.cpp` - also what colours the far landscape as woods). Cells are generated on background threads, nearest first, and their instances are written into a pool of `kWvPool` slots inside the vegetation instance buffer (between the home tile's instances and the clustered rocks), so the same GPU culling / LOD / impostor / shadow / ray-tracing path draws them. With distance only a leading share of a cell's rank-ordered trees is live (all, half, a quarter, then only the trees under a rank limit - "Far trees: to 3.8 km on the ground" below; bushes within 260 m). Trunk colliders for cells within 200 m (`wv_cols_`). `VIBE_NO_WORLD_VEG=1` turns it off. When the camera is too high to see a tree, or moving faster than 900 m/s, everything is given back and nothing is queued (space flight). **Grass beyond home**: `wv_nf_poll` bakes the ground around the camera (512 m at 1 m, the world formula, packed into an RGBA8 texture in normal-texture slot 22, re-baked every 72 m of travel) and `grass_raymarch.frag` (`nf_*`) stands its blades on it, where a rule of height and slope allows. Not yet: bullets / physics bodies against these trunks, blast damage that survives a cell reload, manual add / remove beyond home.
- **Screen-space displacement mapping (SSDM)** (`vk_engine/ssdm.cpp`, `state/ssdm.h`, `shaders/ssdm_*`; rewritten 2026-11-26 as a 1:1 port of the reference game's "rasterizer / reconstruct" route, read from all 315 entries of its compiled screen-space tessellation shaders - the reading notes are outside the repo). The stonework and the ground are drawn flat; then, in the middle of the main pass (`render_ssdm`, called by `render_world` right after the castle meshes, before lighting, trees, effects, grass and the weapon): (0) colour, motion, both G-buffer layers and depth are copied; (1) *height feed* (`ssdm_height.*` for mesh parts and brushes, `ssdm_ground.frag` for the raymarched ground) - per pixel the vertex normal and a height byte 0..1, as the game keeps them in its G-buffer (height texture x the material's factor `amplitude / (0.2 x scale)`, no fade, the sampler's own mip); (2) `ssdm_raster.comp` - 16 x 16 tiles of 17 x 17 threads, one vertex per pixel corner displaced by `ssdm_displace.glsl` (the game's TileVS body: reach T, amplitude `0.2 x scale x fade x (1 + 4 x saturate(d/100))` with the fade over the last quarter of T, a 4 x 4 agreement-weighted Gaussian blur of the height - a 5-tap cross for the terrain class - with a 2 px step close by and 1 px from 16 m, vertices cut at depth jumps, the normal smoothed over 2 x 2 by depth, the screen-edge fade), each cell of four displaced corners rasterized in software into a 64-bit word per pixel by atomic max (depth, offset to the source pixel, octahedral normal); cells with a triangle over 16 px^2 go to a list; (3) `ssdm_hwargs.comp` + `ssdm_hwraster.*` draw those cells with the hardware (the game uses a mesh shader; same vertex code, same word); (4) `ssdm_resolve.frag`, full screen: a covered pixel takes the G-buffer of its source pixel, the displaced depth and within 32 m the shading normal turned from the vertex normal onto the displaced one (stone sides: a 4-tap blur of normal and albedo); an uncovered pixel keeps its G-buffer but its depth is pulled toward the camera by its own height (fading over the last 8 m of T) and close by marks the half-resolution behind-material mask; (5) `ssdm_dilate.comp` spreads that mask, `ssdm_behind.comp` blends the displaced copy back toward the original by it; (6) the displaced copy is copied into the live attachments (raw bits: the albedo copy holds sRGB bytes, the normal copy 10:10:10:2 packed by hand). Class mapping (`ssdm_class` in `ssdm_common.glsl`): sky 0, raymarched / mesh terrain 3, foliage 12 (never displaced), everything else 1. Our constants (not in the game's shaders): reach `kSsdmReach` 48 m, amplitude scale `kSsdmAmpScale` 1 (height 1 = 0.2 m close by). Not ported: the game's puddle map (ours is always dry), its copy of the undisplaced G-buffer in the rasterizer (the resolve overwrites it in this route), the temporal and height-scale features of its other route. Deviations: the material id is not blended in the behind pass (the nearer one is kept); a pixel whose displaced normal is opposite its vertex normal keeps its normal (the game divides by zero). Rule kept: the stones only rise from the drawn surface (ray-traced shadows and collision are those of the drawn surface). Needs `shaderInt64` + `shaderBufferInt64Atomics` (setup.cpp). Displaced: mesh parts with `CastleMeshGpu::ssdm > 0`, home's static box brushes with albedo 1 / 4 (walls) or 5 / 6 (floor tiles, top face; `brush_ssdm_amplitude`), the raymarched ground; while the pass is on they are drawn flat (`ssdm_active()`). `VIBE_NO_SSDM=1` off (parallax / box relief back), `VIBE_SSDM_NO_BRUSHES=1` mesh parts only, `VIBE_SSDM_DEBUG=1` paints covered pixels red. **Producers, as the reference game feeds it** (2026-11-26, second round): the height maps are stretched so joints are 0 and tops 1 (the walls' and floors' photo scans in `ssdm_height.frag` from measured 5th / 98th percentiles - their joints sat at mid grey and wasted a third of the range; the service station's brick, asphalt, cladding, pavers and wall tile at load in `world_textures.cpp`, whose height is in the normal map's alpha and is now displaced too: `kStationRelief` in `castle_mesh.cpp`, 0.4 - 2 cm). The ground writes its material height out to the reach (`kSsdmReach` 48 m in `terrain_rm_heightfield.glsl`) with no fade or cap of its own, and its cobble and soil parallax is off while the displacement is on (the game's terrain shader marches only when its displacement is off). **Height maps made for it** (`tools/textures/make_ssdm_heights.py`, Blender's Python): the castle wall's, the courtyard's and the road paving's scans as `<name>_SSDM.png` - the scan's own height measured against its surroundings (it drifts across the picture), joints 0 and stone tops 1, a power of 0.55 - 0.7 so each stone rises out of its joint as a rounded body; the photographed colour and normal maps unchanged. `kWorldSpomPaths` and the terrain loader (`<set>_SSDM.png` in place of the scan's height when there is one) read them. Cost: not measured yet.
- **The ground displaced in screen space** (2026-11-25; `shaders/ssdm_ground.frag`, `terrain_rm_main_ground.glsl` "How far this pixel's material stands out", `ssdm.cpp ground_ssdm_active`). How the reference game gives its near ground relief, read from its compiled shaders: its terrain pixel shader writes the material's height as a byte into the G-buffer and its screen-space tessellation pass moves the pixels by it (the same pass as for its walls; no dense near mesh for ordinary ground, hardware tessellation only for its snow sheet). Here: while `GND_SSDM` is on (scene constant `rm_perf.x` bit 64), `terrain_raymarch.frag` works out how far the material at the pixel stands out of the marched surface - cobbles 4 cm from joint to top, the soil / rock scan's upper part (what lies above 0.4 of its height: stones, clods, ribs; 7 - 9 cm), roadside gravel 3 cm, asphalt grain; nothing under a lawn, sand, snow or water; out to the displacement's reach (48 m), with no fade of its own - and writes it into the blue channel of the second G-buffer layer (`0.7 + 0.3 * h / kGndSsdmMax`, 0.7 = out of reach; `rr_guides.comp` only tells 0.6 from the rest). `ssdm_ground.frag` (full-screen, into the height target after the meshes) turns that into normal + height for material-8 pixels: the normal from the depth buffer, leaned toward straight up on ground that is not steep. The same rasterizer and resolve passes as for the walls, over the whole screen (the terrain is class 3: a 5-tap height cross; the resolve copies the lit colour of the source pixel too). While it is on, the cobbles' parallax march and the soil's parallax shift are left out. **Height blends** (`rm_height_blend`, the game's formula: weight x height, the higher one wins over a band of 0.15) with the same switch: soil against rock in the scan's normal and tint, the cobbles' edge against the earth, the roadside gravel against the land - close by only. **Rule kept**: the ground only rises from the marched surface, so shadows, collision, and the grass's roots (`ground_relief` in `tuft_place.comp` and its twins) are unchanged; the height is 0 under a lawn, elsewhere a root can be hidden by up to the height of the stone beside it. Things drawn after the pass (plants, props, people, cars, marks on the ground) are depth-tested against the raised ground. Only with the terrain at full resolution (`terrain_raymarch_scale` 1): the low-resolution terrain's compose pass carries no height, so the quality levels with a smaller terrain do not have it. `VIBE_NO_GROUND_DETAIL=1` off (parallax and plain blends back); `VIBE_SSDM_DEBUG=1` shows what moved. Cost: `w_ssdm` 0.5 - 0.8 ms at 3440 x 1400 with render scale 50 % and 0.64 ms at 1280 x 720 internal, both read while another program was using the GPU (the terrain pass read 34 and 16 ms in the same runs); not measured on an idle GPU. It runs whenever the camera is within 350 m of the water level, also when no displaced pixel is in view (no test for that yet). Seen in screenshots only: cobbles (stones stand up and hide each other's far sides, softer than the parallax version at stone edges), soil under trees (little moves there). Not done: heights through the low-resolution terrain path; a real normal for the lit ground's displaced sides (the colour is moved, not re-lit); a tile list instead of the full-screen grid; unit tests.
- **Small plants** (`scatter.cpp`): 32 m cells, culled on the CPU (frustum, distance, walls, hills). Each species has three meshes: full, ~32 % (beyond 22 m) and ~11 % (beyond 48 m), made at load time with meshoptimizer (`simplified` in `build_scatter`). `VIBE_NO_PLANT_LOD=1` turns them off.
- **Trees, the reference game's way** (2026-11-25). Written after reading the game's compiled tree shaders (foliage shader, impostor shader, tree shadow shader, its diffuse-GI pass; notes outside the repo: `dev/cd-shaders/trees/REPORT.md`). The game's trees are authored meshes; ours stay the engine's own (`eztree.cpp`) - what was taken over is how they are drawn.
  - *Far trees: octahedral impostors with depth* (`vk_engine/veg_impostor.cpp` bake, `shaders/veg_leaf.glsl` `veg_imp_sample`, `veg.vert` the card, `cube.frag` / `veg_imp_depth.frag` the pixel). Every tree type is baked from 8 x 8 directions on a hemi-octahedron (96 px tiles, orthographic, framed on the sphere round the tree's middle) into two atlases: colour + coverage, and normal (octahedral, rg) + depth (b) + leaf mark (a). 24 types: 4608 x 3072, baked in about 1.3 s at start-up. The card is a square facing the camera on the NEAR side of that sphere; the pixel cuts its view ray with the plane through the tree's middle, projects that point into the three baked views round the direction to the camera and blends them - only in the middle fifth between two views, so mostly one view is shown sharp - cuts at alpha 0.5, and moves the point by the blended depth: that is the depth written (never nearer than the card, so `depth_greater` holds), in the pre-pass and in the colour pass alike (the colour pass tests EQUAL: both compute it with the same `precise` lines). So a card looks right from above, cuts into the ground and other trees like the tree, and can take over early (`tree_impostor_px`). The normal: the game takes the slope of the depth, turns it to the eye where edge-on and leans it 10 - 20 % up; ours is that, half way to the baked crown normal (so card and mesh are lit alike). Roughness 0.9. Numbers shared between bake and shaders: `kImpN`, `kImpTile`, `kImpCols`. Measured in one view of a tree line at 64 px hand-over: tree passes 2.8 -> 0.45 ms against meshes.
  - *Wind* (`shaders/veg_wind.glsl`, called by `veg.vert` for this and last frame's pose): the game's one body for branch, frond and leaf - the wind here (global, turned and strengthened by the wind field within 64 m), its fade with distance, a few millimetres for the whole plant, the branch swing (the visible motion), a ripple and a tumble for each leaf card about the point it grows from, and low plants leaning away from who walks through them (the trail picture; divided by (height - 1.5)^1.8, so trees do not). What its modelling tool bakes into every vertex of the game's meshes (branch weight, phase, direction, leaf pivot) is worked out from the vertex's place in the tree. The ray-traced shadows are cast by the still tree.
  - *Leaves in the lighting pass*: leaf pixels of trees and bushes are material id 2 like the grass: the game's foliage back-light, roughness 0.45 + 0.005 a metre, and occlusion only coming in with distance.
  - *Gusts* (`shaders/wind_gust.glsl`, ours - not in the game's shaders): the wind's strength is a pattern of fronts travelling down the wind at 6 m/s (about 30 m apart, in groups, with lulls), read at each plant's place by the grass (`tuft_cd.glsl`) and the trees; under a front blades and branches also lean down the wind. This is what makes a field move in waves instead of all at once.
  - *Not taken over*: the game's shadow-map passes for trees (alpha 0.45 on sharper mips, dithered fade, leaf-coloured static shadows, impostor shadows) - shadows here are ray-traced; its compute / mesh-shader paths that draw far impostors in bulk; its coverage trick for leaf edges under 2 x 2 shading; the terrain-colour tint of impostors.
- **Far trees: to 3.8 km on the ground** (2026-11-26; `world_render.cpp` far plane, `veg_stream.cpp` far levels, `veg_cull.comp`, `veg.vert`, `veg_leaf.glsl` `veg_imp_sample`, `veg_impostor.cpp`). What stopped them before, and what was done:
  - *The far plane.* It was 1500 m up to 1 km altitude. With the near plane at 0.05 the depth 0.99999 - which compose, TAA, the upscalers, the clouds and the fill passes all read as "sky" - then lay 1154 m out: nothing could be drawn further on the ground (trees between there and the old 1450 m limit showed as pale blue patches). The far plane is now 30 km at every height (what it already was above 2 km): "sky" lies 4.3 km out, the terrain's own depth clamp (0.999989) 3.9 km. Depth resolution is set by the near plane and is unchanged (about 1.5 m at 1 km, 11 m at 3 km). The terrain shaders that turn its depth into a distance by hand carry `1.0000016667` = far / (far - near) (`terrain_rm_upscale.glsl`, `terrain_rm_resolve.frag`, `terrain_raymarch_compose.frag`): change them together with `view_far_`. `object_far_` (trees, rock clusters, the rocket) is 3800 m at every height; the tree setting `tree_draw_distance` (200 - 3800) now means what it says on the ground too.
  - *Far cells are "lite".* Streamed cells have 7 levels: all + bushes to 260 m, all to 650, half to 1100, a quarter to 1600 (these need the cell's whole list), then the trees whose rank is under 0.14 to 2400 m, under 0.085 to 3200 m, under 0.05 beyond (`kFarRank`; rank = random / size, so the big trees stay longest). A cell that is only wanted at a far level is generated lite: candidates are dropped by rank before the forest rule is asked, no bushes, no river scrub, no colliders. Its trees are the head of the whole cell's rank-ordered list, so when the camera comes within 1600 m the whole cell is made and takes over the live slots as they are (nothing moves or pops); a whole cell further than 2700 m is cut back to its lite head. Measured at the home valley, 3896 m reach: 2714 cells, 2422 of them lite, 23 610 plants live of the pool's 180 000, 7.1 MB of cell data, all made within 3 s of start-up on 4 threads; a scan of the cells 0.3 - 0.5 ms (at most ten a second, only while moving or loading; it was 0.04 ms for 262 cells at 1546 m). `VIBE_LOG_WVEG=1` logs these numbers every two seconds.
  - *Thinned, so drawn larger.* A tree's card grows with distance, x1 at 1500 m to x1.35 from 3200 m on (`veg.vert`, the same line in `veg_cull.comp` for the bounds): each far tree stands for the ones left out round it.
  - *No line at the far end.* In `veg_cull.comp` every plant has a number of its own: it is dropped when its size falls under 0.9 - 1.6 px (by that number - small plants go first, not all at one size) and over the last fifth of the draw distance (more and more of them, the same ones every frame). Beyond that the ground has the woods' colour (`terrain_rm_main_light.glsl`, from 1200 - 1700 m on, by the same forest rule).
  - *The card's pixels far away* (`veg_imp_sample`). The mip went no further than 2 (24 texels a tile): a tree a few pixels tall was read at scattered points of it. Now up to mip 4 (a tile is 96 = 3 x 32 texels, so mips up to 5 never mix two tiles; the margin past mip 2 is half a texel of the mip in use). The alpha cut follows the mip as in the reference game: 0.5 up to mip 2, down to 0.3 at mip 4 - a small mip's coverage is an average over leaves and gaps, and cut at 0.5 a far crown shrinks. The bake fills what stays empty in a tile with the tree's mean colour (coverage 0), or those mips darken toward the rim.
  - *A far card keeps its own depth.* Beyond 300 m the card stands in the plane through the tree's middle and its pixels do not write the baked depth (`veg.vert` hands the pixel shaders a negative radius). The colour pass tests depth EQUAL; out there its own sum for the baked depth came out a step away from the pre-pass's on many pixels, and the tree was drawn as a hole in the cleared picture's colour (with the far plane moved out, every far tree was a pale blue shape; `VIBE_VEG_NO_EQ=1` showed them right). Within 300 m the baked depth is written as before, and the two shaders still have to agree there.
  - Far cells cast no ray-traced shadows (the traced set is the nearest trees within 650 m, as before), have no colliders (200 m) and no wind (cards have none). All far cards of a type are one indirect draw (24 types, one multi-draw call for the card level).
  - Not done: a compute / mesh-shader path for far cards; the terrain-colour tint of far cards; far cards that stand for a clump are single trees drawn larger, not baked clumps; unit tests for the lite-head rule (checked by one run and the log line).
- **Roads in the ground's displacement** (2026-11-25; `terrain_rm_main_ground.glsl` "The paved road, worn", `rm_hw_asphalt`'s `lvl` in `terrain_rm_noise_road.glsl`, applied in `terrain_rm_main_light.glsl`). The reference game's roads are decals along a spline that MULTIPLY the height the ground wrote for the displacement pass (a road only presses the ground down) and darken it. Here the roads are part of the raymarched ground, so the same is done where the height is written: cobbles 6 cm from joint to top, pressed down along two wheel lines 1.5 m apart (they come and go) and in settled patches; the trodden earth beside the paving at under half its relief; the asphalt a sheet 1.6 cm thick with its grain, times `lvl` - 0 in cracks and tarred joints, 0.7 in the wheel ruts, 1.35 on a patch. Generated wear with no texture behind it (`rm_hw_asphalt`, within 40 m; colour and level together): stretches where the sheet is broken into plates in the wheel tracks (`rm_hw_plates`, irregular cells 18 cm across, each settled its own way), and a pothole in about a quarter of the 53 m stretches of a lane (down to the grit of the base course). The cobbles' wheel lines also show in colour (dirt in the joints, tops rubbed pale). Same limits as the ground's displacement (full-resolution terrain, 16 - 24 m reach, the ground only rises). Seen in two screenshots (cobbles, highway); not in motion. Not done: the mesh terrain (`cube.frag` writes no height for the pass), several texture sets along a road.
- **Patch terrain: the visible ground from a mesh, not a march** (2026-11-25; `vk_engine/terrain_patches.cpp`, state `vk_engine/state/terrain_patches.h`, `shaders/terrain_patch.vert`, `rm_patch_height` / `rm_patch_hit` in `terrain_rm_heightfield.glsl`, `patch_mode` in `terrain_raymarch.frag`). The way the reference game finds its ground, read from its compiled shaders: no ray march.
  - *What it is.* ONE grid patch of (N + 3)^2 vertices (N = 64 quads a side; the outer ring repeats the edge and hangs down from it as a skirt of `30 * saturate(distance / 2048)` m) drawn once per instance. An instance is the patch's corner and its vertex spacing; the spacing is the level of detail (0.25 m, doubling up to 32 m: patches of 16 m .. 2048 m). A quadtree on the CPU picks the instances every frame (`update_terrain_patches`): a node nearer than twice its side is split into four, a node behind a side plane of the view is dropped - tested with the node's box, whose lowest and highest ground come from the CPU's ground function (5 x 5 samples, a margin that grows with the node) and are kept in a map until the chart changes. Seams between levels are hidden by the skirts alone: no morphing, no stitching. The vertex shader puts each vertex at the height formula's value - it includes the formula's own files (`terrain_rm_noise_road.glsl`, `terrain_rm_pebbles.glsl`, `terrain_rm_heightfield.glsl`), so there is no second copy of the world; the close relief goes in as far as a 0.25 m grid can carry it (the main rills, the sand drifts and the snow; not side cracks, soil grain or stones). On the castle road the surface is the baked one, as for the march.
  - *How the mesh is used (this engine's own part).* The patches are rasterised depth-only, in a pass of their own before the world pass, into the depth target of the low-resolution terrain (scene binding 11; that target is at full size while the setting is on, and the low-resolution terrain does not run). Their projection is the world's with the depth replaced by `near / distance`: reversed, exact at any range, no far plane. The terrain's full-screen pass then reads the pixel's distance there in place of calling `raymarch()` (`rm_patch_hit`; `tex_params.x` negative says so), and puts the point straight up or down onto the formula's surface - one evaluation of the formula a pixel instead of dozens, and the sun-shadow march never starts under the ground. Everything after that is the code it was: materials, roads, the height for the screen-space displacement, shadows, what stands in front (the depth pre-pass copy), the water plane (same pass, same test against the ground's distance), the far march beyond 1500 m, the depth it writes. Why not draw the patches into the world pass with the full shader: level ground at a distance is slivers thinner than a pixel, and every sliver shades whole 2 x 2 blocks - the heavy shading would run several times a pixel; and the world's depth is too coarse beyond a kilometre to sort far hills.
  - *Where it does not run.* From the air (eye above 260 m: the far march finds the whole landscape, as before), in the mesh-terrain mode, and on a frame whose depth target is not yet at full size. The march pre-pass (`terrain_march_lo`) is skipped with it unless the raymarched lawn or 'sun shadows at low resolution' need it.
  - *Setting and hooks.* `terrain_patches` (1 = on, the default; Terrain tab, under the raymarch switch; `VIBE_TERRAIN_PATCHES=0|1` overrides the file). `VIBE_LOG_TERRAIN_PATCHES=1`: a line every 120 frames - patches, per level, vertices, triangles. `VIBE_TEST_PATCH_SPLIT=<0.75..6>`: the split distance in node sides (2). GPU passes: `terrain_patches` (the depth picture) + `w_terrain_raymarch` (the full-screen pass).
  - *Measured* (RTX 4080, the test window: 1280 x 720 at render scale 0.5 = 640 x 360 rendered, camera held - a small picture, so the full-screen pass weighs less here than in play and the patches, whose cost is per vertex, more): castle cobbles `terrain_march_lo` 0.47 + `w_terrain_raymarch` 4.79 ms -> `terrain_patches` 0.23 + 1.52 ms; highway 0.82 + 1.93 -> 0.13 + 0.82 ms. 146 patches, 1.27 M triangles at the castle view. The pictures matched the march's.
  - *Limits / not done.* The vertices evaluate the formula directly (0.2 ms: no height cache was needed - a compute pass writing height tiles per patch is the next step if the vertex cost ever shows). No occlusion culling of patches (the reference tests them against last frame's depth pyramid). The ground material is still worked out per pixel, not baked into a virtual texture. The loose stones of the march's close relief are not in the mesh; the displacement pass raises them (next bullet). Only the upper side of the mesh is drawn, so an eye that is under the surface for a moment looks through it at the ground beyond. Node bounds are not refreshed when the ground is sculpted in the editor. Other charts, winter, the view from under water and the hand-over at 260 m were not looked at.
- **Every ground material has a height** (2026-11-25; `terrain_rm_main_ground.glsl` "Old, hand-laid paving", "Beaches", "How far this pixel's material stands out ..." and the snow block; the colour's side in `terrain_rm_main_light.glsl` "Road" / "Roots"). The reference game's rule for its ground: every layer has a height, layers meet by height (the higher one shows, over a narrow band: `rm_height_blend`), snow fills the low places first, a road presses the height down by a factor. The height block is now in two parts - all that lies under the snow (`gnd_h`), then the snow on it - and is the one place that says how far a ground pixel stands out for the screen-space displacement (metres, only upward, at most `kGndSsdmMax` = 12 cm, faded out by 24 m as before).
  - *The old cobble road*: the scan is one even sheet of stones; what makes it old is generated from noise at the stones' scale laid on the scan's height, colour and height together. Stones settled in groups (`rm_cob_sink`: lower and dirtier); a stone missing here and there with earth in the hole; earth standing in the joints, higher toward the edges and where the bed has sunk, moss on it (`rm_cob_fill`, `rm_cob_moss`; the surface is `rm_cob_lvl` = the higher of stone and earth); a ragged edge - the paving's share is pushed about stone by stone before earth and stones are laid over each other by height, so single stones stand in the earth; the wheel lines as before. Earth, not the land's palette, is what lies under the paving. A per-stone id does not exist (the scan has none): a sunk group's border can cross a stone.
  - *Sand* (the drifts on the road; the palette's beaches below 2.5 m): a sheet about 3 cm thick with the fine wind ripples the normal is made from (kept gentle: 6 mm in the height, a rounder profile and little contrast in the colour - stronger, they read as hard stripes), its lumps, and the prints pressed in (45 % of their depth, as in the marched sand). Beaches get those ripples in the normal too (they had none). Stones that stand higher show through.
  - *Snow*: close by its cover is no longer a wash over everything - its surface is a level that rises with the cover (11 cm at full), so it lies in the paving's joints and between stones and clods first and takes the tops last; a full sheet is written as its lumps (the third term of `snow_layer_h`, the one the snow's normal has) on a 5 cm floor the prints are pressed into.
  - *Bare earth*: on top of the scan, generated clods and crumbs (most where the scan is flat) and, under trees (the baked tree cover, home tile only), roots across the forest floor - with a bark colour. Under a lawn the height stays 0, except a rim of 3 cm where turf meets bare ground (the sod's edge). The loose stones of `terrain_rm_pebbles.glsl` are raised by their own height (with the patch terrain they are no longer in the surface itself: `rm_patch_height` leaves them out).
  - *Not done*: the palette's own bands (grass / dry earth / rock by slope, red sand) still cross-fade plainly - they are colours of one soil scan, with no second height to blend against; the highway's asphalt edge likewise. The beach ripples and the roots were not looked at in a picture. The grass's blades are drawn after the displacement and depth-tested against it: the sod rim hides their lowest 3 cm there, nothing floats.
  - `VIBE_RM_SKIP` bit 4194304 (with 1 + 2 + 512): the ground without its material - one colour, no road or highway surface. What is left of `w_terrain_raymarch` is the light.
- **The baked ground texture** (2026-11-25; `vk_engine/terrain_bake.cpp`, state `vk_engine/state/terrain_bake.h`, `shaders/terrain_rm_bake.glsl`, the `VIBE_BAKE` build of `terrain_raymarch.frag` = `terrain_bake.frag.spv`). The reference game bakes its ground layers into a virtual texture and lights that; here the ground's material was worked out for every pixel of every frame, and measured it was most of the terrain pass (played size, `VIBE_RM_SKIP=4194819`: castle 6.05 ms of which 3.7 material, highway 4.08 of which 2.2).
  - *The pictures*: 7 levels of 2048 x 2048 texels, each level's texel twice the last (7.5 mm .. 48 cm; windows of 15 m .. 983 m), all centred on the camera, three pictures a level - sqrt(albedo) + the displacement height; the normal's x and z (16 bits each); asphalt share, sand cover, gloss and a "may be used" flag. 336 MB. A texel's place in its picture is its world index modulo 2048 and the sampler repeats, so nothing is ever copied: when the camera has moved by 16 texels of a level, only the strips that came into that level's window are drawn.
  - *The same material code, not a copy*: the bake build's `main()` stands over each texel (straight above it, at the level's own distance), runs the two material parts and writes what the lighting needs. To make that possible the material and the lighting are now included as four parts (`RM_PART` in `terrain_raymarch.frag`): ground 1 (normal, heights) and light 2 (colour) are the material - inside `if (!rm_baked) { ... }` -, ground 3 (the sun's term) and light 4 are the lighting. What crosses from one to the other is declared at the top of part 1: `nor`, `mate`, `sunDir`, `rm_road_vis`, `sand_cov`, `hw_pave`, `hw_gloss`, `peb0_h`, `lo_ok` / `lo_sh` and the global `g_gnd_ssdm_h`.
  - *Reading it* (`rm_bake_fetch`, patch terrain only): the level goes by the DISTANCE - level 0 up to `t0` (the distance at which its texel is a pixel: 3.2 m at 720 lines), each level twice as far, the two nearest blended - so everything in the material that fades with distance is read at very nearly the distance it was baked for. Not read, and worked out per pixel as before: ground beyond 64 x t0 (205 m at 720 lines), steep faces (normal.y < 0.6: a picture laid out from above has too few texels down a wall), ground at or under the water, the castle's floor, and a circle round the footprints (they change from step to step and are not baked).
  - *Dropped for baked pixels*: what depends on the view or the sun inside the material - the cobbles' and the soil's parallax (they were already off within the displacement's reach), the cobbles' self-shadow term beyond it, the shadow a loose stone throws, the slide of the hit along the ray onto the road, 'sun shadows at low resolution'. Very close (under about 2 m at 720 lines) a texel is larger than a pixel: the ground is a little softer there than worked out per pixel.
  - *Made again* when what the material is worked out from has changed (`terrain_bake_signature`, a hash of the scene constants: snow cover, season, the palette and grass colours, road, site, chart, water level, the formula's octaves and distances, render height, the displacement switch; `terrain_bake_invalidate` when the ground is sculpted) and has then stood still for 20 frames - a quarter of a level a frame, 28 frames; until the levels are whole the terrain pass works the material out per pixel. While snow is falling or a season is turning that is every frame: the bake waits. The sun and the time of day are not in the hash: lighting stays per pixel.
  - *Craters* (`update_terrain_bake`, `craters_seen`): a crater that is new or changed in the list is drawn again in every level at once, only within its reach (3 x its radius + 1 m), with no wait; the height texture's update that follows it a frame or two later draws the same part once more. A blast whose reach is most of the near levels (over 3 M texels: a crashed aircraft's) makes the levels invalid instead - they are filled again at the usual rate and the per-pixel path draws meanwhile. Sculpting still makes everything again.
  - *At speed*: the finest levels are the ones a moving camera redraws most of. Above 12 m/s reading starts at level 1, above 28 m/s at level 2, and the finer levels are not drawn; slower again, they are filled first and read when whole (the first level in use rides in the eights of `grass_params.y`). After any fill the coarse levels are made first and serve as soon as levels 2..6 are whole. Measured on `VIBE_TEST_HIGHWAY_RIDE=40` (0.67 m a frame; test window): `terrain_bake` about 1.0 ms a frame (0.5 M texels) before this rule, 0.28 ms (0.13 - 0.16 M texels) with it; the picture taken mid-ride had no stale or missing strips. Note: the hash of the material's inputs once held the formula's distances to the last digit, which change with the camera's height over the water - on a drive over a hill the pictures were never made; they are in it in steps now.
  - *Wetness*: rain's sheen, darkening and puddles are worked out in the lighting from the normal and the position, so they are the same for a baked pixel. A puddle now also scales the displacement height down (level water), there too. Not seen in a picture: the test run's weather could not be made to rain.
  - *Bands by height, close by*: the palette's rock / earth, beach and red-sand bands meet by the scan's height (`rm_band`, `g_mat_h`: rock comes up through the earth where the scan stands high, sand lies in its hollows first); the asphalt's edge is the sheet laid over the gravel by height across a hand's width, and its top is 3.4 cm up, a step over the gravel for the displacement.
  - *Setting and hooks*: `terrain_bake` (1 = on, the default; Terrain tab; `VIBE_TERRAIN_BAKE=0|1`), only with `terrain_patches`. `VIBE_LOG_TERRAIN_BAKE=1`. GPU pass `terrain_bake`. Its shader is built on a thread (as large as the terrain's own): on a first start the bake comes in when the driver has compiled it.
  - *Measured* (RTX 4080, camera held, `w_terrain_raymarch`): played size (3440 x 1440 at render scale 0.5) castle 6.38 -> 4.79 ms, highway 4.19 -> 2.10; test window (640 x 360 rendered) castle 1.53 -> 1.26, highway 0.82 -> 0.42. `terrain_bake` itself is below 0.05 ms with the camera still; a quarter level (1 M texels) is one frame's work while filling. The cost while moving was not measured. For the record, the march at the played size: castle 1.24 + 18.05 ms, highway 1.65 + 7.84; the patches 0.26 + 6.05 and 0.17 + 4.08.
  - *Anti-tiling* (`rm_at` / `rm_at_normal` / `rm_at_albedo` in `terrain_rm_shading_helpers.glsl`, used by `rm_det_sample` for the soil and rock scans and by the highway's asphalt grain and verge gravel): the plane is cut into cells of two tiles; each cell lays the scan its own way (turned by a multiple of a quarter turn - the mip stays the same - and shifted, hashed from the cell's number modulo 32), the sampled normal is turned back by the same angle, the cells' borders are bent by noise, and within 9 % of a cell from a border the neighbouring cell's layout is sampled too and blended in (half and half on the border): one sample over most of the ground, two near a border, three near a corner; no blend where a tile is under a few pixels (lod 7 and up). It is in the shared sampling code, so the baked and the per-pixel path are the same. Cost at the played size, castle view: per pixel 6.38 -> 7.13 ms, with the bake 4.79 -> 5.36 ms (one run each). It comes on top of the two tile sizes laid over each other, which stay. Not given it: the fine soil layer under the camera and the verge's soil tint (single direct reads), sand and the grass ground (generated, no scan). The cobbles: no variant per tile along the road - the scan is not symmetric, so a mirrored or shifted tile cuts every stone along the joint, a line across the road each tile, which is worse than the repeat; the variation generated for the old paving (settled groups, missing stones, joints) is what breaks the repeat there. That judgement is from the scan's layout, not from a picture of the attempt.
  - *Not done*: Not looked at: winter and other charts with the bake on, the picture while moving fast (strips), the hand-over at 64 x t0.
- **Tree shapes: grown by habit** (2026-11-25; `eztree.cpp` "Habit growth", `eztree.h` `TreeHabit`, set per tree type in `world_props_meshes.inl` `build_pine` / `build_oak`). The trees' skeletons are no longer the EZ-Tree recursion with other numbers (that stays for bushes and river scrub, `habit.kind` 0) but are laid down the way each kind grows, in metres: **oak** (1) - a short trunk that divides into two to four heavy limbs, lower limbs standing out wide, a broad dome (a young one keeps one stem); **birch** (2) - one slender stem, many thin rising branches whose ends and twigs hang; **spruce** (3) - a straight leader and whorls of boughs that rise at the top, lie level in the middle and droop below with up-turned tips, side shoots to the left and right of each bough (flat sprays); **pine** (4) - a bare lower stem, crooked limbs with up-turned ends, needle tufts at the shoot ends, a head of rising limbs in place of the leader once it is grown, dead stubs on the stem. Common to all (`grow_axis`): a branch starts on its parent's centre line and swells where it leaves the bark (collar: two more rings), sags by its weight near the base and turns up toward the tip (a level branch bends most), wanders, tapers; limbs are cut to the crown's outline (two half ellipsoids, pushed to one side by `asym`); side branches avoid the underside of a limb; twigs leave to both sides in turn; leaf cards hang on the twigs only (crown full outside, limbs and sky inside), each turned about its own line at random so the crown has cover from the side and from below. `age` (from the type's height; the old-growth types are old) sets stem thickness, clear trunk, limb sag and crown width; `lean` tilts the trunk above its first section (the foot stays upright for the root flare) and it grows back up. What `veg.vert` reads is unchanged: tube vertex u = priority + radius (< 1 m), leaf card u = 4 x priority + corner, leaf priorities uniform (the shader's leaf enlargement counts on that), limbs early and thin orders late. Triangles per type (full / the three thinner buckets, summed over the 12 conifer types: 127 / 61 / 20 / 10 thousand before, 123 / 63 / 22 / 12 after; 12 broadleaf types: 195 / 93 / 32 / 18 before, 195 / 90 / 32 / 17 after). Test hooks: `VIBE_LOG_VEG=1` logs `[vegtris]` (triangles of every type per bucket); `VIBE_TEST_TREES=x,z` stands one tree of every type in rows of six from there and logs where (`[vegpos] test tree`). Not done: no unit test (the generator is not in `vibe_core`); the trunk collider is still the fixed box of `world_props.cpp` (0.38 m x scale), not the generated radius.
- **Grass, the reference game's way** (2026-11-25; species 15 = long tufts, 17 = turf). Written after reading the game's compiled shaders (foliage shader, placement shader, interaction shader, its diffuse-GI pass); our own code, the game's formulas and constants. The notes on what was read are outside the repo (`dev/cd-shaders/grass/`: `REPORT.md`, `WIND_FLUID.md`).
  - *One kind of grass.* The game has no lawn of its own kind: its ground cover is the same instanced clumps as its long grass, placed densely. So the short grass is **turf** (species 17, `build_grass_turf`: 16 short blades) and replaces the raymarched lawn (`grass_raymarch.frag` is not drawn while `turf_on()`; `VIBE_LAWN_RM=1` brings it back). The turf grows where the lawn's own mask (`grass_mask_`, scene binding 13) has grass, on the whole home tile.
  - *Placement* (`shaders/tuft_place.comp`, run by `cull_plants` in `vk_engine/plant_gpu.cpp` right after `plant_cull.comp`, once per kind): no list of automatic grass exists. Every frame the 4 x 4 block of cells round the camera is gone through: N points of the R2 sequence a cell, 5 candidates a point on a disc of a metre (clumps), range = min(setting, 3 cells), everything shrinking away over the second half of the range (the game's placed grass has no dissolve), then the ground (height and slope from the terrain's height map, binding 6; what may grow from the lawn mask, binding 7, or from `ScatterSet::tuft_rules`, binding 5), density (keep 1 - 1/(10 d + 1)), the six-octave clustering noise (long tufts only), a turn and a size from hashes, frustum and depth-pyramid culling, and the record appended to the species' three (level of detail) lists - the lists `plant_cull.comp` fills with the hand-placed plants, drawn as before. The cells are fixed in the world, so a clump is the same one every frame. Each kind's numbers are in a table in `cull_plants` (`kinds`); they go to the shader after the species table in the buffer at binding 3.
  - *Beyond the home tile* the placement reads the ground from the picture the engine bakes round the camera there (`veg_stream.cpp` `wv_nf_poll`: 512 m at 1 m, heights in RGB, "grass may grow" in A; binding 8) and applies the wider world's grass rule (`grass_raymarch.frag`: keep in step) - so turf and fields carry on past the tile's edge. The vertex shader has no height map there: blades stand upright and are not re-seated.
  - *Fields* (`flower_fields_` in `vk_engine.h`: x, z, radius, how frayed the edge is; at most four, sent after each kind's numbers): a kind with `lawn == 2` grows only inside them, wherever the short grass may. The one kind so far is the **flowering meadow** (species 18, `build_meadow`: 16 to a clump, eleven of them stalks ending in a yellow head - mesh part 10, a grass blade in everything but the colour of its head, `scatter.frag`), placed to 230 m: every clump close by, from 12 % of the range a growing share shrinks away and the fifth that stays grows, so the field stays closed from far off at a fraction of the clumps. The launch site has one (`rocket.cpp`, radius 205 m round the pad). The kind is not dispatched at all when the camera is out of reach of every field. `VIBE_NO_MEADOW=1` leaves it out. A new field: one `push_back`; a new kind of field plant: a mesh, a species number, a row in `kinds`.
  - *No grass in the snow, and a field's far colour* (2026-11-25, later). Placement (`tuft_place.comp`, step 4) now applies the ground's own snow line (`getMaterial` in `terrain_rm_shading_helpers.glsl`: 60 - 80 m by a slow noise, up to 16 m lower on a north face, higher on a south face - keep the two in step): over the 9 m of height below the line the clumps thin out and shrink to 45 %, none from the line up; grass that grows by the lawn's mask also ends where the ground's green does on the home tile (55 - 75 m, `grassEligibility`) - before, the mask alone decided there and turf stood on white ground. In winter the kind's numbers carry the snow cover (`P_FADE2.z`): clumps shorter than 0.3 m are left out under full snow, taller than 0.6 m all stand (the turf goes, long tufts and the meadow stay). The meadow's plants are half as long again (stalks 0.43 - 0.73 m, `kMeadowShape[0]` 0.66). The ground inside a flower field takes the meadow's mean colour with distance (`terrain_rm_main_light.glsl` after the wider world's turf, `rm_flower_field` in the helpers: nothing within 20 m, all of it by 185 m - the plants end at 230 m), so the field does not turn green where its plants stop; the fields reach the terrain shader as `scene.flower_field[4]` (appended to the scene constants, filled in `descriptors.cpp`, in the baked ground texture's hash). `VIBE_NO_FIELD_TINT=1` leaves the tint out.
  - *Cover, near and far* (2026-11-25, after two rings were seen round the camera): the turf's and the meadow's levels of detail are built by hand (`build_grass_turf` / `build_meadow` with a level: all 32 blades to 22 m, every other one wider to 48 m, every fourth beyond) - 16 blades left the ground showing between them close by when looked at from above, and the general simplifier lost the meadow's flower heads. A kind that thins out with distance (the meadow) grows what stays by 1 / sqrt(share kept), so the ground it covers is the same at every distance. A person standing still presses the grass only 40 % down (`grass_trail.frag`; the game: flat).
  - *The rules picture* for the long tufts (`ScatterSet::tuft_rules`, 1024 x 1024 over the plants' 560 m square, made by `scatter.cpp`): every rule that used to reject a tuft on the CPU (painted grass, buildings, the road, water, rock, thinner under trees), and dryness. A crater clears its patch (`craters.cpp`). Whatever must keep long grass away has to be in `ScatterParams` when the plants are built; whatever must keep turf away has to be cleared from the lawn mask.
  - *The record* is the 32-byte plant instance: a NEGATIVE size marks GPU-placed grass (already faded: `scatter.vert` does not fade it again).
  - *Vertex shader* (`shaders/tuft_cd.glsl`, included by `scatter.vert`): the wind here (global wind, turned and strengthened by the wind field within 64 m), its fade (full to 48 m, none from 64 m), the sway of the whole clump, the per-blade "branch" term, the ripple, the "tumble" (the blade as a whole about its root: a twist and a lean down the wind), the basis on the ground's normal, every blade set on the ground under its own root (and a turf blade over bare ground cut), then the trail, read at the blade's root: the blade turns about its root by up to 0.3 pi away from the push. What the game's meshes carry per vertex (the blade's root, direction, weights) is worked out from the blade's number: the meshes number their blades (uv.y = (blade + along) / 32) and place their roots on a spiral the shader repeats (`tuft_root` / `tuft_cd`; the shape numbers are `ScatterSet::kTuftShape` / `kTurfShape`, sent in `pc.tex_params`). Hand-placed tufts also get a lean of their own, as the game's seeded instances do.
  - *The trail* (`vk_engine/grass_trail.cpp`, `shaders/grass_trail.frag`, `grass_trail_h.frag`; scene bindings 41 - 44): 256 x 256 texels of 25 cm round the player. One picture holds direction, speed and how far down (decay exp2(-1.7847 dt), climbing under a body at the game's rate), the other between which heights the body stood - a blade reacts only when its root lies between them, so nothing bends under a bridge. Pushers: the player on foot, the players' cars, the world's people and their cars - the nearest twelve, a person 0.6 m, a car 2.1 m.
  - *The wind field* (`vk_engine/wind_fluid.cpp`, `shaders/wind_fluid.comp`; scene binding 45): the game's fluid simulation on a 48^3 grid of 2 m cells round the camera - advect, forces, curl, vorticity confinement, 8 pressure steps, gradient, history - stirred by whatever moves near by (the player, cars, people). Grid size, cell and step count are ours (the game's CPU sets them). `VIBE_TEST_WIND=<power>` puts a vortex in front of the camera.
  - *Pixel shader* (`scatter.frag` part 8): the ground's hue at the blade's own luminance, back faces x1.1, roughness 0.45 + 0.005 a metre. Material id 2 = grass: `deferred_lighting.frag` adds the game's back-light for its foliage ids in place of the leaf lobes: diffuse += max(0, 0.3 - N.L) x 0.2319 x the shadow term (the phase-and-thickness formula used at first belongs to other shading ids - corrected after the tree shaders were read, `dev/cd-shaders/trees/REPORT.md`).
  - *Shadows*: GPU-placed grass is drawn into the small plants' shadow maps (`clutter_shadow.cpp`, `shaders/tuft_shadow.vert`: the colour pass's blade maths, so flattened or wind-blown grass casts the shadow of what is drawn) - the 64 m map, the 16 m near map and the flashlight's, not the lanterns' (the meadow: not the 64 m map). It is drawn from the lists the placement filled for the camera, so grass out of view casts no shadow into it. Ours: the game's dynamic cascades leave out everything under 1.5 m bounding radius. `VIBE_NO_GRASS_SHADOW=1` none, `VIBE_GRASS_SHADOW=1` long tufts only, `=2` near map only. Cost read on a busy GPU: about +0.7 ms in `clutter_shadow` on the frames the maps are redrawn (every other one).
  - *Not carried over, and why*: the game's instance record of four rows (the ground normal is looked up again in the vertex shader); its top-down captures of the terrain and of everything that moves (ours: the height map, the lawn mask, a list of pushers); its screen-space contact shadow and AO tweak for foliage; the terrain-paste pass that blends ground material onto the grass's base. All per-asset and per-quality numbers (cell size, points a cell, density, noise size, sizes, fade distances, mesh shapes) are ours: they are not in the shaders.
  - Switches: `VIBE_NO_TUFTS=1` no automatic grass (and the raymarched lawn back); `VIBE_LAWN_RM=1` the raymarched lawn instead of turf; `VIBE_TUFTS_CPU=1` the long tufts placed on the CPU as before (also with `VIBE_NO_PLANT_GPU=1`); `VIBE_NO_TUFTS_GPU=1`; `VIBE_NO_GRASS_TRAIL=1`; `VIBE_TEST_TRAIL_R=<m>`; `VIBE_NO_WIND_FLUID=1`; `VIBE_TEST_WIND=<power>`. GPU mark `trail_wind` = the trail and the wind field. Checked by screenshots only (no unit tests); costs could not be measured on an idle GPU yet.
- **meshoptimizer** (FetchContent) is available engine-wide: simplification, index / vertex ordering, meshlets. `clusterlod.h` from the same project is the starting point for Nanite-style cluster LOD if that is built.
- **terrain_raymarch.frag** is compiled three ways: normal (with winter: snow layer, footprints, ice), `VIBE_SUMMER` (no winter code; bound while there is no snow) and `VIBE_WATER`.

## Cluster LOD (Nanite-style) - rocks

- `src/engine/cluster_lod.h/.cpp`: builds a cluster hierarchy for a mesh with meshoptimizer's `clusterlod.h` (clusters of <=128 triangles, simplified level by level; each cluster carries its own and its parent group's error sphere).
- `vk_engine/cluster_draw.cpp` + `shaders/cluster_cull.comp`: every frame, one compute thread per clustered instance culls it and tests each cluster (drawn when its error is under one pixel while its parent's is not), writing one draw command per cluster; `draw_clusters` issues them with `vkCmdDrawIndexedIndirectCount` through the vegetation pipelines.
- Static world meshes (castle, chapel, gravestones; parts of 3000+ triangles): ``init_static_clusters`` / ``cull_static_clusters`` in the same file with ``static_cluster_cull.comp`` - one thread per cluster, one indirect-count draw per mesh part (camera passes only; the sun shadow map draws whole parts). ``VIBE_NO_STATIC_CLUSTERS=1`` turns it off.
- The sun shadow map draws from the same hierarchy: a second selection per frame (light box, cut at 2 px by the camera's distance).
- Streaming: a part's finest level (the original triangles, half the index data) lives in ``cache/stream_*.idx`` and is read by a background thread into a full index buffer when the camera comes within reach (``stream_static_clusters``); the coarse buffer is a prefix of the full one, the shader's ``loaded`` mask makes level 1 stand in meanwhile. ``VIBE_STREAM_ALL=1`` keeps everything loaded. Vertices and textures are not streamed.
- Sub-pixel triangles: the cut keeps the finest triangles at about a pixel or more when first drawn (logged at start), so no software rasteriser is used.
- Hierarchies are cached in ``cache/cluster_<hash>.bin`` (``build_cluster_mesh_cached``; bump ``kCacheVersion`` when the builder changes).
- Occlusion uses LAST frame's depth pyramid on purpose: the main occluder, the terrain, is ray-marched after the object passes, so this frame's depth does not contain it when the culling runs (a two-pass scheme would lose the hills).
- Clustered rocks: the Blender-baked rock archetypes of the vegetation system (their instances are sorted to the end of the instance buffer; `veg_cluster_first_` / `veg_cluster_count_`). `VIBE_NO_CLUSTERS=1` draws them with the old discrete levels.
- To cluster another mesh type: build a `ClusterMesh`, add it to the cluster / table buffers in `init_cluster_draw`, and give its instances slots like the rocks. Each drawn cluster is tested on its own against the view and last frame's depth pyramid (`cluster_cull.comp`, `static_cluster_cull.comp` - checked 2026-11-23: this line used to say it was not done). Not yet done: ray-tracing structures from clusters (the structures are built from the archetypes' LOD 1 meshes and the static parts' full meshes).

## Leaving the planet (state: 2026-11-18)

**Rule: nothing below 1 km altitude may change.** Every piece here is gated by the camera's height.

What exists:
- **Flight**: free-fly (V) speeds up with height above the ground (x altitude / 60 m, up to 2000x), ceiling 1000 km (`vk_engine.cpp`, fly block).
- **The planet as a far field** (`compose.frag` `planet_surface`, used by `atmo_sky` in the sky-fill and compose passes): view rays that leave the local landscape and meet the planet's sphere (radius 6360 km, the atmosphere model's) are shaded as land / sea / snow / weather systems with day and night; fades in between 1 and 6 km. Above the atmosphere (40 - 110 km) the sky-view LUT is replaced by black space, a veil over the day side and a limb glow.
- **Directions at height**: above 2 km the sky passes get the inverse of projection x rotation only (`frame.cpp`), because the full inverse view-projection loses the view directions in float rounding.
- **Local systems step aside**: the terrain march returns at once when the landscape lies beyond its 1500 m range (`terrain_rm_heightfield.glsl`); the water plane is not drawn beyond 2.5 km from above 1 km; the local cloud layer fades out over 3 km above its top.

- **The middle distance** (`planet_surface`, the `w_mid` block): out to 45 - 90 km from home the sphere is shaded from the landscape's own height formula (`pl_terrain` = `terrainM` without plateau and sculpting, with only the octaves the view resolves), with the same height layers (sand / grass / dirt / rock / snow from the terrain settings), water level, ground snow and the aerial-perspective volume's haze. So the country seen from an aircraft is the one walked on. The CPU hands the shader the camera's map position, water level, snow and the height layers through the tail of the exposure buffer (`frame.cpp`, before the culls; the compose push constants are full at 256 B). The planet's map is fixed to the world now (the camera's travel rotates the sphere under it); it used to follow the camera. Fades in between 1 and 1.2 km altitude; from 1 km up the water plane ends where the terrain march ends (1.5 km).
- **The raymarched landscape from the air** (`terrain_rm_heightfield.glsl` `rm_far_limit` / `raymarch_far`, called from `terrain_raymarch.frag` only above 1 km for rays the normal march cannot reach): the march range grows with altitude (1.5 km at 1 km up, 80 km from about 1.5 km up), with all octaves so lakes and the snow line stay put. Depth for those hits is clamped just in front of the sky value, as before. The ground haze grows slowly past 1500 m and counts only the part of the ray in the 2.5 km of air near the ground (`rm_haze_dist`; `planet_surface` applies the same to the far field behind it). 
- **Objects from the air** (`world_render.cpp` `view_far_` / `object_far_`): up to 1 km altitude the projection is 0.05 .. 1500 m as always; above it the far plane moves out (to 30 km) and trees, rocks and buildings are drawn to 3.8 km (`veg_cull.comp`, `cluster_cull.comp` take `object_far_`; the upscalers get `view_far_`). The near plane stays (the weapon in the hand), so depth out there is metres coarse - fine for objects a few pixels tall. The 3.8 km follows from where the terrain shader clamps its depth (0.999989); going further needs reversed depth, and camera-relative positions beyond ~1000 km.
- **The hand-overs, castle to space** (state 2026-11-18; all thresholds are camera altitude):
  - The far march runs from the ground (range 1.5 km + 100 m per metre of height, 80 km from 0.8 km up); the far field comes in between 100 and 220 m and the cross-fade into it with it (distance = where the ray first passes below the highest ground, so it starts above 220 m); the water plane ends with the range from 220 m; it draws in again between 25 and 60 km, where the painted far field and its weather take over); the water plane ends with it; the planet's far field fades in (300 - 450 m).
  - The raymarched ground cross-fades into the far field over the outer half of its range, in `compose.frag` (after the aerial perspective; the distance is that of the ground under the pixel's ray). Keep `rm_far_limit` and that block in step.
  - Far hits get their normal from the height formula (`calcNormal`, t > 1500), a fixed share of occlusion and no contrast curve - beyond the range of the AO / shadow terms the ground was a step brighter and sunlit snow went to plain white.
  - 1 km: far plane and object distance grow (above).
  - Above the cloud layer's top the clouds are composited over the land (`clouds.comp` renders over far geometry then; `compose.frag` filters them wide); they fade out 4 - 14 km above the top.
  - 20 - 110 km: sunlight on the far field goes from the engine's own term to the orbit value; from 40 km the sky-view LUT gives way to space and the veil.
  - The view-projection inverse is computed in double precision, the terrain rays and the viewmodel are camera-relative (float positions shook with altitude).
  - The far field's home country (same height layers, snow, average look) reaches 350 - 900 km from home.
  - **Clouds: one weather map for every view.** `clouds.comp` grows its volumetric clouds where a 2D weather field says so; `compose.frag` `weather_cloud()` is the same field (same hash, cells, wind drift, coverage). The clouds painted on the raymarched land (compose, before the cross-fade) and on the planet's far field (`planet_surface`) come from it, weighted by `weather_paint_w()` = how far the volumetric ones have faded. So what is seen from orbit over home is what is found on the way down. From inside or above the layer the volumetric clouds are rendered over the land as well (the land lies below the layer's base) and composited before the upscaler by the `CLOUDS_OVER` variant of compose.frag (`frame.cpp`, next to the sun-shaft pre-pass). Change the field in both places.
  - Test hooks: `VIBE_TEST_FLIGHT=<y0>,<y1>,<frames>[,<yaw per frame>]` (with `VIBE_TEST_HOLD`) flies the camera between two altitudes in one motion; with `--frames` a given frame is the same in every run, so a flight can be captured frame by frame.
  - **One lighting at every altitude.** The far field's land (`planet_surface`) and the painted clouds are lit as `terrain_raymarch.frag` lights the landscape: the same sunlight (`u_exposure.sun_land` = scene.sun_color, golden hour included), the same sky fill (scene.sky_color x 0.75 x (0.5 + 0.5 up)) and the same direction; around the planet the sun's strength changes only relative to home. There is no dimming toward orbit. In the terrain shader `rm_air_look()` blends the ground look (occlusion, contrast curve) into the air look by distance and by camera height (250 - 1200 m), so the land right below is not a brighter patch.
  - **The world as one formula at every zoom level: `shaders/world_map.glsl`.** Included (the same text) by `terrain_rm_noise_road.glsl` for the raymarched landscape and by `compose.frag` for the planet's far field, each with its own `WM_NOISE` (same hash). `wm_continent(km)`: > 0 land, < 0 sea - a domain-warped fractal for continents (2400 .. 150 km), a detail band for coasts (90 .. 5 km) and for the shoreline (3 km .. 700 m); home is placed on a continent's east coast by two broad terms laid out on a bent copy of the map. `wm_ranges`: where the high ranges stand. `wm_height(a, wp, water)`: every height the landscape formula makes passes through it - home (1.5 km) untouched, then low country with ranges, a coastal plain coming down to the beach, shallows, shelf and deep sea bed. The far field takes its land mask, shallows, ranges and snow from the same functions. The CPU has its own copy (`terrain.cpp` namespace `wm`, `world_ground_height`; keep it in step line for line): outside the baked home tile `sample_terrain_height` returns it, so the player can land, walk and swim out there. Physics bodies (Jolt) still exist on the home tile only. `VIBE_TEST_GROUND=1` (with `VIBE_TEST_HOLD`) stands the held camera on that ground - a capture shows whether it is the drawn ground. Cost: it roughly tripled the terrain march from the air (0.6 -> 1.8 ms at 1280x720); the continent value could be evaluated once per pixel instead of per step.

**The globe** (`engine/globe.h/.cpp`; state 2026-11-20). The planet is a real sphere (radius 6360 km); flying on round it comes back home. Three parts:
- *Charts.* The engine still works in flat float coordinates (x east, y up, z south). They are a chart of part of the planet: the sphere unrolled round one point (distance and bearing from it kept), with a frame there (which way its east / up / south point in the planet's frame; home is the planet's top, identity). Home's chart reaches 100 km. Beyond it `globe_update` (rocket.cpp, every frame) gives the player a far chart, re-anchored under them every 16 km, its origin at local (64 km, 0) - so coordinates stay under 100 km (float fine to a centimetre) and nothing of home's content, which sits at its home coordinates near (0, 0), is ever in range. Within 92 km of home the home chart is taken up again. `globe_enter` moves the player between charts (same place and heading on the planet). The shaders get the chart in the scene UBO (`globe_frame`, `globe_info`).
- *The world on the sphere* (`shaders/world_map.glsl` "The globe", mirrored in `terrain.cpp` + `globe.cpp`). Near home the world is the flat formula it always was (home coordinates = the azimuthal map round home). That map cannot go round a sphere, so away from home the world is made from 3D noise at the point: the landscape FBM between 40 and 90 km from home (`wm_shape3` / `rm_shape`), continents and ranges between 1800 and 3200 km (`wm_continent_globe`). A float cannot hold a position on the planet finely enough for the FBM's small octaves: the CPU supplies, per octave, the lattice cell and fraction of a nearby origin in double precision (`globe::lattice`; `globe_cell` / `globe_frac` in the scene UBO for the chart's origin, `gcell` / `gfrac` in the exposure buffer for the camera's ground point) and the shader adds the local offset. In a far chart the terrain shader switches off everything that belongs to home's tile (`kHeightmapSide` is nothing there, no plateau, no road, `rm_home_dist`).
- *The far field* (`compose.frag` `planet_surface`) gets the frame at the camera's ground point (`gf0..2`) instead of the old "how far has the camera travelled over the flat map", and its mid-distance heights (`pl_terrain`) come from the same formula everywhere on the planet.
- The rocket's simulation plane is placed on the planet through the pad; its drawn position is the straight line from the eye in the eye's own frame, in whatever chart. The rocket camera simply sits beside it and the chart follows.
- The sun (`environment.cpp`, `from_here`): the clock is home's; the sun and moon stand where they stand over the planet and are seen from the player's own east / up / north, so it is another time of day elsewhere and night on the far side.
- Not done: weather, the shader's fine ground noise and the streamed trees are laid out in chart coordinates, so they change when a far chart is re-anchored; multiplayer positions assume home's chart. Test: set `player_pos_x` (home coordinates, m) beyond 100000 in the cfg copy - the engine re-anchors on the first frame (`[globe]` in the log).

**The launch site** (`vk_engine/rocket.cpp`): a terrace in the world formula (`wm_pad` in `shaders/world_map.glsl`, `wm::pad` in `terrain.cpp`, `kLaunchSite*` in `terrain.h` - keep in step) at (1600, 830) on the coast, outside the home tile. Apron, mount, tower, tanks, masts and the control house are static level meshes from `assets/rocket/*.mesh` (written by `tools/rocket/make_rocket.py`, run in Blender; it also writes the hull and concrete textures, slots 23 / 24, whose normal maps the engine derives from the `_h` height images). The rocket is drawn with a model matrix (`draw_rocket`, parts in `rocket_parts_`, in the TLAS while near). E at the button in the house starts a 10 s count. The flight is `RocketSim` (`engine/rocket_sim.cpp`): a point mass in the orbit's plane, planet-centred, doubles, fixed 0.05 s step - gravity turn, closed-loop climb to a 180 km circular orbit, one revolution, a retrograde burn timed by running a copy of the flight ahead, drag-only entry, zero-effort-miss landing burn onto the mount (93 minutes of flight). `tick_rocket` runs it faster than the clock while the rocket is out of sight (x5 above the air, x60 in orbit). Its world position is the simulation's on the round planet (it sets below the horizon downrange and returns from the west). Beyond the object range it is drawn as a proxy on the line of sight (`rocket_view`: pulled in to 0.9 x the object far plane and scaled by the same ratio; hidden when the planet or - by a CPU march over the ground - the landscape is in the way); exhaust, entry glow, smoke trail and the pad's clouds are FX ribbons (`rocket_build_fx`, consumed in `fx.cpp`). C toggles the rocket camera (`rocket_toggle_camera`; the cam block in `tick_rocket`): the view rides 190 m beside the rocket, the mouse turns it.
