- **The mixer's cost** is measured now (`AudioEngine::mixer_cost_take`: the device callback is miniaudio's own with a clock round it - two clock reads and three atomic adds a block, no allocation, no virtual call; `VIBE_LOG_AUDIO_COST=1` logs it every 300 frames with the engine voices held and loops mixed). On the 9800X3D, 10 ms blocks: 2.5 - 4 us a block with no car (0.03 % of a core), about 55 us with two near cars (8 loops: 0.55 %) - about 6.5 us a loop a block. One pass of two pickups by the station, the lines with a voice held: 0.41 % of a core before (`VIBE_CAR_SOUND_OLD=1`), 0.27 % after - one sample of sparse traffic, not a benchmark; the saving is the loops that are no longer mixed between 75 and 240 m. Not heard by ear.

### Sound in space (2026-11-25; car sound, fifth pass)

"Still the fading of car sounds is not so nice. Make a really nice sound engine, with echo, doppler, fading, dampening." The four passes above each changed a curve inside `car_audio_tick`; this one takes the question out of it. Every moving source goes through ONE spatialiser, and `car_audio_tick` only says where each car is, how fast it goes and how loud its engine is beside it. (`engine/car_sound_curve.h` and `VIBE_CAR_SOUND_OLD` / `VIBE_LOG_CAR_SOUND_CURVE` are gone.)

**Files.**
- `engine/sound_space.h` - the curves, as plain functions of numbers, and every constant an ear may want to turn (each with what it does). No audio library: `tests/test_sound_space.cpp` checks them.
- `engine/sound_dsp.h` - what runs on the audio thread: `SpatialVoice` (one a source) and `SpaceReverb` (one for everything). Its rule: no allocation, lock, system call or per-sample branch in `process()`; the game thread talks to it through atomics only, and every parameter is followed a sample at a time (a one-pole, 20 ms for a voice, 60 ms for the reverb) - whatever the game writes once a frame arrives as a curve.
- `engine/audio.cpp` "SOUND IN SPACE" - the two kinds of node in miniaudio's graph (a source's sounds, unspatialised -> its `SpatialNode` -> bus 0 to the endpoint, bus 1 to the one `ReverbNode` -> endpoint) and `Impl::spatial_update`: once a frame for each source, the curves from where it and the listener are, followed over time (loudness up in 0.10 s, down in 0.25 s; what is in the way 0.30 s; Doppler 0.15 s; the surroundings 0.6 s).
- `vk_engine/sound_env.cpp` (state: `vk_engine/state/sound_env.h`) - what the audio engine cannot know: the surroundings (`sound_env_tick`, four times a second) and whether something is in the line to a source (`sound_occlusion`, asked about five times a second for each source). Game thread, with tests the game already has; nothing is traced.

**The interface for anything that sounds from a place** (`engine/audio.h`):
- `h = source_start(ClipID, { ref_m, top_hz }, loop)`; every frame `source_update(h, pos, vel, level, pitch)`; `source_stop(h)` (fades to nothing, then frees itself). `level` 1 = the clip as recorded, heard from within `ref_m`; `top_hz` = how bright it is from beside it. Returns `Heard` (gain, distance, low-pass, Doppler ratio, pan, reverb share, blocked).
- Engines are the same thing with the loops' cross-fade on top: `engine_start(set, shape)`, `engine_update(h, pos, vel, rpm, load, level, ridden_hz, simple)`. `ridden_hz > 0`: the listener rides in or with it - no distance law, no Doppler, in the middle, through that low-pass (12 kHz chase view, the cabin's 2.4 kHz).
- `set_space(Space)`, `set_occlusion_probe(fn)`: `sound_env.cpp` calls them; nothing else needs to.

**The curves** (`sound_space.h`; a car is `{ ref 6 m, top 4500 Hz }`):
- loudness `gain(d) = ref / sqrt(ref^2 + d^2) x 10^(-0.06 d / 20) x (1 - smoothstep(300, 450, d))`: the inverse of the distance (-6 dB a doubling), 0.06 dB a metre for ground and air, and a window to exactly nothing. One slope from beside the car to silence; where the window begins the car is 52 dB down already.
- tone `cutoff(d) = top / (1 + d / 30)`, not under 300 Hz: a low-pass of two one-pole stages (12 dB an octave, soft knee; the figure is its -3 dB point), per sample, never re-designed in steps (the biquad before was, every 4 % of change).
- Doppler `(c - v_listener) / (c - v_source)` along the line between them, c = 343 m/s, clamped 0.75 .. 1.35, nothing within 1 m, full from 5 m; applied by the voice's resampler together with the engine's speed (one resampling).
- direction: equal-power panning by the bearing (width 0.85), the far ear up to 0.66 ms later (a fractional delay) and up to 70 % through a 1.5 kHz low-pass (the head), a source behind 30 % duller, a source within a couple of metres in the middle.
- blocked (0..1): loudness x (1 .. 0.40), cut-off x (1 .. 0.28). The land: the line over the height field, 4 m of hill = fully (as fx.cpp does for a gun). Walls: the level's collision boxes at least 3 m long and 2 m high within 250 m, a segment test, 0.85.
- reverb share `0.5 x gain^0.8 x Space::amount`: it falls slower than the sound itself - far off a source is heard more by what comes back than by itself.
- a voice is not mixed under -72 dB (`kFloor`, 0.00025) and let go there if its car is no longer one of the heard; a car gets a voice when the distance alone leaves it above twice that (about 375 m), the nearest 8, and one that has a voice counts as 15 % nearer (two cars equally far do not take the last voice from each other in turn).

| distance | gain | dB | low-pass | reverb share (dB, x amount) | Doppler at 30 m/s, coming / going |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 m | 1.000 | 0 | 4500 Hz | -6.0 | 1.000 / 1.000 |
| 3 m | 0.876 | -1.1 | 4091 Hz | -6.9 | 1.048 / 0.960 |
| 6 m | 0.678 | -3.4 | 3750 Hz | -8.7 | 1.096 / 0.920 |
| 12 m | 0.412 | -7.7 | 3214 Hz | -12.2 | 1.096 / 0.920 |
| 25 m | 0.196 | -14.1 | 2455 Hz | -17.3 | |
| 50 m | 0.084 | -21.5 | 1688 Hz | -23.2 | |
| 75 m | 0.048 | -26.5 | 1286 Hz | -27.2 | |
| 100 m | 0.030 | -30.5 | 1038 Hz | -30.4 | |
| 150 m | 0.014 | -37.0 | 750 Hz | -35.6 | |
| 200 m | 0.0075 | -42.5 | 587 Hz | -40.0 | |
| 250 m | 0.0043 | -47.4 | 482 Hz | -43.9 | |
| 300 m | 0.0025 | -52.0 | 409 Hz | -47.6 | |
| 350 m | 0.0011 | -58.9 | 355 Hz | -53.1 | |
| 400 m | 0.00025 | -72.2 | 314 Hz | -63.8 | |
| 450 m | 0 | - | 300 Hz | - | |

(the code's own output: `VIBE_LOG_SOUND_CURVE=1`; the table before, for comparison: -37 dB and 482 Hz at 100 m, nothing at 180 m.)

**The reverb** (`SpaceReverb`): a pre-delay line (12 ms to the tail) with three early reflections (17, 31, 47 ms: left, right, both) and ONE slap-back echo tapped off it (through 2.6 kHz, from its side; a new delay is cross-faded in over 0.3 s on a second tap, never slid - sliding bends the pitch), and a tail of four delay lines (32.5, 40.6, 50.8, 60.8 ms) fed back through a Householder matrix with a low-pass in the loop; the feedback is set from the wanted decay and the tail is levelled so that a longer decay is longer, not louder. It is worked on when nothing comes in (the tail rings out) and stops itself once nothing has come in for longer than any echo and its output is under -120 dB.

**The surroundings** (`sound_env_tick` -> `Space`): amount `0.03 + 0.55 land + 0.45 walls + 0.28 woods + 0.70 bridge (+ 0.15 under a roof)`, tail `0.35 s + 1.5 land + 0.5 walls + 0.3 woods + 0.6 bridge`, the early reflections' share from walls / bridge / roof, the tail's damping from 4.5 kHz down to 1.8 kHz in woods.
- land: the height in 8 directions at 10, 20, 35, 60, 100, 160 m - how much of the horizon stands above the ear (24 degrees all round = 1), and the best steep face (6 m above the ear, 20 degrees up) gives the echo: after there-and-back at 343 m/s, from its side.
- walls: 8 rays of 40 m against the big boxes; a wall beyond 8 m may give the echo if no cliff is better.
- a roof (the rain's shelters): with walls within 15 m on 5 of 8 sides it is a room - what is outside x 0.5 through 1.8 kHz, amount at most 0.25, tail 0.5 s, and the walls are not counted a second time as "in the way"; without, a canopy (early reflections).
- the cabin (driving, nearest view): what is outside x 0.45 through 1.4 kHz (as before).
- the stone bridge over the ear; `world_forest` at the ear and 22 m round it.

**Also found on the way**: a traffic car's speed was taken from where it was a frame ago; now and then that came out up to a quarter wrong for a frame (19 -> 14 m/s), which went into the engine's speed, its load and the Doppler shift - a flutter in its note. Cars of the NPC engine now use the speed the NPC engine drives them at, along their nose.

**The siren** (2026-11-25; "sound of alarms of police cars is not fading correctly when distance becomes larger"). It was the one moving sound that did not go through the spatialiser: a tracked voice with its own law in `siren_audio_tick`. What was wrong with it:
- the law: half at 40 m, then by the SQUARE of the distance - 41 dB down at 300 m already, with no loss of highs and no echo: a siren that drove off sank away within the first 200 m and was then a thin, unchanged tone;
- it sounded only while the car was DRAWN: the voice hung on the roof bar, and a bar is only on a police car that is lent a model (the nearest 15 NPC cars within 700 m). A police car that was the sixteenth nearest had no siren at any distance, and got one - at whatever loudness its distance gave, within an eighth of a second - when a model came free; the other way round it was cut off in a third of a second;
- the voice belonged to the bar SLOT (two, dealt out anew every frame in the order of the drawn police cars): when the first police car left view, the second car's siren moved to the first slot's voice - another place in the clip, another place in the world, another loudness;
- its Doppler shift came from where the lamps were a frame ago (the flutter noted above), and the clip was started again by hand when it ran out.
Now: a source like any other. `siren_audio_tick` (called from `car_audio_tick`) goes through the NPC world's views - every police car with its siren on, by its agent number, from the NPC engine's own place and speed - and for each one within hearing holds a looping source (`source_start(ClipID::Siren, kSirenShape, loop, start)`; each car begins elsewhere in the clip); `source_update` every frame; `source_stop` when it is switched off, taken or gone (it fades where it was). The same on a client (the views are replicated; protocol unchanged). `Shape` got three more numbers for a source that is made to carry - `hear_m`, `excess_db_m`, `air_half_m` (an engine: 450 m, 0.06 dB/m, 30 m; `kSirenShape`: ref 20 m, top 9 kHz, 1000 m, 0.02 dB/m, 120 m; the window to nothing begins at two thirds of `hear_m`) - and the siren's level beside the car is 0.55 (`VIBE_SIREN_GAIN`). The curve: -29.5 dB at 300 m, -41.5 dB at 600 m, -52 dB at 800 m, nothing at 1000 m (`tests/test_sound_space.cpp`). Checked by numbers, not by ear: `VIBE_TEST_SIREN=stay VIBE_TEST_HOLD=1 VIBE_LOG_TRAFFIC=1` puts the eye 12 m beside a police car and leaves it there; the log has the siren's distance and gain every half second - one fall from 0.42 at 16 m to 0 at 1000 m, through the moment the car's model is given back (717 m) without a step, pitch x0.903 going away at 36.8 m/s.

**Hooks**: `VIBE_LOG_SOUND_SPACE=<n>` (every n-th frame, 1 = every tenth: for each car with a voice the distance, gain, dB, low-pass, Doppler ratio, pan, reverb share, blocked, speed; and every 2 s the surroundings with what they were made from), `VIBE_LOG_SOUND_CURVE=1` (the table), `VIBE_LOG_AUDIO_COST=1`, `VIBE_LOG_CAR_SOUND=1`, `VIBE_CAR_SOUND_GAIN`, `VIBE_NO_CAR_SOUND` as before.

**Checked by numbers, not by ear** (nobody has listened):
- tests (`[sound]`, 8 cases): the loudness never rises with distance and never steps (a quarter of a metre at a time to 470 m), is 0 at 450 m; Doppler's four cases and its clamp; equal power in the two ears; a stepped gain comes out of a voice with no sample moving more than 0.002; the far ear's impulse arrives 24 samples late when told so; the reverb rings longer for a longer decay, does not build up, falls silent; the echo comes back after its 0.5 s on its side.
- a saloon passing a standing player at 16 m on the coast highway at 19 m/s (`VIBE_LOG_SOUND_SPACE=6`, 817 lines): first mixed at about 315 m (-72 dB), -60 dB at 190 m, -48 dB at 90 m, -29 dB at 16 m, and the same way out; low-pass 400 Hz -> 2900 Hz -> 300 Hz; Doppler 1.058 steady coming, through 1.01 at the nearest point, 0.951 going; pan from the middle to +0.88 and back. Biggest change between two lines 0.1 s apart while it was audible: 0.6 dB, 0.008 of Doppler ratio, 3.4 % of cut-off. Four cars in that run: 0.6 - 1.1 dB at the most (the 1.1 on a car that was speeding up from 8 to 26 m/s).
- the surroundings, one look each: the open highway `amount 0.03, tail 0.35 s, no echo`; inside the station's shop `roof 1, 6 sides near, room 1, outside x 0.50 through 1800 Hz`; under the pump canopy `roof 1, room 0, early 0.73, echo 0.31 after 179 ms from the left` (the shop's wall, 31 m off).
- **the mixer's cost** (9800X3D, 10 ms blocks, `VIBE_LOG_AUDIO_COST`, two runs of sparse traffic): nothing sounding 0.05 - 0.10 % of a core (5 - 10 us a block; the reverb idles); one far car (1 loop) 0.20 - 0.40 %; two cars with 5 loops mixed 0.46 - 0.72 % (46 - 72 us a block). The fourth pass's figure for two near cars with 8 loops was 0.55 %: the spatialiser and the reverb are within the run-to-run spread of the loops' own resampling. Not measured: 8 voices at once (the traffic there never gave more than 3).

**Not checked / open**: how any of it sounds. Valley, cliff echo, woods and bridge were not visited (their terms are reasoned; only open field, room and canopy were looked at in the log). The cabin and the driven car's own sound through the new voice were not run. Walls come from the home level's boxes only (a site's buildings do not block or echo yet); a canopy's posts and a big rock count as walls if they are 3 m long and 2 m high. Cars other players drive still take their speed from positions. One-shots (`play_at`, `play_effect`: guns, explosions, thunder) do not go through the spatialiser or the reverb yet - the interface would take them (`source_start(..., loop = false)`).

**Shot bodies: why they flew, and how they go down now (2026-11-25).**
- THE FAULT: a bolt is a physics body of 10 kg at 220 m/s. The first bolt is taken out by `npc_bolt_hits`, but the next one in flight struck the ragdoll's parts (2 - 28 kg) with its own weight: measured under continuous fire, the pelvis reached 35 m/s (`VIBE_TEST_BOLTS_HIT_BODIES=1` brings that back to compare); in the open the body left the scene. RULE: a ragdoll's parts are in the physics layer `CORPSE` (physics_world.cpp): the world, what moves and other bodies touch them - bolts and sparks do not. A bolt through a fallen body is found by the game (`corpse_bolt_hits`, combat.cpp): a knock of at most 22 N s on the part, and blood. A part's speed is capped at 25 m/s whatever hits it. Now: 8 m/s at most, within 0.2 m of where it fell.
- Every ragdoll says once, 3 s on, how far its pelvis went and its top speed (`[ragdoll] fell within ...`); more than 8 m is a WARN ("a body flew").
- Going down: see docs/animation/actions.md "Death". `VIBE_NO_DEATH_CLIPS=1`: the ragdoll at once, as before. `VIBE_TEST_NPC_KILL=<frame>[,<skip>]`: the six nearest people with a body are shot at that frame.
- The shot's push on a ragdoll is halved (0.7 m/s for the body, 3.5 m/s for the limb it struck; players' bodies too).

**NPCs and every player (2026-11-25, protocol 7).** What a player does to the NPC world is an event (net_state.h `Event`); the machine whose NPC world is the world (the host, or a machine alone) decides, every machine shows it. No bones, clips or poses cross the wire here either: the NPC state is 27 bytes an agent four times a second (place, heading, speed, pose, act, style), and each machine's animator picks and plays the clips from that.
- **A shot** (`Event::NpcHit`: which agent, where, the bolt's direction). The shooter decides the hit against what is on the shooter's screen, shows it at once and tells everybody; `npc_hit` on every machine: blood, and the body drawn for that person falls (each machine its own ragdoll from the same pose - the fall itself is not sent). The authority calls `npc::World::kill`; a copy keeps the person out of sight for 3 s (`npc_gone_`) until the host's state says the same.
- **A car** - three steps, the same alone and on a network (jeep.cpp, the comment above `npc_take_car`): ask (`Event::NpcCarTake` from a client), decide (`npc_host_take`: `take_car`, the next number in `jeep_all_`), make (`Event::NpcCarTaken` -> `npc_car_make` on every machine: a model of that kind and paint leaves the pool of lent cars and joins `jeep_all_` under that number; the one who asked gets in). From then on it is a car like the others (`jeep_net_send` / `jeep_net_receive`). RULE: `jeep_all_` must be the same list on every machine - a car is named by its number - so a car is only ever added through `npc_car_make`, in the order of the numbers (`npc_taken_wait_` holds one that came before the one in front of it).
- **Somebody who joins later**: a second after a new player appears every machine tells of the taken cars it knows, where they stand now (`npc_taken_tick`). Known gap: two machines that each took cars before they met have different cars under the same numbers.
- Tests: `tests/anim/test_net.cpp` `[npc]` (the wire form of a taken car, what is refused, the events pass the gate at once). Two instances on one PC: `VIBE_NET=1 VIBE_NET_PORT=48100 VIBE_TEST_REALTIME=1`, `VIBE_NET_ID=100` on the one that must host (the lower id hosts), `VIBE_NPC_WARM=200` on it; the client with `VIBE_TEST_FIRE=1500` at (1602.6, 42.9, 532.0) yaw 1.945 pitch -0.085, or `VIBE_TEST_USE=1500` at (1617.0, 42.6, 558.5) yaw -1.57. Both logs then show "person 15 shot" / "NPC car 18 ... is a players' car now: number 8", the client "the host gave us NPC car 18 ... in it 1". Checked 2026-11-25 this way; not checked: a third player watching, a late joiner.

**The diner's posters (2026-11-25).** Eighteen posters of the base ("Cape Vibe Space Command": recruiting, 25 years, launch night party, base rules, safety, history, launch schedule, softball, a lost cat, ...) hang in the diner: ten along the party wall, eight in the band over the kitchen's pass. Pictures: `tools/gasstation/posters/p00..p17.png` (image generator, 2:3). `tools/gasstation/make_poster_atlas.ps1` packs them UNDER the pavilion's screens in the same picture, `assets/office/tex/screens.png` (texture slot 36, now 4096 x 4096: rows 0..1151 the 4 x 2 screens from `tools/office/screens_src.png`, below them 6 x 3 poster cells of 682.67 x 981) - no texture slot was free. `poster(i, x, y, z0, face)` in `make_gasstation.py` hangs number i; material `g_poster` (rocket.cpp table) is self-lit and samples the atlas with the cell's own uv, with v + 2 to tell cube.frag's display block (which picks a changing picture by number, and now divides by the screens' share of the atlas) that it is paper. Another poster: put the picture in the folder, run the atlas script, add a `poster()` line, run the Blender script.
- **Thin layers on a wall (the posters that showed their frame's colour by the triangle and flickered when walking)**: not the mesh, the texture or the culling - depth. A mesh part whose vertices are world positions (model = identity) had `mvp * position` add terms the size of the world coordinate (1600 m, one float step 0.12 mm) per vertex, clip z and clip w rounded apart; depth is their ratio, so a surface moved by error x distance / near plane (0.05 m): centimetres at 9 m, different at each vertex - more than the 4 mm between a poster and its frame. `cube.vert` now transforms such a vertex from the eye (`inPos_ext - scene.camera_pos`, exact in floats; `mvp` without its translation) and adds the eye's clip position, one value per draw (the depth pre-pass uses the same shader, so the two passes still agree). Rule: a vertex shader that draws world-space meshes must not multiply the full matrix with a large world position; millimetre layers are then safe at room distances.

**The launch site's lamps behind a mountain (2026-11-25).** The lamps' flares (`fx.cpp`, `pad_lights_`) are drawn over the picture and far land is not in the depth buffer, so from across the hills they shone through. Each lamp further than 350 m now has its line of sight marched over the world's ground formula (a third of the lamps a frame, the answer eased). `VIBE_TEST_PAD_LIGHTS_THROUGH=1`: as before. Not confirmed by a picture (two tries found no spot where the lamps showed either way).

**Cars with people in them, cars on the road, and people in cars (2026-11-25, protocol 8; unit-tested, NOT yet seen working in the game).**
- **Any NPC car that stands or barely rolls can be taken** (`npc::kTakeSpeed`, 1.5 m/s), whoever sits in it: `npc::World::take_car` puts them out beside it (`put_out`: at the site they go about as locals; out on the road they stand 40 s and are gone). Before: only a standing car with nobody in it.
- **Cars do not drive into players on foot** (`npc::World::set_walkers`, given every frame by `npc_tick` on the machine that steps the world: the local player and the other players' avatars). On the road a car brakes for somebody on its lane (within 2.3 m of the lane's middle, looked for 90 m ahead) and waits; on the site as for its own people. That is how a car in the traffic is stopped to be taken. `VIBE_NPC_NO_WALKERS=1`: as before.
- **The pool of lent cars is refilled** (`npc_car_add`, jeep.cpp) when one becomes a players' car; the old limit of eleven taken cars is gone, `net::kMaxCars` (32 players' cars in all) remains.
- **People in cars can be shot** (`npc_bolt_hits`: the bodies drawn in shown cars - driver and front passenger, within 90 m). `npc::World::kill` takes a hidden rider, or a car's number for the driver nobody of the NPC world sits for (the traffic the other way; `Event::NpcHit` then carries the car's number, `npc_nodriver_` hides him on a copy of the host's world). A car whose driver is shot rolls to a stop on its lane (pose Park at once), the others get out, and 120 s later it is cleared away. The body is handed to the physics at the car's speed; the lent car has no physics, so it falls through it on to the road.
- Hooks: `VIBE_TEST_NPC_SHOOT=<frame>[,<who>[,<skip>]]` turns the player to the nearest person the bolt can reach (who 1: in a car) and fires a real bolt (the log says when something stands right in front of the muzzle: `fire_projectile` then fires nothing); `VIBE_TEST_NPC_CAR=<frame>[,<throttle>[,<mode>]]` puts the player at the door of the nearest standing NPC car (mode 0) or on the lane 75 m ahead of the nearest driving one and waits for it to stop (mode 1), presses Use and drives (`[jeep] test:` lines; not with `VIBE_TEST_HOLD`).
- Tests: `tests/test_npc.cpp` - a car stops for a walker on its lane without touching him and is taken with its people in it; a driver shot (his own, and by the car's number).

### Engines and gearboxes: each kind of car its own (2026-11-25)

A kind of car is a handful of numbers in `PhysicsWorld::VehicleDesc` (set in `init_jeep`, jeep.cpp), all shares of `engine_max_rpm`:

| | engine | limit | idle | pulls hardest at | changes up, pedal down / held | change takes | loudness |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| sports car | flat-plane V8 (Ferrari F136) | 9000 | 1000 | 0.68 | 8600 / 3600 | 0.11 s | 1.0 |
| pickup | even-fire V6 | 4800 | 650 | 0.38 | 4320 / 2200 | 0.40 s | 0.85 |
| saloon | four-cylinder (Honda B18) | 6500 | 750 | 0.45 | 5980 / 2200 | 0.28 s | 0.42 |
| jeep | flat four (Subaru EJ25) | 4600 | 800 | 0.35 | 4140 / 2300 | 0.35 s | 0.80 |

- **One rule for the box**, `VehicleDesc::shift_up_rpm(pedal)` / `shift_down_rpm(pedal)`: early when the pedal is only held, at the top of the range with it down, a gear down when it is floored at low revs. The driven car's box (`PhysicsWorld::vehicle_input`, every frame) and the sound of every other car (`car_audio_tick`, which works a car's gear out of its road speed) use the same two functions. The change down is capped under what the engine turns right after a change up (the widest step between two gears x 0.72), or the box would go up and down without end.
- **The pedal the box sees is the driver's**, not what traction control lets through (`vehicle_input`'s `pedal`).
- **Why the cars sat on their limiters**: Jolt's automatic refuses a change up while a driven tyre slips more than 0.1 or the engine's speed is not rising, and a car under full power has its tyres right there (ours grip best at 0.12). A change up that is due and has not come within a tenth of a second is made by `vehicle_input` itself: the transmission is put in manual for that one change (drive cut for `shift_time`, clutch let in over 0.7 of it, throttle following the clutch), then handed back.
- **A change up needs the road to ask for it too**: the engine speed the road speed would give in this gear must be over 0.8 of the change-up speed. Spinning wheels alone take the engine there; changing then leaves the next gear at half the speed (the box went 1-2-1-2 through a wheelspin). So a car with its wheels spinning stays in gear on its limiter until it grips.
- **The torque curve** is made from `torque_peak` (physics_world.cpp, where the controller is set up).
- **Recordings**: `assets/sounds/engines/<kind>.txt` + loops, baked with engine-sim (`dev/engine-sim/assets/vibe_<kind>.mr` names the engine; pickup 650-5200 in 8 steps, saloon 800-6600 in 8, sports car 1000-8900 in 9). Every loop is levelled to the same loudness; `engine_loudness` is what makes one car quieter than another, and it also closes the low-pass of the driven car's near view.
- Test: `build/trshot/drive.sh <car 0..7> [top speed / 30 m/s] [frames]` drives a car along the highway by itself; with `VIBE_LOG_JEEP=1` every change of gear is a log line (from which engine speed, the most reached in that gear, road speed, pedal). `VIBE_TEST_JEEP_VIEW=<0..2>` sets the view.
- Set by numbers, not by ear.

**Tyre smoke stays out of the car** (`jeep_marks`, `jeep_fx`): a puff is a flat round picture turned to the eye that grows to a metre and a half; one at a rear wheel reached across the whole car, and seen from behind the seats stood in smoke. Now a puff is born with a push out to its wheel's side, its middle is kept out of the body's box (`JeepState::body_box`), and it is drawn no wider than its distance from that box plus 0.3 m. `VIBE_TEST_SMOKE_OLD=1` draws it as before. Not confirmed by a picture: the test drives made too little smoke to show a difference.

## The lane's dry-stone wall and the river's plants (2026-11-25, late)

- **The lane's edge is a dry-stone wall, streamed** (`vk_engine/lane_edge.h/.cpp`; it replaces the concrete parapet of `place_lane_rocks`, which is gone from `bridges.cpp`). Rule, from the ground as drawn: a station every 2 - 3 m of every road line but the coast highway; a wall on a side where the ground falls more than 1.5 m within 4 m beyond the verge (`kDrop`, `kWithin`), not on a bridge, not where another road joins, not round the car park (`no_wall` given by the caller), gaps of up to two stations closed, runs under four stations dropped. Stones are rounded blocks (superquadrics, 108 triangles), a function of (station, side, place in the course): a lower course, an upper course half a stone along, now and then a boulder, and a footing course on the side of the drop where the ground lies lower. Three tints of the triplanar rock texture (slot 7). The stations and flags are made once (`lane_edge::make`, called from `hw_objects_poll`); the stones are made in 120 m stretches by `highway_furniture.cpp` (ids `kLaneId + c` in `hw_chunks_`, loaded within 520 m, freed beyond 700 m, home's chart only). Collision: for cars and bolts turned boxes through `hw_rail_boxes` (`lane_edge::car_boxes`); for a walker upright boxes half a metre long in `lane_rock_cols_`. Measured at start-up: 1332 m of wall (the concrete one stood on 752 m, by its own rule). Hooks: `VIBE_LOG_LANE=1` (every run of wall with its end points, stones per stretch), `VIBE_NO_LANE_ROCKS=1`.
- **The river's plants are one rule** (`engine/river_plants.h`): stations 7 m apart along the stream's real middle; per side the bank is found, then lily rafts on the shallows (mostly on the inside of a bend, never in the middle half of the stream, never under a bridge, none below the coast road's bridge), reed beds at the waterline (a second stand in the water inside a bend), and up the bank ferns, long grass and drifts of buttercups, nettles, foxgloves, thistles. `scatter.cpp` asks it for the stations inside the home tile at start-up (1647 lily rafts, 3336 reeds, 4684 bank plants; before: 1297 / 972 / 2540). **Beyond the home tile** `vk_engine/river_stream.cpp` asks for the rest in 70 m stretches on a background thread (2 - 9 ms each) within 260 m of the camera, writes them into one of 12 slots of a 786 KB instance buffer, draws them with the small plants' pipeline and meshes on the CPU-cell path (`world_render.cpp`, after the species loop) and gives them back beyond 340 m. They cast no shadow of their own and get no long grass (species 15). Hooks: `VIBE_LOG_RIVER=1`, `VIBE_NO_RIVER_PLANTS=1` (also the home tile's).
- **River-bank scrub** (`world_props.cpp river_shrub_density`, home tile and streamed cells alike): the gaps between thickets narrower, more of it near the castle.
- **Not measured:** the `w_scatter` pass before / after (other sessions were using the GPU: the same view read 0.7 to 18 ms). Not checked: driving a car into the wall, walking against it, night.

## People in the pavilion and on the launch pad; the walk that read as the walking dead (2026-11-26)

- **Why some people walked like the dead** (`vk_engine/npc_world.cpp` "WHAT IT PLAYS", `anim/character_animator.cpp` `action`). Four things, each seen in a still from the side: (1) two of the eight ways of walking were just that - `walk_tired`, a shuffle at 0.57 m/s with the body hanging, and `walk_swagger`, the arms held wide of the body: a quarter of everybody. Nobody walks them any more (`look % 8` 2 is `walk_relaxed`, 6 the plain walk; `npc::kPace[2]` 0.82); the clips are still in the set. (2) Every hand on a walking clip was the rest pose's, flat with the fingers straight and spread: the clips come from a body without fingers. `action()` now curls the fingers of a hand that holds nothing. (3) The plain walk leaned BACKWARD with the chin up: the "upright" correction (`VIBE_NPC_UPRIGHT`) was 0.42 at a walk - 7 degrees of back, 12 of head; it is 0.26 now, and what levels the head of somebody who stands is unchanged. (4) A third of the time the head of the `look_around` clip was laid over a walking body - a head turning on its own clock; walking, the head is now the walk's own, with the look layer's glances. Also: a walking clip is only played once the person is at half its pace (the first and last steps are the plain walk's, which has starts and stops), not slowed to 0.4 of its rate.
- **More sites than one** (`npc::World::add_site`, `npc.cpp` "THE ADDED SITES"). The NPC world had one site (the service station). A site can now be ADDED: its nodes, seats and spots join the same lists (zone 1, 2, ...), a link may name a node of a site read before. A seat or a spot can name a point after its yaw - what the hands or the eyes go to there - which reaches the renderer as `View::aim` (it was only used while sitting down; no new bytes on the wire). **Their people are only there while a player is near**: `set_eyes` (every player's place, each frame, from `npc_tick`) wakes a site when somebody comes within 430 m of its edge - everybody at the place he begins on - and puts it to sleep at 560 m (hidden, not stepped, places freed). They are made last in `populate`, with a generator of their own, so the station's life and everybody's number there are what they were (the unit test checks it). `kMaxAgents` 100 -> 160; **protocol 10** (the same 27 bytes an agent, more of them; new values of `style`).
- **The pavilion** (`assets/office/office.nav`, written by `nav()` in `tools/office/make_office.py` from the model's own chairs and keyboards: 45 seats - 31 at desks, 8 round the meeting table, 6 on sofas - 25 spots, 48 nodes; the task chairs stand nearer their desks than they did). 22 people: 17 with a desk of their own (role `Desk`: at it two thirds of the time; else beside a colleague who sits, pointing at his screen; at the kitchen island; at the glass looking at the pad; at the wall of screens; on a sofa), 3 round the meeting table, 2 who are on the way most of the time (`Floater`: the rail of the mezzanine up the stair, the terrace, the same places). What they play: docs/animation/actions.md "Desk work, pointing, talking".
- **The pad** (`assets/rocket/site.nav`, written by hand from the colliders in `place_launch_site`: 54 spots, 29 nodes, joined to the pavilion's steps). 37 people: 18 in six groups of three that talk (`Visitor`), 8 who look up at the rocket, 4 on the apron's edge, 7 technicians (`Crew`) who go from one thing to see to - a tank, the pump house, the tower's foot, the mount, the control house's door - to the next and are busy there a while. Everybody moves on after a minute or two.
- **Cost, measured** (`VIBE_LOG_NPC`: "[npc] cost", the mean of 300 frames, standing on the apron): the world's steps 0.10 ms a frame with 142 agents; the bodies' poses 0.21 - 0.23 ms a frame with 40 bodies posed (it was 28; the frame can skin 48). One window of 300 frames read 2.2 ms for the poses while other sessions' games ran: not explained, not seen again.
- Hooks: `VIBE_NO_CROWD=1` (no added sites: the world as it was), `VIBE_NPC_CAM=desk|point|group|watch|tech`, `VIBE_NPC_DESK=<0..3>`, and `VIBE_LOG_NPC` says per person at a desk what he does and where the keyboard is from his hips. Tests: `tests/test_npc.cpp` "an added site's people are there only while a player is near" (the first site unchanged step for step, desks with their keyboards in reach, twenty minutes sane, the wire, asleep and awake again, somebody shot replaced).
- **Seen**, one still each: the hall with its desks taken, hands on the keyboards (and once more after 80 s of their life: somebody walking the aisle, somebody at the glass); a group of three on the apron, and one of them with the pointing arm out (`VIBE_NPC_GESTURE=6`); the apron from its north-west corner; the plain walk from the side in mid-stride - upright, the head level, the arms close to the body (their swing is small in that still). **Not checked**: any of it in motion (how the typing, the pointing, the talking and the walk MOVE is for whoever plays it); two machines (the larger message, a client's view); night; the meeting room, the mezzanine and the stair up close; whether a walker's line through the pavilion clips furniture; a player's shot at somebody at a desk. Known: everybody is the one soldier model; sitting down on a task chair goes sideways through its armrest; chairs do not turn.

## Cars strike cars; traffic that follows and overtakes; the military police (2026-11-25, night)

- **Cars are solid to each other** (`jeep.cpp` "CARS STRIKE CARS", `cars_collide`, called from `jeep_tick` before the physics). One car is simulated on a machine; every other car near it - players' cars that stand, cars other players drive, the NPC traffic - is given to the physics as a body that is moved, not simulated (`PhysicsWorld::vehicle_set_cars`: kinematic, the car's own collision boxes, moved to where its owner says it is so that it has a speed; the simulated car's wheels do not stand on them). In multiplayer each machine does this for its own car against the others' told places. **What is struck moves, and its owner moves it**: a players' car nobody simulates slides and turns on the ground, less and less (6 m/s2, stops at a wall or a tree) - on the machine that left it there, or, if it was nobody's, on the machine whose car struck it, which takes it over and tells the others (`jeep_net_send` sends such a car 30 times a second while it slides; `CarTrafficState::Shove::claim` keeps an old state from another machine from undoing it). An NPC car is the NPC engine's: `npc::World::push_car` (what goes along the car changes its speed, the rest slides it; after a hard blow its driver stands 2 - 7 s) - called by the host, asked for by a client with `Event::NpcCarPush`. The blow: the two outlines on the ground plan, their gap and closing speed; the struck car leaves with the striker's share of the closing speed by weight. Leaning on a standing car under power pushes it slowly. State: `vk_engine/state/car_traffic.h`. **Protocol 9** (the event, and the NPC wire format 5). Hooks: `VIBE_LOG_CARS=1`, `VIBE_NO_CAR_COLLIDE=1`, `VIBE_TEST_CAR_CRASH=<frame>[,<metres>]` (another car is stood in the simulated car's way; with `VIBE_TEST_JEEP=1,0`).
- **The traffic** (`npc.cpp` "THE TRAFFIC"). Every driver is somebody (`make_driver`): slow (0.60 - 0.74 of the common 21 m/s, pickups), normal (0.86 - 1.08), fast (1.14 - 1.34), and a speeder (1.75 - 2.0: one always, more by chance 3.5 %); each has his own acceleration, bend speed, time gap and patience. Each step everything on the road goes in one list (`build_things`: this world's cars and the players' cars from `set_obstacles`, each with metres along the road, metres right of the centre line, speed along it). From it a car **follows** (never nearer than its time gap, never faster than it can brake from; 8 m short of what stands), slows **for a bend ahead** in good time, **passes** (`can_pass`: up to three cars nose to tail; the time at its passing speed plus 3 s; nothing coming within that time plus 3.5 s; nobody beside or coming up behind; the road straight over the distance; not by the station's ways in and out; round something that stands only as far out as needed) and **gives up** when somebody comes after all (back in behind, or on if all but past, or braking until there is room). Whoever meets a car in his lane brakes and keeps right. The same seed and steps give the same world; clients see the replicated places. `View::style` of a car: `kCarPolice`, `kCarSiren`, `kCarOvertaking`. Tests: `tests/test_npc.cpp` `[traffic]` (following, a car left on the lane, going round it, overtaking after oncoming traffic, somebody appearing mid-pass, half an hour of mixed traffic without two cars within 2.6 m, making way for a siren, a struck car). `World::add_car`, `populate(seed, traffic)`, `traffic()`, `set_sirens()` are for tests and logs.
- **The military police** (`npc.cpp` class `Police`: two of the traffic's cars; `jeep.cpp` "THE MILITARY POLICE"). Olive-drab saloons with a light bar (three boxes made in `police_init`, placed on the roof of the model lent to a police car by `police_place`). They patrol at the common pace; the siren goes on for 45 - 100 s every 1 - 3 minutes: then they want 37 m/s, the lamps flash blue and red in turn (emissive, and a glow in `jeep_fx`), the others go to the road's edge and slow down, and the police car goes down the middle if they have. **The siren's sound is made in code**: `engine/siren_synth.h` (16 s that loop: three wails 650 - 1500 Hz and four seconds of yelp), written to `assets/sounds/siren_wail.wav` the first time it is needed and not there, played by `siren_audio_tick` (`jeep.cpp`) as a source of the spatialiser (`AudioEngine::source_start / source_update / source_stop`, shape `sound_space::kSirenShape`) - one a police car whose siren is on, by its NPC agent, drawn or not: see "Sound in space", "The siren". A police car that is taken by a player keeps neither its paint nor its bar. Hooks: `VIBE_LOG_TRAFFIC=1`, `VIBE_TEST_SIREN=1`, `VIBE_NO_SIREN=1`, `VIBE_SIREN_GAIN=<x>`, `VIBE_NPC_CAM=police` / `passing`, `VIBE_NPC_NO_OBSTACLES=1`.
- **Checked:** the unit tests; one run each of a crash (`[cars] ... struck ... at 13.0 m/s ... goes off at 6.2 m/s`), a police car with its bar and siren voice, and 20 minutes of traffic on the coast road (64 overtakes, one given up). **Not checked:** anything on two machines (the event, taking over a struck car, a client's view of a pass), cars at the station meeting a player's car, night, how the siren sounds to the ear.

## Regions: custom ground and content on the procedural planet (2026-11-26)

The planet is a formula everywhere. Hand-made work used to be possible in two forms only: home's 2 km sculpted tile, and a site's ground "by rule" (a level top). A **region** is the general form: stored data laid over the formula at ANY place on the planet, streamed in near the player and freed out of range. This section is the design; the code is `src/engine/sector.h` (addresses), `src/engine/region_tile.h` (height tiles: format, reading rule, store, the loaded set), `src/engine/vk_engine/regions.cpp` + `state/regions.h` (streaming, GPU, editing), `shaders/region_tiles.glsl` (the shader's reading). Tests: `tests/test_sector.cpp`, `tests/test_region_tile.cpp`.

### 1. Sectors: one address for a piece of the planet

Engine coordinates are a chart - small numbers that mean another piece of the planet every few kilometres ("Small coordinates everywhere"). Stored data, and later servers, need a name for a place that does not change with the chart. `globe.cpp` had none (charts wander with the player; home coordinates are singular on the far side), so there is one now: **the planet as a cube blown up to a sphere, each face a quadtree** (`engine/sector.h`).

- A sector is (face 0..5, level 0..28, i, j), packed in 64 bits (`sector::Id`), named in files and messages as `face-level-i-j` (`2-14-8191-8192`). Level 0 is a face (9,990 km across); each level halves the cell: level 8 = 39 km, 10 = 9.8 km, 14 = 610 m, 20 = 9.5 m, 28 = 3.7 cm.
- Face coordinates are equal-angle (cells the same width along a face's axes, at most 1.41 times smaller in area at its corners). Cells are not squares of round metres - nothing is on a sphere - so stored data is laid out in the cell's own 0..1 coordinates and mapped into the chart by the engine.
- Home is the middle of face 2 (the planet's top); its a axis is east, b is south. The nearest face edge is 5,000 km away.
- Pure functions, integers and doubles only (as exact 5,000 km out as at home): `cell_at(G, level)` / `point(id, fa, fb)` (planet unit vector <-> sector), `parent` / `child` / `ancestor` / `contains`, `neighbour` / `neighbours` (across face edges; 7 at a cube corner), `cover(G, radius, level)` (the sectors within reach of a point), `to_chart` / `from_chart`, `name` / `parse`.
- **For shaders**: the exact mapping goes through the planet's unit vector, which a float holds to half a metre. `sector::chart_map` gives chart position -> cell coordinates as a second-order series round an anchor, relative to a base cell so the numbers stay small; within 4 km it is the exact mapping to under 2 mm (tested at home, 5,000 km out and by a cube corner). The CPU uses the very same series, so both sides agree.

### 2. Height tiles

A region's ground is stored as **height tiles**: a DELTA over the formula (0 = pure formula), so the formula's own detail carries on under sculpted ground and everything outside blends by itself. Home's sculpt has always worked that way (`sampleHeightDelta`); a tile is the same idea, addressable anywhere.

- **One tile = one level-14 sector** (`kTileLevel`: 610 m at most, 430 m by a cube corner), **513 x 513 samples** (`kTileN`: 512 steps, 1.19 m a step at most - home's sculpt is 1 m), **16-bit**, in units of 1/64 m (1.6 cm steps, +-512 m; the unit is in the file). Why these: 1.2 m is the scale sculpting works at (what is finer comes from the formula and the materials); 513 puts the outer samples ON the sector's edges so neighbours share them; a tile is 526 KB - one disk read and one small upload - and a 3 x 3 block round the player is 4.7 MB.
- **Reading rule** (the same on both sides: `region::sample` on the CPU, `region_delta` in `region_tiles.glsl`): bilinear between the four samples round the point, read with four exact texel fetches (not the sampler's filter, which moves in 1/256 steps of a texel - the normal takes its slope over 2 cm), times the edge mask.
- **Edge mask**: a tile fades to zero over 1/16 of its side (38 m, smoothstep) toward every edge where the ground does not continue into a LOADED neighbour, and toward a corner point unless all four tiles round it are loaded and joined. So there is never a step at a tile's border - whatever was painted there, and also while a neighbour is still on its way from a server. Two tiles continue into each other when both say so (`joined` bits in the file, set by the tools when the shared edge samples agree) and both are loaded. The rule is worked out per loaded set (`ActiveSet::update_fades`), 8 bits a tile.
- **File** `h_<sector>.vht`: 32-byte header (magic `VHT1`, format version, samples a side, sector id, unit, joined bits, compression, payload size, FNV-1a checksum), then the samples raw. The full layout is at the top of `region_tile.h`. No compressor is linked into `vibe_core` (the project's only one is inside the EXR reader), so tiles are raw; the header has the field, and a file with an unknown version or compression is refused, not guessed at.
- **Index** `idx_<level-8 sector>.txt`: text, one line `tile <sector>` per height tile in that 39 km sector. It is what tells the engine that a place has stored ground at all - nothing is probed per cell. Other first words are skipped, so other kinds of region content can be listed in the same file later.
- **Where**: `assets/regions/` (shipped), then `cache/regions/` (fetched, or made in the editor; a cache tile stands in for a shipped one of the same sector). **A server client plugs in without the engine changing**: `region::Store::request(file)` is called when an index, or a tile an index lists, is wanted and in neither folder; it has only to put the file into `cache/regions/`. The engine looks again every 3 s; until then the land is the formula's.

### 3. Streaming and the GPU

- **Budget: 32 slots** (`kSlots`) - one R16_SNORM array texture of 32 layers of 513 x 513 (17 MB on the GPU, the same again on the CPU when all are in use). **Load within 1,800 m** of the camera (distance to the tile's bounding circle), **free beyond 2,400 m**. More tiles in reach than slots: the nearest win - a farther loaded tile is put out for a nearer one - and it is logged (`[regions] budget`).
- **Off the frame's critical path**: a tile's file is read and checked on a worker thread, one at a time; the upload is one 526 KB copy recorded at the start of a frame; the tile joins the loaded set two frames later (when no frame in flight can read the layer half-written).
- **How a shader finds the tile under a point in constant time** (`shaders/region_tiles.glsl`, scene set bindings 49 = a small uniform block, 50 = the array texture; nothing was added to the scene block): a bounding circle of everything loaded (most ground is outside it: one test); then the series of section 1 turns the chart position into cell coordinates within a **16 x 16 window of sectors round the camera**; one byte per window cell says which slot, if any; the fraction within the cell is the tile coordinate. No loop over tiles. A second window serves another cube face when one is within reach. The window is placed again when the camera has moved a few cells, or the chart changed.
- **Limits**: tiles are streamed at one level (14). The address and the file carry the level, so a coarser level for the distance (a 2.4 km tile at 4.8 m a sample) can be added with the rule "the finest loaded tile at a point wins" - not built. The ground may not rise above the terrain shader's ceiling (`terrainMaxHeight`: 200 m over the datum). Beyond the 1,500 m the ground is drawn to, the far march shows a tile only while it is loaded (2.4 km).

### 4. One ground function, both sides

- **GPU**: `sampleHeightDelta` (`terrain_rm_height_tex.glsl`) returns home's delta plus `region_delta`; every height the terrain shader makes already ends with it (`terrainM*`, `terrainB_slope`, so: the patch vertex shader, the march, the far march, the shadow march, the baked ground texture).
- **CPU**: `world_ground_height` (`terrain.cpp`) adds `region::ground_delta(x, z)`, and `sample_terrain_height` adds it over home's heightmap. It reads the loaded set, published as one unchanging object (`region::set_active`), from any thread; with nothing loaded it costs one load of a number.
- **What is told when a tile arrives, leaves or is edited** (`regions_ground_changed` in `regions.cpp` - a new thing that stands on the ground hooks in there): the patch terrain's kept node bounds under the tile (`tp_->bounds`), the baked ground texture (drawn again over the tile's box: `terrain_bake_redraw`), the near-field ground bake the grass stands on (`wv_nf_invalidate`), the streamed woods' cells under the tile (made again, the old trees staying until the new ones are there: `wv_ground_changed`), and - when the tile overlaps the home tile - home's full-height texture (the grass and the castle road read it), physics heightfield, ray-tracing mesh and its fixed trees (`regions_refresh_home`: each tree is lifted by what the tiles add where it stands). That last part is the end-of-stroke work of home's own brush - a wait for the GPU and a rebuild of the whole 2 km: **0.8 - 1.2 s, once, when a tile over home arrives, leaves or a stroke on it ends**; a tile anywhere else costs nothing of the kind. Not moved under a tile over home: home's small plants and loose stones (`scatter`), its rocks, the castle lane and the road (they keep their own heights), the baked sun shadow and the curvature bake. Away from home there is no physics ground at all yet (cars and people take the ground from the CPU function, which has the tiles).
- **Home is not a region yet.** Home's sculpt stays as it is: a 2048 m float heightmap at 1 m in home coordinates, baked whole (formula + plateau + river + road cut), from which a dozen systems are built at start-up (the delta and full-height textures, the physics heightfield, the ray-tracing mesh, the CLOD pyramid, the baked sun shadow, the curvature bake, the grass mask, the fixed planting). Turning that into tiles means resampling it onto sector cells (which are not aligned with home's grid: home sits on the corner of four cells) and rebuilding each of those systems from tiles - with the castle's look depending on every centimetre of it. Not safe in one round; a region tile may overlap home (it adds to home's ground).

### 5. Authoring

- Menu > Terrain Edit > "Region tiles": *New tile here* (an empty tile for the sector under the camera, in `cache/regions/`), *Edit region tiles* (the brush - raise, lower, smooth, flatten - then works on region tiles anywhere, found by a march over the CPU's ground; saved at the end of each stroke), *Stamp mesa / crater here*.
- By code: `region::stamp(store, G, shape, radius, height)` adds a mesa or a crater-with-rim to every tile it reaches (made if not there), joins them and saves them - the test generator, and the shape of what an importer would do.
- Hooks: `VIBE_REGION_TEST=<mesa|crater>,<east m>,<south m>[,<radius>,<height>]` stamps one at start-up (home coordinates); `VIBE_REGION_NEW=1` makes an empty tile under the camera at start-up; `VIBE_LOG_REGIONS=1` logs every load, slot, free and the frame's cost; `VIBE_NO_REGIONS=1` turns the system off; `VIBE_REGION_DIR=<folder>` is used instead of `cache/regions/` (test runs: nothing is left in the game's own cache). The test shape is not stamped a second time where stored ground already lies.

### 6. Content in regions, and sites

A site's content still has one slot (`sites.cpp`). Lifting that is deep, not a constant: every site has a chart OF ITS OWN and its content is built at fixed engine coordinates in it ((0, 12288): "Sites"), drawn only while the engine is in that chart (`site_here()`); the scene block carries one road and one site's ground; the shader and the far field know the nearest site only. Two sites' content at once needs content placed by sector in whatever chart the engine is in (as the roadside objects and the woods are), i.e. sites rebuilt on this section's addressing. Left as is. **A site and height tiles coexist**: the tile's delta is added after the site's ground rule on both sides, in any chart.

### 7. How per-sector servers would use this (nothing is built; for the session that does)

- **The unit of ownership is a sector of one fixed level** (say level 8, 39 km, or level 10, 9.8 km - a constant of the protocol). `sector::cell_at(G, level)` of a player's planet point is "which server". A server's world state is keyed by sector id; a coarser sector's owner can be the parent of finer ones (`ancestor`), so a quiet continent is one process and a busy town is split.
- **Interest = the sectors within reach**: `sector::cover(G, radius, level)` is the set a client subscribes to and a server replicates from; it crosses face edges and cube corners by itself.
- **Hand-over at a border**: positions on the wire must be chart-independent - a sector id plus the 0..1 position in it (`cell_at(..., &frac)` / `point(id, fa, fb)`), or the planet unit vector in doubles; never engine coordinates (today's LAN protocol sends home-chart floats: "Multiplayer (LAN)"). A player whose `cell_at` changes owner is handed to the neighbour (`neighbour` / `neighbours` name it); with hysteresis (hand over a few hundred metres past the border) so a walk along it does not ping-pong. During hand-over both servers have the player in their interest set, because `cover` of a point near a border holds both sectors.
- **Data follows the same keys**: a server serves `idx_<its sector>.txt` and the `h_*.vht` files it lists (`region::Store::request` asks for exactly those names), and would list its sites and other content in the same index.
- **What a server session should NOT take from this round**: tiles of several levels, more than one site's content at once, and physics ground away from home are not there; positions in today's network protocol are engine floats.
- **Read first**: `engine/sector.h`, `engine/region_tile.h` (top comments), `engine/globe.h`, this section, "Sites", "Small coordinates everywhere" (rule 2: nothing may assume engine coordinates are a place), `net/net_state.h`.

### 8. Checked, measured, not checked (2026-11-26)

- **Unit tests** (15 cases, run in under 2 s): sector ids and names; point <-> sector round trips at levels 0..28 at home, 5,022 km from home, by a face edge, by a cube corner and on the far side (under 0.01 mm); the hierarchy; neighbours mutual everywhere, across a face edge, 7 at a cube corner; `cover` holds every point within reach; chart <-> sector 5,000 km out; the shader's series against the exact mapping (under 2 mm within 4 km, four places); the bilinear rule; the edge mask (0 on a fading edge, no step); the file (round trip, damage, unknown version or compression refused); the store and its index; a mesa over the corner of four tiles is whole, and with any one of the four not loaded there is still no step anywhere (walked in 5 cm steps along and across every border); the CPU's series, a float copy of the shader's code fed from the very table the shader gets, and the exact mapping agree (5 mm / 2 cm) at the castle, outside the home tile and 5,000 km away; `ground_delta` through the published set, also across a change of chart.
- **In the game** (RTX 4080, the 1280 x 720 test window): a mesa 3.5 km west of home, outside the home tile (two tiles, the shape whole across their border, trees on its flanks, snow on its top where it passes the snow line); a crater with rim 250 m south of the castle, inside the home tile (trees lifted with the rim); the camera stood on a mesa in the home woods by `VIBE_TEST_GROUND` stands on the drawn ground with the trees round it at ground level. A flight past the tiles at 500 m/s logs read (0.9 - 1.9 ms on the worker), slot, joining, and freeing beyond 2.4 km.
- **Frame time**: `regions_update` stayed under 0.5 ms on every frame of that flight, the frames a tile joined included (the 526 KB upload is one copy). Two such flights showed 33 and 4 hitch lines, the same flight with the system off 29: no spike that follows a tile; the differences are other programs on the machine. GPU passes with the tile in view: sum 4.9 ms, `terrain_bake` 1.7 ms while it redraws.
- **The castle**: the same view with a tile loaded 250 m away and with `VIBE_NO_REGIONS=1` differs by 3.07 grey levels of 255 on average; two runs with the system off differ by 2.93. (Both from this build: there was no build from before the change to compare with.)
- **Not checked**: the brush on region tiles and the menu's buttons (written, built, never used by hand - the hook `VIBE_REGION_NEW=1` makes and loads an empty tile); more tiles in reach than slots (the budget rule is code and a log line); a second cube face's window in the game (5,000 km away; the unit tests cover the mapping); a tile at a site; a tile fetched late by a server client; Vulkan validation; the AMD test PC.

## Rock formations: a kit made in Blender, stood by rule, pasted into the ground (2026-11-26)

**What it is.** Large rock pieces with real gaps, as static meshes that carry the landscape's own rock - the reference game's way for arches, overhangs and cliffs (its ground is a height field; what a height field cannot be is a mesh on top, blended into it). Files: `tools/rocks/make_formations.py` (the generator), `assets/rocks/formations.rockf` (the kit, 29 MB), `vk_engine/rock_formations.h/.cpp` (the kit's file, the placement rule, collision boxes - no Vulkan), `vk_engine/rock_formations_gpu.cpp` + `state/rock_formations.h` (levels of detail, drawing, colliders), `shaders/rock_form.glsl` (the material; used by `cube_main_surfaces.glsl` and `ssdm_height.frag`).

- **The kit** (14 pieces, each at 3 levels of detail). Formations: arch (21 x 8 x 12 m, an opening 7 m wide to walk or drive under), cleft wall (split by a crack one can see through), overhang, stack of slabs, leaning pillars (five and a fallen one), boulder pile (holes between the boulders), cliff buttress (22 x 15 x 32 m), low outcrop (5 x 5 x 2.4 m); level 0 is 14 - 44 thousand triangles, level 1 3.5 - 9 thousand, level 2 0.8 - 2 thousand. The cliff-face family: `face_a / face_b / face_c / face_cave` (30 m wide, 45 m high at size 1; buttresses and gullies between upright joints, faint beds with flint lines, scars, a wave notch, a rubble ramp with fallen blocks, the turf's lip; b has a fresh scar, c a ledge and a flake, cave two hollows at its foot), `headland` (matches at its left end, turns the corner at its right), `seastack`; 60 / 12 / 2.5 thousand triangles (stack 30 / 6 / 1.4). **The faces' ends match**: every variant has the same block-out at its ends and its relief goes over, within 4.5 m of an end, into one relief shared by all - pieces of one size at one height, 96 % of a width apart, meet without a step (they are closed solids laid a little into each other, not welded).
- **The generator**: `"C:\Program Files\Blender Foundation\Blender 5.3\blender.exe" -b --python tools/rocks/make_formations.py` (6 minutes; `-- --only a,b`, `--quick`, `--preview DIR` renders each piece). Block-out boxes and chopped lumps -> voxel remesh -> bent by noise -> boolean cuts (the opening, the cleft, hollows) -> voxel remesh at 3 - 6 million triangles -> geology by numpy on the vertices (beds of uneven thickness and hardness, two joint sets, blocks in and out of line and fallen out, weathered tops; then lumps, laminations, hair cracks and pits along the normal) -> edge-collapse to the three levels -> smooth normals split at 42 degrees -> per vertex ambient occlusion (20 rays against the level-0 mesh) and curvature, stored in the vertex uv -> a grid of solid cells (0.5 - 1.4 m) for collision. Deterministic (seeds per piece, no random operators). **No normal-map bake**: 14 pieces would need 14 more texture slots and an unwrap of shapes with holes; relief finer than the mesh is the tiling material's. The file is the generator's own (`RKF1`: indexed, binary) - the text `.mesh` format has no index and no levels.
- **The material** (`rock_form.glsl`). The landscape's rock scan (slot 7; colour, normal, height in the normal's alpha - it has a height, none was generated) laid on by world position exactly as `terrain_rm_main_ground.glsl` lays it on a steep face: the same three planes, 0.30 repeats a metre, the tile growing four times per distance band with two bands laid over each other, the same shift per band; within a plane the repeats are broken as the ground's are (`rm_at`: cells of two tiles turned by quarter turns, bent borders, blended) - `rf_plane`. Colour: the cliff's own tone, beds and patches (same formulas), then from the mesh: dark and damp by the baked occlusion, pale worn edges and dirty creases by curvature, dust / lichen / moss on what faces up, the ground's snow line (70 - 90 m, by aspect); winter snow comes from the lighting pass as for every mesh. **Chalk** (normal index -10, set per formation): white to cream, rust and grey streaks, algae from the foot up, turf on the top and on ledges; its beds and joints are worked out in the shader (`rf_chalk_h`: beds 0.55 m, blocks 1.1 m, flint bands) - drawn as lines, as a bent normal and as the displacement's height.
- **Displacement**: the pieces go through the screen-space displacement pass as mesh parts (`LevelMesh::ssdm` 7.5 cm, chalk 10 cm). `ssdm_height.frag` takes the height from `rf_material` (what stands above a third of the scan's height; for chalk its blocks) - the same layout as the colour.
- **The blend into the ground** (`cube_main_surfaces.glsl`, the rock-formation branch): the reference game's paste by height. Within a zone above the ground the ground's weight falls from 1 to 0; of rock and ground the one whose weight x height is higher shows (`rf_height_blend` = `rm_height_blend`, band 0.15). The zone is 22 cm close by (the game's 10 cm plus what the ground's height can be off by) growing to 3 m from 500 m on. The ground's height: the home tile's height map; beyond the tile the ground baked round the camera for the world's grass (slot 22), else the piece's own ground line. The ground's material: on steep ground the same rock, elsewhere the soil scan the terrain lays there (slot 9, 0.45 repeats a metre) in an earth tone, greener where the grass mask has grass. Above the zone a stain of earth and the contact's shade. **The baked ground texture (bindings 46 - 48) is not used**: it is addressed by distance levels whose state reaches only the terrain pass, and it is empty on steep faces - where these pieces stand.
- **Placement** (`rock_formations.cpp`, once, from the ground and the roads; hashes of place, the same on every PC): along every narrow road a station every 5 m per side - a *cut* (ground 1.4 m over the road 5 m beyond the verge and 3 m at 12 m) is dressed with a small buttress, cleft wall, overhang, stack or outcrop, front 1.6 m beyond the verge; the *foot of a bank* gets a pile, outcrop or stack (it may stand in the river's edge); elsewhere now and then an outcrop or pile; two *gates* beside the road (arch, its passage along the road; pillars). *Cliffs*: the home tile's slope summed over 96 m cells, the three steepest cells 240 m apart (not the castle's rock): a *crag band* of three face pieces along the contour at the steepest point, buttresses at the foot sunk to over half their depth, overhangs / cleft walls / stacks / outcrops on the slope, piles at its foot. *The sea cliff*: the open shore between x 900 .. 3700 and z -2600 .. 3400 is searched for the stretch where the land stands highest 30 m in from the waterline; the waterline is followed from there, a piece at a time (at most 18), white faces with their feet in the water, a headland at one end, two stacks 45 - 75 m out. Everything keeps off roads (1.3 m beyond a verge; a cliff run 30 m), bridges, the car park, the station, the launch site, buildings, the castle, water. Single pieces are let into the ground under their lowest point; the pieces of a run share one height.
- **Streaming** (`rockf_poll`, once a frame): each formation gets a level by distance - 0 within 55 m, 1 within 170 m, 2 within 900 m, none beyond (x up to 3 for tall pieces, never past 1380 m) with hysteresis; a piece's level goes onto the GPU (mesh + BLAS) when first wanted, one a frame, and is given back after 900 frames unused. What is drawn is a list of mesh parts sharing the kit's buffers with the formation's transform (`mesh_parts_now`): the static mesh path draws, shadows and ray-traces them. Collision is the rock's own surface (2026-11-26; the solid-cell boxes before it made a staircase of every slope and walls in every gap): the triangles of level 1 (`rockf::collision_triangles`, `kCollisionLevel`). The physics world builds one `MeshShape` per piece on a worker thread (`PhysicsWorld::mesh_pieces_build_async`) and stands a static body per formation within 70 m of the player (`add_mesh_instance`, scaled and turned; friction 0.8) - the car's body and wheels, crates and debris touch the rock itself. The walker gets the world-space triangles of the formations within 14 m (`rockf::collision_mesh`, a 2 m grid; `rockf_walk_meshes`) and is resolved against them after the terrain clamp in `game_loop.cpp` (`game/tri_collision.h`: a capsule pushed out of the triangles; a face with normal.y >= 0.55 is ground and lifts straight up, a steeper one is a wall slid down, an overhang a ceiling; a sticky floor snap of 0.45 m going down; unit tests `tests/test_tri_collision.cpp`). Home's chart only.
- **Hooks**: `VIBE_LOG_ROCKS=1` (every formation with place and size, the shore's steepness, what is drawn), `VIBE_TEST_ROCKS=x,z` (the kit in a row along the hillside from there), `VIBE_TEST_ROCK_DROP=1` (the player dropped onto the nearest formation once: the per-second position log shows where they come to rest), `VIBE_NO_ROCK_FORMATIONS=1`, `VIBE_NO_LANE_FORMATIONS=1`, `VIBE_NO_CLIFF_FORMATIONS=1`, `VIBE_NO_CRAG_BANDS=1`, `VIBE_NO_SEA_CLIFF=1`.
- **Measured** (1280 x 720 test window, other programs on the GPU): 110 formations placed (40 cuts, 10 feet of banks, 9 roadside, 2 gates, 26 on cliffs, 3 in bands, 20 of the sea cliff). On the lane near the car park 69 formations and 291,600 triangles were in the draw list; `w_castle` 0.09 ms and `w_ssdm` 0.10 ms appeared and `deferred_lighting` went from 0.22 to 0.36 ms; the frame's sum 5.28 against 5.03 - 5.45 without (within the noise). The shore: 182 points, the land 30 m in stands 12 m over the sea on average, 24 m on the steepest stretch (round 1658, -1844) - the sea cliff there is 24 m high and 280 m long.
- **Limits / not done**: the terrain is not carved - the coast and the inland "cliffs" are slopes of about 40 degrees, so a sea cliff has lower ground behind its top and a full-size buttress stands out of the slope as a tower (the regions work can carve the ground later; the pieces and the rule are ready for it). Trees and bushes are not cleared where a formation stands (they grow through the faces). The terrain does not know the meshes: no occlusion or blend on the ground's side. Bolts and bullets do not hit them (they are not in the projectiles' ray test). No formations beyond home's chart or the home valley's roads. The face pieces' ends match in shape, not vertex for vertex. Not checked: walking under the arch, driving against a piece, night, winter, the levels changing in motion, the contact blend close up on level ground (seen only on a steep bank), chalk in direct sun (the look was taken with the face in shade).

## The ground and the objects on it: paste, far colour, decals, debris (2026-11-26)

Four techniques of the reference game's ground (read from its compiled terrain-blend, scene-capture, deferred-decal and automatic-placement shaders; notes outside the repo in `dev/cd-shaders/ground`, `roads`). One block on the GPU for the first three: `vk_engine/ground_fx.cpp`, state `vk_engine/state/ground_fx.h` (`GroundFxGpuHead`), `shaders/ground_fx.glsl` (keep the two layouts the same) - scene set binding 60 (the block) and 61 (the capture), deferred lighting set binding 30. `ground_fx_frame` runs once a frame after the baked ground texture (`frame.cpp`, GPU mark `ground_fx`). `VIBE_LOG_GROUND_FX=1` logs the capture's draws and the placed decals.
- **Paste into the ground** (`shaders/ground_paste.glsl`, called in `cube.frag` after `cube_main_surfaces.glsl`; setting `ground_paste`, `VIBE_NO_GROUND_PASTE=1`). The reference's paste pass: within a zone above the ground (`gfx.paste.w` = 0.22 m near, + 2.9 m over 500 - 1000 m: `1 - h / (zone + 2.9 saturate((dist - 500) 0.002))`) the ground's weight falls from 1 to 0, and of mesh and ground the one whose weight x height stands higher shows (band 0.15: `gp_height_blend`, the same as `rm_height_blend` / `rf_height_blend`) - soil in the joints and clefts, the stones standing clear; above the zone an earth stain (to ~0.75 m) and a damp band (to ~0.4 m) creep up the foot, ragged; at the foot the contact's shade. The ground's height: the home tile's height map, beyond it the near-field bake (`u_normal[22]`), else the caller's own ground line (a rock piece's). The ground's material: the **baked ground texture** where it is readable at that distance (`gp_bake`: the same three pictures and the same level choice as `rm_bake_fetch`; `gfx.paste.y/z` = t0 and the first level, 0 when not whole), else the soil scan (slot 9) / grass-mask green / a rock tone on steep ground. The rock formations' own block moved into it (they set `g_gp_h`, `g_gp_self_rock`, `g_gp_rock_above` and are pasted even with the switch off); the vegetation's trunks and boulders use it for their soil (their moss stays). **Left clean** (by material flags, no per-mesh table): the ground itself, leaves and far tree cards, people (normal index -8), army paint (-7), any draw with `tex_params.w = -1` (the cars - `castle_mesh.cpp`, the parts in `jeep_draw_` - and the weapon in the hand, `props.cpp draw_viewmodel`), glass (not drawn by `cube.frag`). Of a building (anything not rock): only faces that are not up-facing (`N.y < 0.7`: floors, steps, forecourts stay clean), and nothing indoors (one ray straight up, 4 m, mask 0x02, only for pixels within reach of the ground; a roof counts as a room only if a second ray straight out from the face also meets a wall within 8 m - so a bridge abutment under its deck, which looks out over open ground, is still pasted). The ground's share also sets the roughness (0.92).
- **Far ground carries the colour of placed objects** ("large scale detail"; `gfx_lsd_capture`, `shaders/lsd_capture.vert/.frag`, `shaders/ground_lsd.glsl` applied in `terrain_rm_main_light.glsl` part 2; setting `ground_lsd`, `VIBE_NO_GROUND_LSD=1`, `VIBE_TEST_LSD=1` paints what the capture covers magenta at every distance). The parts in `mesh_parts_now()` (castle, sites, highway furniture, rock formations - not the cars, glass or what glows) drawn straight down with an orthographic matrix into 1024 x 1024 texels of 4 m round the camera (RGBA8: sqrt of the material's mean colour - its texture's last mip x its tint, the rock's mean tone -, coverage; D32, the highest surface wins). Addressed by world position (repeating sampler: a texel's place is its world index modulo 1024, as the baked ground texture's), the window moves in steps of 32 texels: only the strips that come in are drawn (split at the picture's wrap), the whole window when the set of parts changes (a site or a highway chunk or formations stream in or out, a re-centre - at most once a second) or the camera jumps. Applied **per pixel beyond the bake** (the bake ends at 64 x t0, ~200 m; nothing is put into the bake): the ground's albedo takes it from 350 m (nothing nearer - the meshes are drawn there) to all of it by 900 m, faded at the window's edge. Tree crowns are NOT in it: the ground's own canopy colour beyond 1.2 km (`rm_forest` / the vegetation map) covers the woods.
- **Deferred decals** (`gfx_decals_frame`, `shaders/ground_decals.glsl` in `deferred_lighting.frag` right after the G-buffer reads; setting `ground_decals`, `VIBE_NO_GROUND_DECALS=1`). What Vibe had for marks: the gun's burn marks (`combat.cpp` `decals_`, max `kMaxDecals`, a box per mark drawn over the LIT picture in the FX pass by `fx_decal.frag`, with a glow while fresh), footprints (a list of prints in the scene block read by the ground's shader), the tracks in snow / sand (`post_fx.cpp`), craters (height + `craters.cpp`); no blood, no tyre marks other than the trail. Now: box decals (centre, three half axes - `ay` the projection axis -, kind, strength, seed, height-blending ratio) projected onto the G-buffer - colour, normal, roughness - with the reference's **height-aware blend** `a *= 1 - saturate(30 h'^2 (1 - ratio))`, h' = the surface height above 0.4 (the ground's material height from G-buffer 1 blue, a mesh's from its colour's brightness) so stones poke through. Looks generated in the shader (no textures): moss (on a wall: a closed band at the foot - there the height-aware blend covers the stones too - and patches climbing the joints above), damp (a dark zone over the band, streaks running up out of it, soaking the stones as well), dirt, oil, leaf litter, cracks, mud, scorch, bullet hole; `+16` = takes any face. On the raymarched ground (material 8, lit already) they tint the lit colour. Culling: the CPU keeps the visible ones (frustum, 160 m; 256 at most, nearest first) and bins each box's screen rectangle into 32-pixel tiles (8 words of bits a tile, uploaded with the block); a pixel tests only its tile's bits. The authored set, by rule round the camera (made again per 64 m cell, within 220 m): moss and damp along the foot of every castle wall brush (masonry slots 1 / 4) where it meets the ground, leaf litter on the ground at the foot, cracks now and then on the keep's walls; moss and damp round both ends of the stone bridge (omni boxes); dirt and oil on the station's forecourt (`kStationRect`) and the castle car park, mud at the car park's entrance (the nearest the land has to a field gateway). The gun's marks on the still world move into it after their first second (scorch + a hole); the FX pass keeps drawing the fresh glow and marks on moving bodies (`combat.cpp draw_decals`).
- **Small debris placed on the GPU** (species 19..26 = kinds 0..7: twig, leaf, pebbles, needles, cone, litter, gravel, stones - meshes `src/engine/scatter_debris_meshes.inl`; placement: the fourth kind in `plant_gpu.cpp cull_plants` (`lawn` 3), mode `P_CAP.w == 3` of `shaders/tuft_place.comp`; setting `ground_debris`, `VIBE_NO_GROUND_DEBRIS=1`). The grass's machinery: R2 candidates in fixed cells round the camera to 55 m, frustum and depth-pyramid culling, three levels of detail, the same lists and indirect draws, records with a negative size (no fade of their own; the last 40 % of the range shrinks away). One die per candidate picks the kind by rule: litter inside the litter zones - rectangles on the paving itself, the station's forecourt stopping short of the highway's verge and the castle car park (`P_FIELD 0, 1`; `P_FIELD 2` the surface: the car park's slab top from `drive_pads_`, so the litter lies on the slab, not under it); gravel at the edge of what the rules picture clears (roads, paving, buildings); stones at the foot of steep ground (a slope over 0.7 three to six metres uphill); under trees (the home tile's vegetation cover, now plant set binding 9; its G channel is now the conifers' own crowns - `bake_veg_shade_pixels`, the readers of the cover read R) needles (a low heap of 90 needles, 0.7 m across), cones and twigs under conifers, twigs and - by the autumn weight (`P_FADE2.w` = `season_w_[2]`) - leaves under broadleaf trees; a few pebbles and blown autumn leaves in the open. Nothing on cleared ground but litter, nothing in water, none under full snow (5 cm pieces: the grass's snow rule), nothing steeper than 42 degrees. `scatter.vert` lays each piece on the home tile's ground normal (upright beyond it), 12 mm over the ground's close relief, no sway; `scatter.frag` colours it from the record (material 1, solid foliage flag: no leaf transmission, roughness 0.8). Small contact shadows: in the near (16 m) clutter shadow map only (`tuft_shadow.vert` debris path, `clutter_shadow.cpp`); not in the TLAS. `VIBE_TEST_DEBRIS=1` draws every piece magenta (where it lies). The road-edge gravel also works beyond the home tile (the edge of what the near-field bake cuts out, 1.5 m).
- **Checked** (one look each, 1280 x 720 test window): the castle's curtain wall from 10 m - the paste is subtle there (grass covers the contact line), moss patches at the foot after the decal fix; the stone bridge's pier stands in the river (the paste leaves water contact alone); the far capture with `VIBE_TEST_LSD=1` from 350 m up (magenta exactly on the lane's rock formations and round the launch pad: the capture's axes are right); the station's forecourt (litter on the paving, some reaching the highway's verge; an oil stain was a mirror of the sun at gloss 0.28 - now 0.5) and the castle car park (oil and dirt on its slab). GPU: `ground_fx` 0.06 - 0.13 ms a frame (mostly the barrier's wait), the terrain pass unchanged within noise with the capture on (2.25 / 2.25 ms at the high view). **Not checked**: the forest floor under conifers (both tries landed in a dark thicket and on a lake), the debris at road edges outside the station, winter and autumn, the decals' cost alone, a full window redraw's cost, the far capture while moving, the paste on the chapel / sites / office, the indoor ray's misses (a bridge abutment under its deck may count as indoors). The car park's litter lies under its slab (the debris stands on the terrain's height).
- **Second round (same day)**: the debris was only ever placed near the litter zones - the fields gate of `tuft_place.comp` (`P_CAP.w > 1.5`) took the debris' litter rectangles for flower fields; now fields only (`< 2.5`). Seen after the fix: needle mats and twigs under an open conifer stand (VIBE_LOG_GROUND_FX=1 logs such stands: 20 m cells with 3 - 6 conifers, e.g. (-31, -885)); litter on the car park's slab and on the forecourt, none on the highway; a closed moss band at the castle wall's foot with patches above; no oil mirror; the far capture while flying 2 m a frame (`VIBE_TEST_FLIGHT=350,350,200,0,2` - a fifth number, metres a frame forward, added to that hook): strips of 2 pieces drawn, no seam seen (with `VIBE_TEST_LSD=1`). NOT seen: the road-edge gravel (the run was stopped - the machine ran short of memory), the debris' shadows, the abutment under the deck (both views missed it; the second roof ray is in), the chapel's / office's foot only from 15 - 30 m (no hard seam; subtle). While the screen-space displacement was being rewritten by another session it was off in these runs.

## Water after the reference game: wind, swell, surf, caustics, the river's current (2026-11-26)

Written from the survey of the reference game's water shaders (notes outside the repo, `dev/cd-shaders/survey/WATER_SKY.md`); its formulas and constants, our code.

- **Wind on the water** (`environment.cpp` `water_wind_now`, `EnvState::water_wind` / `water_drift`; scene block `water_wind`, `water_swell` - appended at the end): speed from the weather's wind amount (clear 2.8 m/s, cloudy 6, rain 11, storm 28; 7 without the weather), direction the clouds' drift (1, 0.35) veering slowly. `water_swell.yz` is the direction summed over time: every drifting water pattern moves by it (direction x clock swung the patterns round when the wind veered).
