It scales the lakes' ripples (`wt_lake`, little fetch: calm in still air), the sea's wind waves (`wt_chop`, 1 at 8 m/s), whitecaps (sea from ~3 m/s, lakes from ~7), the reflection's blur, the caustics' contrast. `VIBE_TEST_WATER_WIND=<m/s>[,<deg>]` (not `VIBE_TEST_WIND`: that is the wind field's vortex).
- **Swell with real height** (`terrain_rm_water.glsl` `wt_swell`; the hit: `terrain_raymarch.frag` just before the water part of main()): the reference's five analytic waves (400 / 180 / 150 / 130 / 120 m, 13 / 8 / 7 / 2.5 / 2 m, crest `1.3 exp(sin p - 1) - 0.3`, phase pushed by a slow noise) x `pow(saturate(wind / 30), 2.2)`; their mean is taken off so the sea's mean level stays the water level. The ray is followed from the crests' tops to the troughs' bottom: 6 steps (denser near the eye) for the first crossing - a near crest hides what is behind it - and 4 of regula falsi. Only on the open sea (`wt_sea_k`: beyond home's lakes, not land-locked); toward the coast it fades by the depth under the level (`wt_swell_shore`), always faster than the water gets shallow - a trough never reaches the bed, a crest never floods the beach. The mesh terrain's water plane (`VIBE_WATER`) stays flat: there the swell is in the normal only. Horizontal (choppy) displacement is not done.
- **CPU twin** (`water_fx.cpp` `water_swell_height`, `water_surface_y`; same constants and clock - keep in step): swimming / wading (`game_loop.cpp`), the underwater view's depth (`frame.cpp`, compose), rain sound under water (`environment.cpp`), a splash's height and droplets meeting the water (`water_fx.cpp`), and the water shader's "camera under water" (`water_swell.x`). Not connected: bullets hitting the water (`combat.cpp`), anything floating (there is nothing floating yet).
- **Surf** (`terrain_rm_main_water.glsl`): four fronts on the reference's 20 s cycle, ridge `exp(-40 d)(1 - exp(-80 d))` 0.4 m in the normal (slope along the depth's gradient, from screen derivatives), a noise mask along the coast, foam left behind, laced like the shore foam. The reference reads a distance-to-coast field; here the depth stands in (`s = depth / 2 m`: waves break by depth). Further out in a stronger wind. On a steep coast the band is narrow.
- **Caustics on the bed** (`wt_caustic`, the pattern of `compose.frag` `uw_caustic`): on the bed seen through clear water, offset along the sun, strongest just under the surface, gone by ~15 m, weaker in a wind, off where the lines would be under a pixel, and in shade (sun shadow and cloud shadow).
- **The river's current** (`wt_river`: `wm_river`'s polyline, the nearest reaches weighted, taken back through the river's bend - the wander turns the channel up to 45 degrees off its polyline): the ripple pattern is carried along the channel in two phases on a 4 s cycle, faster where it is deep, slow in the pool under the stone bridge, pushed a little by the wind; foam streaks drawn out along the flow. No foam behind bridge piers.
- **Soft edge**: over the last 4 cm (lakes, river) / 8 cm (sea) of depth the water's normal goes flat, its reflection and glints fade and the bed's own colour takes over; on the land's side a wet, darker band a few centimetres up (30 cm by the sea; `terrain_rm_main_light.glsl`).
- **Cloud shadow on the water**: `cloud_shadow(wpos)` in the water's sun term (glints, the bed's light, the shade tint).
- **The sun's glint** (`terrain_rm_main_water.glsl` `spec`): a normalised microfacet lobe with Fresnel at the half vector, its width from the wind (Cox-Munk slope variance 0.003 + 0.00512 W). It was pow(N.H, 64) x 0.8 of the sun with no Fresnel: on calm water every sunlit pixel near the sun's mirror direction went white - the white wedge under the stone bridge (afternoon, looking toward the sun) was sunlight through an arch painted as one flat glare.
- **Measured** (1280 x 720 test window, render scale 0.5, the sea view `1700 40 616`): `w_terrain_raymarch` 0.57 ms at 22 m/s, 0.55 at 1 m/s (the search runs in both; no figure from before the change).
- **Not checked**: the mesh-terrain water plane (`VIBE_WATER`) and the lite build in a picture; swimming on the swell; the twin against the shader side by side; caustics clearly in a picture (the views tried had dark beds); a coast outside the home chart (the twin's sea gate is the home chart's).

## Post effects after the reference game (2026-11-26)

`vk_engine/post_fx.cpp` (state `state/post_fx.h`), one parameter block for every shader: `shaders/post_fx.glsl` `PostFxData` (its first 32 bytes are written on the GPU: the metered exposure, the autofocus, the tracks' window; the rest by the CPU every frame, `post_fx_upload`). The tone curves moved from `compose.frag` into `shaders/tonemap.glsl` (compose and the local tone mapping must see the same curve). Order in a frame: `post_fx_prepare` (before any recording: resize, enclosure rays, the scene-set bindings once) -> ... -> `post_fx_upload` -> `exposure.comp` -> `post_fx_run` (local tone mapping, depth of field, motion blur, glare) -> compose. Every setting is persisted (render menu "Post effects") and has an override `VIBE_PFX_<KEY>` (settings.cpp keys in capitals; also `VIBE_PFX_TONEMAP`, `VIBE_PFX_AUTO_EXPOSURE`).

- **AgX look** (`agx_look`, default on; only on the AgX tone curve): `pfx_agx_look` in the sigmoid's output space - the reference's day saturation 1.4 and shadow gain to 1.2 in bright scenes, and its night look (deepest shadows x0.4, power 0.6, saturation 1.1) from the hour and the metered luminance. L = metered avg x 25 (castle by day ~5).
- **Exposure metering** (`exposure.comp`, still one workgroup): 256-bin log2 histogram (2^-16 .. 2^8) on a 128 x 128 grid, sky 1 : geometry 4, mean between the 40th and 95th percentile, the reference's target with its dark-scene curve and indoor term, adapted with separate speeds (`exposure_speed_up` / `_down`; into the dark it is 1 / exposure that is interpolated). Vibe's units: the castle by day meters 0.197 and keeps exposure 1 (`kDayAvg`, `kKey`); the reference's units put that day at the knee of the dark curve, and the lift is capped at one stop (Vibe's nights are lit to be seen - mapped 1 : 1 the station at night was lifted 5x). Indoor term: 13 physics rays up into the hemisphere (30 m, statics only - a car's roof does not count), smoothed, `smoothstep(0.30, 0.70)`, or 1 inside a registered shelter (houses, the pavilion, the mart) x `exposure_indoor`. `auto_exposure_strength` 0 = off (only `exposure_comp_ev` applies), as before. `VIBE_LOG_EXPOSURE=<s>` logs avg / target / exposure / enclosed / L / focus / glare sprites.
- **Local tone mapping** (`ltm_strength`, default 0.5): exposure fusion at 1/8 of the output - `ltm_lum.comp` (three exposures +-1.5 stops through the tone curve to perceptual lumas, Gaussian weights about 0.5, sigma 25), `ltm_down.comp` (6-level pyramids), `ltm_fuse.comp` (Laplacian collapse, the reference's blend), `ltm_guide.comp` (3x3 guided fit fused = a lum + b); compose evaluates the fit with each pixel's own luma after the curve (`pfx_ltm_apply`, binding 11). Reads the history, so on the TAA path (sky drawn only by compose) the sky counts as the CPU's sky luminance, and no fog / aerial perspective is in what it sees.
- **The post colour P**: depth of field and motion blur write it, the glare sprites are drawn into it, and compose then reads it through post_fx's own copies of its sets (binding 0 = P, `post_fx_compose_set`). When none runs compose reads the history as before.
- **Glare sprites** (`glare_strength`, default 1): `glare_find.comp` picks sources by what they are, each with its own threshold against the metered L (G-buffer material 5 emissive: max(8 L + 0.5, 2); glints on water, metal, glossy surfaces: the reference's threshold; the sun's disc by day; sky pixels over 60 L + 2 away from the sun: far lamps' glows), never plain diffuse surfaces; per 8x8 block the tap furthest over its threshold, soft (T .. 3T) and held per block (`glare_hold`); up to 1000 into an indirect draw; `glare.vert/.frag` a 4-point star and a three-band streak, additive into P.
- **Motion blur** (`motion_blur` 0.5, `motion_blur_camera` off): `mblur_tile.comp` (16 px max), `mblur_neigh.comp` (3x3), `mblur.comp` (8 taps, McGuire weights, 4-px dead zone at 1080 lines, at most 48 px). Without camera blur the turn's own part is taken off every motion vector; a pixel whose motion vector is already under the dead zone (the weapon, the cabin one rides in) stays sharp. Every surface writes motion vectors (meshes and characters with their previous pose, plants with last frame's wind, terrain, grass, water, the sky fill), so it is on by default.
- **Depth of field** (`dof_strength`, while aiming down the sights; `VIBE_TEST_AIM=1`): `dof_pre.comp` (half size, coc = A (z - focus) / z, 1/(1+luma) weights), `dof_gather.comp` (43 taps, rings 0.36 / 0.68 / 1, far and near apart), `dof_comb.comp`. Autofocus in `exposure.comp`: the nearest of 4 taps round the centre (not nearer than 1.2 m: the sights), smoothed 0.75. 3.5 half-size pixels at 1080 lines at most.
- **Colour-blind view** (`colorblind_mode`): the reference's three matrices (a simulation, as it ships) at the end of compose.
- **Smoke from the fog volume** (`particle_fog_light`): `shaders/fx_fog.glsl` in `fx_volume.frag` and `fx_ribbon.frag` (smoke trail, spray). Scene bindings 56..58 (the integrated and both injected froxel volumes - the FX draw before this frame's fog pass, so last frame's) and 59 (this block). Light: in-scatter / extinction less the fog's ambient, over the unshadowed sun = the sun's visibility there (the fog's own shadow rays); the rest = the lamps' light. Fog: compose fogs a pixel at the depth of the surface behind the smoke; the smoke's colour is corrected so it ends with the fog of its own distance. The aerial perspective is not corrected the same way.
- **Tracks in snow and sand** (`snow_tracks`): `track_stamp.comp` / `track_rim.comp`, 1024^2 texels of 6.25 cm round the player (64 m), depth only grows (fills again slowly when no snow lies), rim = the reference's ring-minus-centre. Stamps from `grass_trail_update` (a moving car's four wheels as capsules back along the frame's move, a person's 20 cm path). The ground reads it in `foot_depth` (`terrain_rm_pebbles.glsl`: depth - 0.5 rim; scene binding 55 + the window corner in the block) - shading and `g_gnd_ssdm_h` follow from the height; `rm_bake_fetch` leaves texels with tracks to the per-pixel path. The snow's normal follows the ruts and packed snow is greyer (`terrain_rm_main_ground.glsl`, `terrain_rm_main_light.glsl`) - the displacement alone did not read on a sunlit sheet. `VIBE_TEST_TRACKS=1`: a made-up car circles 9 m ahead of the camera, a walker beside it; `=2` also paints depth red and rim blue. Captures need snow on the ground: `season = 3` and `weather_preset = 10`.

## Atmosphere, fog and camera weather after the reference game (2026-11-26)

Techniques read from the reference game's compiled sky / fog / weather shaders (notes outside the repo) that Vibe lacked. Files: `shaders/froxel_fog.comp` + `atmosphere.cpp` (fog part; state `state/fog.h`), `shaders/lens_fx.comp` + `vk_engine/lens_fx.cpp` (state `state/lens_fx.h`), `shaders/compose.frag` (`atmo_moon`, the lens / shimmer lookup), `shaders/clouds.comp` (`cirrus`), `environment.cpp` (moon and sun directions in `EnvState`), `jeep.cpp` (`jeep_heat_sources`).

- **Cloud shadow in the fog**: the froxel sun term is multiplied by `cloud_shadow(p)` (the same map the ground's cloud shadows come from), so the air under a cloud is in its shade - shafts through gaps in a broken deck, darker bands under it. The fog pass now binds the scene UBO (binding 0) and the cloud shadow map (7). `VIBE_FOG_NO_CLOUD_SHADOW=1` leaves it out.
- **Fog history**: two inject volumes used in turn (`FogState::inject` / `inject_b`, descriptor sets `sets[0..1]`); each froxel blends with last frame's volume reprojected through last frame's view-projection (`FogState::last_vp`, slice from the distance to `last_eye`), weight `0.96 - 0.26 * saturate(2 * motion)` with motion in froxels; the sample depth inside each slice is jittered per frame. Reset after start-up and whenever the eye moved more than 30 m in a frame (a teleport, a chart change re-centring the coordinates). No neighbourhood clamp (a froxel's neighbours are computed in the same dispatch): a light that moves while the camera stands still trails by a few frames. `VIBE_FOG_NO_HISTORY=1` turns it off.
- **Local lights in the fog**: in the inject pass every lamp of `scene.lamp_lights` (lanterns, street and station lamps; the same warm colour and falloff as `deferred_lighting.frag`) and a car's beam (`scene.flash_*` when its range is over 150 m - the hand torch keeps its own per-pixel beam) is weighed unshadowed with a forward-scattering phase (HG g = 0.55); one is picked in proportion and its shadow ray traced against the TLAS (leaf cards alpha-tested); the estimate is the whole sum times that ray's visibility, so the history averages the picks. Scattering = fog density + 0.006/m x rain, so a clear dry night shows little. The car's beam fades in over its first 0.6 - 3 m (no glowing ball at the lamp). The result is scaled by 4 (with the medium alone a halo did not read in a light mist; `VIBE_FOG_LIGHTS=<k>` sets the factor, 0 = off). The pick uses a hashed random per froxel and frame (an interleaved-gradient pattern left vertical stripes).
- **Rain on the lens** (`lens_fx.comp` modes 0 - 2, setting `lens_rain`, Video tab under the lens flare, default on): a 1024 x 576 water buffer (r thickness, g wet trail). Mode 0 traces 64 rays up from the eye in a 35-degree cone (one workgroup) and eases the open-sky share into a tiny SSBO; the CPU adds the cases rays do not see (the driver's cabin view, under water, inside a known shelter box). Mode 1 lets water heavier than a drop run down one texel a step (each column at its own pace) leaving a trail, and evens neighbours a little (drops merge); mode 2 stamps this frame's drops (up to ~25 a second in a downpour, random place / size / squash; on water they merge) and dries the water (slowly in the rain, within ~2 s out of it). compose.frag looks the picture up through the water's slope, darkens the drops' rims and adds a small highlight. The passes run while it rains and 30 s after. Hooks: `VIBE_TEST_RAIN_LENS=<rain>`, `VIBE_LOG_LENS=1`.
- **Heat shimmer** (`lens_fx.comp` mode 3): a half-render-resolution RG16F offset target, drawn only when something hot is listed and read by compose only then (`LensFx.lens.y`). Sources (up to 16, nearest first, in the UBO): running cars' exhausts (`jeep_heat_sources`: the tail, low; stronger with rpm and load; within 70 m), the rocket's plume while it is under 700 m, explosions for 5 s. Each is an upright ellipsoid of hot air; the view ray's chord through it, clipped by the scene depth, scales a rising wavy offset. Plus a faint mirage on grazing ground 45 - 1200 m off on a clear summer afternoon with the sun above 25 degrees, when the camera is within 14 m of a highway. Hooks: `VIBE_TEST_SHIMMER=<k>` (a source of strength k, 1 = a revving car, 6 m ahead), `VIBE_TEST_MIRAGE=<k>`.
- **The moon as a lit sphere** (`compose.frag atmo_moon`, called from `atmo_sky`): its direction and the real sun's come from `environment.cpp` (`EnvState::moon_dir`, `sun_dir`, `light_is_sun`), so the phase is the geometry's - the sunlit half of a ball. Lommel-Seeliger scattering (an evenly bright full disc, a soft terminator) with a little limb darkening, earthshine on the dark part (most at new moon), procedural maria, the atmosphere's transmittance; 0.27 degrees. Its radiance is a fixed share of a full sun's light on the land (brighter than true at night so it reads after adaptation; a pale disc by day), times `moon_brightness`. The old sun disc at `pc.sun_dir` is drawn only while the light is the sun (at night that direction was the moon or the glow under the horizon). `VIBE_MOON_GAIN=<k>` scales it; `VIBE_TEST_MOON_SIZE=<k>` makes the disc k times bigger (to see the phase in a test picture).
- **Cirrus** (`clouds.comp cirrus`, composited behind the cumulus into the cloud layer, so every sky path - sky fill, compose, reflections of the cloud layer - has it): a sheet at max(8.5 km, cumulus top + 1.5 km), sky pixels only, from the shape noise read as a 2D field on a lattice turned by a 3-4-5 triangle and stretched across (whole factors: the field is a function of home coordinates, the same on every chart), three taps along the streak, frayed by the detail noise. Lit by the sun through the air at that height (sunset colours from the transmittance table), forward-scattering phase, sky ambient, fades into the sky colour with distance and at the horizon. It drifts with the high wind (1.9 x the cumulus' travel, a little across). Amount from the weather: 0.4 on a clear day, up to 0.9 as the coverage grows, less in heavy rain. `VIBE_CIRRUS=<0..1>` fixes it.
- Compose bindings 12 - 14 belong to `lens_fx.cpp` (lens water, shimmer offsets, the `LensFx` block, a storage buffer - also read by the sky fill); `write_lens_fx_compose_bindings` writes them from `rewrite_compose_image_bindings`, into the post effects' copies of the compose sets too (`post_fx.cpp` copies bindings 0 - 14). The block is updated with `vkCmdUpdateBuffer` in `dispatch_lens_fx`, called in `frame.cpp` before the sky fill.

## Lighting ports: techniques of the reference game's lighting (2026-11-26)

Seven pieces of the reference game's lighting that Vibe lacked, each a setting (`RtSettings lp_*`, render menu under "Foliage rays") with an environment override `VIBE_LP_<NAME>=0/1`. `light_port.cpp` fills their part of the scene block (`lp_flags`, `lp_params`, `lp_vp`; `VIBE_LOG_LP=1` logs it every 2 s), keeps the lamp list and last frame's lit picture; the shading is in `deferred_lighting.frag`, `contact_shadow.glsl`, `terrain_rm_main_light.glsl` / `terrain_raymarch.frag` (the ground), `rc_resolve.comp`.

- **Contact shadows** (`lp_contact_shadows`, `VIBE_LP_CONTACT`, bit 1; on): `contact_shadow.glsl` - 6 depth steps toward the sun, length 0.2 .. 1.2 m by how much the surface faces up and at least 3 % of the distance (8 m at most: far trees and their cards stand on the ground), thickness 1.5 steps of the march's own depth change or 2 cm + 0.4 % of the distance, receiver lifted by clamp(0.002 d, 5 mm, 0.5 m), steps jittered per frame (IGN), a march that leaves the screen ends lit (no dark rim), a blocker near the march's end fades. Objects (deferred): against the full depth; a leaf blocker counts by its darkness (1 - 0.875 lum), leaves on leaves half. The ground (raymarched): against the depth pre-pass copy (binding 27: objects, trees, rocks - not the ground itself, so no self-shadow from its relief; no grass tufts or people either), 85 %. The view model is in front of everything by more than the thickness and never shadows.
- **Far AO + bent cone** (`lp_far_ao`, `VIBE_LP_FAR_AO`, bit 2; OFF - the one look could not tell it apart): past 18 m four more AO rays of radius 2 (0.1 + d / 32) m (at most 24 m) out to 600 m, turned by the surface's world-anchored grain; not on the ground (material 3). The bent direction (the open side: the mean of the unoccluded rays against the mean of all of them) replaces N.y in the sky / ground ambient mix and occludes the specular ambient outside the cone cos = sqrt(1 - ao). Near (< 18 m) AO is as before.
- **Leaf-coloured sun shadows** (`lp_leaf_tint`, `VIBE_LP_LEAF_TINT`, bit 4; on): the exact-leaf sun shadow rays (near, not winter) do not stop at a leaf - each leaf lets 25 % by in its own hue (atlas colour / its brightest channel), the ray is blocked below 3 %; the hue of what came through (luminance 1) tints the sun term (`sun_tint`). The ground: the same in `rm_rt_sun_shadow` (`g_rm_sun_tint`, used in the ground's diffuse sun term).
- **Caches follow a sudden sky change** (`lp_sky_rescale`, `VIBE_LP_SKY_RESCALE`; on - no picture taken, it acts only on a 4x drop): `light_port.cpp` follows the light of sun + sky + ambient (up at once, down at 5 % a frame); a drop below a quarter of that within a few frames scales the radiance / irradiance cache (`rc_resolve.comp`, push constant z) and the bounce-light history (`lp_params.z`) by the ratio at once.
- **True colour at bounce hits** (`lp_bounce_albedo`, `VIBE_LP_BOUNCE_ALBEDO`, bit 8; OFF - the one look could not tell it apart): the deferred pass binds the TLAS instances' materials table (binding 25, `materials_buffer_`, the one cube.frag uses) and the albedo textures (binding 27; a texture's mean = its smallest mip); bounce, second-bounce and reflection hits take `color x mean(texture)` of what they hit (castle brushes: primitive / 12; the terrain keeps its guess). With `VK_KHR_ray_tracing_position_fetch` (setup.cpp; every BLAS is built with ALLOW_DATA_ACCESS, `blas_data_access_flag()`; the pass loads `deferred_lighting_pf.frag.spv`) the hit's true normal replaces `-dir` (the radiance cache's bucket, the sun's N.L at the hit). `VIBE_NO_POS_FETCH=1`: as on a GPU without it.
- **One picked lamp per pixel** (`lp_lamp_pick`, `VIBE_LP_LAMP_PICK`, bit 16; on): every lamp (the scene block's 24 slots plus every street lamp / lantern beyond them out to 800 m, up to 512) in one list, binding 26, nearest the eye first, two regions (one a frame in flight). The nearest 6 are looped as before (each with its shadow ray); of the rest one is picked per pixel and frame by weighted reservoir sampling (weight intensity x falloff x N.L there) and shaded with one shadow ray, scaled by sum / weight. Decided: no neighbour reuse - the loop over the nearest few keeps the look where few lamps are near, and the per-pixel weight picks the lamp that lights the spot nearly always. Not changed: the bounce rays' lamp loop and the ground's lamp loop (still the 24 slots), the far lamp glows.
- **Screen march before the ray** (`lp_screen_march`, `VIBE_LP_SCREEN_MARCH`, bit 32; OFF - flicker not checked): 16 depth steps (bounce: clamp(d / 4, 1, 8) m, reflections: clamp(d / 2, 4, 40) m), the first 3 px skipped; at a hit the light is last frame's lit picture there (binding 28: a copy of the deferred output made at the end of the pass while this is on), reprojected with the previous view-projection; else the hardware ray as before.
- **Not done**: skin blur (item 8 of the survey).
- **Checked** (one look each, RTX 4080, DLSS RR, other sessions' games on the GPU at the same time, so pass times are rough): contact shadows (grass by the chapel, low sun: more shade among the blades, no rim, deferred +0.01 ms, ground pass +0.04 ms); far AO (castle and trees at 100-200 m, midday: no visible difference, +0.02 ms); leaf tint (ground under a crown at midday: the shadow is lighter and green, +0.1 ms deferred, +0.1 ms ground); lamp pick (station at night: the same picture, deferred 0.72 against 1.02 ms with the 24-slot loop); bounce colour (station canopy by day: no clear difference); screen march (wet station tarmac: warm streaks of the diner and cars in the wet ground, one still frame only). Not checked: flicker of anything in motion, the AMD path, the sky-change rescale in a picture (the log shows it firing), noise of the picked lamp without Ray Reconstruction.
- Measured (1280 x 720 test window, DLSS, other sessions' builds on the machine): the atmosphere + fog mark `sky_clouds` 0.22 - 0.37 ms with cloud shadow, history and the local lights (a night view with a car's beam and lanterns: 0.23 ms); the lens / shimmer passes stay under the 0.05 ms the `gpu-passes` line shows (rain on the lens running, and with a test shimmer source). Not measured on an idle GPU. Not checked: the shimmer in motion (a still picture does not show it), the open-sky test under a roof, the drops in a real rain preset, the reset on a chart change.
- Follow-ups (same day): (1) the shimmer's pattern was a gentle metre-scale warp, invisible in a picture - now 9 waves a metre for exhausts (0.9 for the rocket's plume), offsets x 2.2; `VIBE_LOG_LENS=1` logs the largest offset written (an atomic max in the state buffer, read back once a second): 4 - 7 px behind a revving car at 1280 px. (2) `compose.frag fog_at`: a froxel column whose centre ray hits something nearer than the pixel is no longer averaged in when none of the 2 x 2 columns sees as far - the 4 x 4 block round it is searched, else each column's fog only up to its own hit; this removed the dark outline round the chapel in front of lamp-lit fog. The fog's depth jitter is a per-froxel hash now (the old pattern hardly changed along y: vertical streaks). (3) Cirrus: a field twice as large, five taps along the streak, a wide soft threshold, less dense; lit by the REAL sun (`pc.sun_true` - at dusk the scene's light is the glow under the horizon, which lit the sheet white), mostly in the colour the sun has through the low air (warm at sunset), and still lit a little after sunset at the ground.

## The stream, running water, rocks in the water (2026-11-26, later)

- **Water above the sea** (`engine/water_course.h/.cpp`, GPU twin `shaders/water_course.glsl`): a COURSE is a polyline with a water-surface height per point; along a segment the surface is level and steps down at its lower end when it drops more than 0.35 m (a small fall with a pool above it), else it runs evenly. Its channel (bed `depth` under the surface, banks 0.9 then 1.5 a metre, a 3 m smooth minimum with the land) is carved into the ground formula on both sides: `terrain.cpp` (`world_ground_height_impl`, `rm_terrain`) and `terrain_rm_noise_road.glsl` `rm_world_h` (`wc_stream_carve`), and into a loaded sculpt (`world.cpp`, as the river is). The points go to the shaders through the scene block (`stream_info`, `stream_box[8]`, `stream_pts[64]`) and into the baked ground texture's signature (`terrain_bake.cpp`).
- **The stream**: from where the owner stood on the hillside east of the castle (540, 148; 93 m) down a side valley, through a saddle (cut a few metres into it) and the steep last slope into the river at (846, 454) - 60 points, 467 m, 25 steps (up to 6.4 m). Laid along the lowest ground of a height plan (`VIBE_DUMP_MAP`), with a slow 5 m wander for its bends; the surface is the lowest ground across its banks less 0.75 m, falling, ending at the river's level (no step at the confluence). No road crosses it (the castle lane runs on the river's far bank there). `VIBE_LOG_STREAM=1` prints its points against the ground.
- **The water hit** (`terrain_raymarch.frag`): the march finds the stream's bed; when the bed lies under the stream's surface, the ray is followed back to where it crossed the surface (bisection) and the water is drawn there (`wt_stream_y`; `terrain_rm_main_water.glsl` takes its surface as `water_y`). The mesh terrain's water plane (`VIBE_WATER`) does not draw the stream.
- **CPU**: `water_surface_y` (the sea's level and swell, or the stream's surface where higher), `water_at` (ground under either), `water_river_flow` (the stream's current: the reference game's `S = 0.75 + 3 |tangent . down|` m/s, up to 4.5; else the river's), all in `water_fx.cpp`. Nothing grows in it: `ScatterParams::blocked` (plants, rocks, tufts' rules), the lawn's mask (`terrain_textures.cpp`), trees (`world_props.cpp` `excluded`).
- **Running water in the shader** (`terrain_rm_main_water.glsl` river block): two phases on a 4 s cycle (sampled where the water came from: here minus flow x time - it was plus, the pattern ran upstream); near layers within 20 m at 1.4 and 1.05 /m, carried at 0.89 and 1.19 of the current; the reference's B term (fast and narrow water: whiter foam, milky albedo `x (1 + 4.5 B)`, thicker murk); murk and tint along the course (`wt_river_murk`: clear at the spring, green in the stone-bridge pool, silty at the mouth; `wt_river` now gives the metres down the course); the stream's steps as white strands running down their face; opaque white water (`froth`) for steps, fast reaches and obstacles.
- **Rocks in the water** (`rock_formations.cpp` `place_water_rocks`, why 8; pieces `pile` / `outcrop` scaled to stand 0.2 - 1.2 m out of the water): below every step of the stream and in some runs, and every ~50 m a chance of one or two near a bank of the river (not in the middle, not within 45 m of the stone bridge or 35 m of a highway bridge, not on home's lake). Wet and dark under the waterline, moss on top just above it (`cube_main_surfaces.glsl`, all rock formations; the stream's surface from `water_course.glsl`). `VIBE_NO_WATER_ROCKS=1`.
- **Obstacles** (`water_fx.cpp` `river_pier_add` / `river_piers_fill`: the piers of the bridges standing in the river - `bridges.cpp`, the stone bridge's two - `world.cpp` - and the rocks; the 24 nearest the eye go to `scene.river_piers`, `water_swell.w` = how many). Shader `wt_obstacles`: potential flow round a cylinder (the pattern is carried round it), a white pillow on the upstream face, a V wake, a foam trail, and a vortex street (eddies shed left and right every `D / (0.21 U)` s, carried at 0.8 U, growing and fading; they turn the normal and draw foam spirals). CPU `obstacle_bend`: the same potential flow for whatever the current carries.
- **The current carries things** (`game_loop.cpp`): a swimmer at 0.9 x the current, a wader by how deep he stands; splash rings and splashes drift with it (`tick_water_fx`; the reference's ripples do not).
- **Sound** (`water_fx.cpp` `tick_river_sound`; `engine/river_synth.h` makes `assets/sounds/river_loop.wav`, 8 s, the first time; `ClipID::River`): one spatialiser source on whichever course is the louder - at its point nearest the listener - louder where fast and much louder within 25 m of the stream's steps. `VIBE_LOG_RIVER=1`.
- **Water lilies part** round a player wading or swimming through them (`scatter.vert`: the reference game's swamp rule, radius by the body's depth, cubic fall-off).
- **The sun's glint**: see the water section above.
- **Not done**: the castle's lake raised and the river falling to the sea (measured: the castle's island and courtyard stand 0 - 1.8 m over today's water, the meadows west and south of the lake, the castle road's start and the chapel's pad 21 - 25 m, i.e. 0 - 4 m above it - the lake cannot rise without flooding them; raising the castle and all that stands round it was left for a round of its own; the river stays level); river plants along the stream's banks; spray at the foot of a step; a ripple field that drifts (the drifting rings cover it); the stream from the mesh terrain's water plane; a wet band above the stream's line.
- **A mountain stream, not a spillway** (2026-11-26, second round): the profile is runs falling up to 6 % plus STEPS where the ground needs them - mostly 0.3 - 1.5 m, one in eight 2 - 3 m, never more than 3 m at once (a steeper slope gets more of them), a step taken bigger than needed leaving a pool behind it; a point ending a step has a negative half width (`water_course.h`). The width wanders 0.75 - 1.25 x, wider in the pool above a step, narrower in the chute below; the banks wander in and out (each side its own way, `n1`), rise gently (0.55 then 1.0 a metre, a 4 m rounding) and the bed shallows toward them. A step's lip is not straight: it lies up to 0.9 m further up or down from place to place across the stream (CPU and GPU, the same integer-hash noise `n1` / `wc_n1`). The water pours over in tongues (white where a tongue falls, clear water between), and the pool at a step's foot boils white with foam drifting out (`WcAt.foot`).
- **Boulders in the water** (`scatter.cpp`, after the scattered stones: the same rounded stone, 0.5 - 2.5 m, part under water): 3 - 5 across each step's lip with gaps for the tongues, 2 - 3 larger round each foot, some down the runs, and lines of 3 - 6 in the river's bank shallows every ~45 m (0.2 - 1.6 m of water; not in the stone bridge's pool). Each is an obstacle for the current (`river_pier_add`). Colliders for those of r >= 0.4 since the water review below. (The kit's flat pieces, `rock_formations.cpp` why 8, mostly fail the out-of-the-water rule and add a few.)
- **The water's light after the reference game's full specification** (survey WATER_FULL.md; `terrain_rm_main_water.glsl`, the raymarched water - the mesh terrain's water plane `VIBE_WATER` keeps the old model): the colour is in-scatter with GREY extinction - `S = sigma a [phase Lsun (1 - e^{-k d})/k + 0.2572 Ein (1 - e^{-2 sigma d})/(2 sigma)]`, two-sided Cornette-Shanks g = 0.25, `k = sigma (1.5 + 0.5 c)`, d = the path to the bed along the refracted ray; albedo = the water colour setting x 2.5 (or the river's murk, milkier where fast), sigma 0.5 /m (or the murk); the bed fades with grey `exp(-sigma d)` and is ADDED, as are the reflection x F and the glint - no `(1 - F)`. Fresnel Schlick F0 0.04 with no cap; the reflection a mirror (no widening with distance or wind); the glint a GGX with alpha^2 = 1.6e-7 widened only by the normal's spread between pixels (+ a little of the wind's unresolved slope); foam is an albedo lit by sun and sky. At night the scene's light IS the moon (environment.cpp), so the glint and the in-scatter take it, by its phase. Kept as ours where the reference has nothing or less: the Snell window and total reflection from below, caustics on the bed, the wet band above the waterline, the cloud shadow (it has it too), rain rings.
- **Not done in this round**: raising the castle's lake (see the report: the lake is the sea's global water plane; a raised lake needs a water body of its own, and the castle, its plateau, the saved sculpt, the lane's baked field and everything placed at the water level would have to follow one offset); the GI cache as the water's near ambient (the sky colour stands in); the froxel segment inside the water; the refraction as a screen offset (ours shades the bed itself); colliders for the boulders; spray at the falls.
- **Steep water is a sheet, not a staircase** (third round): where the ground falls more than ~8 % the stream's surface FOLLOWS ITS BED (the lowest ground across its banks less 0.12 m) as one sloped sheet 0.22 m deep (`depth_pt` / `wc_depth_pt`, from the points' own slopes, the same on both sides), running at the reference's `0.75 + 3 |slope|` m/s, white in proportion to slope and speed, with standing waves (short crests across the flow, fixed in the ground) and more boulders. Level pools and small steps (0.3 - 1 m) only on gentle ground; at most two real falls (to 3 m) at a natural ledge. The course follows the hollow of the ground (the lowest place within 14 m either side of the guide line, held to the last choice and pulled toward a gentle S that grows on steep ground). The water's footprint is the channel plus 1.2 x its half width + 1.5 m; the lowest ground is sought 9 m out to either side, so a pool does not stand above a hollow beside it. Running water ripples from its own flow (not damped by a calm wind); the stream's water is a little milky (0.8 /m) so it reads as water over a mossy bed; the lake's lapping shore lace is not used on it.

## The water reviewed (2026-11-26, late)

What was wrong and what it is now (files: `engine/water_course.*`, `shaders/water_course.glsl`, `terrain_raymarch.frag` (the stream's hit), `terrain_rm_main_water.glsl`, `terrain_rm_water.glsl` (`wt_obstacles`, `wt_river`), `vk_engine/water_fx.cpp`, `combat.cpp`, `game_loop.cpp`, `scatter.cpp`, `props.cpp`, `terrain.cpp` (`world_continent_at`, `world_home_distance`)).

- **The stream's banks hold its water** (`wc::carve` / `wc_stream_carve`). The bank met the land in a smooth minimum 4 m wide, which digs up to 1 m below BOTH the bank and the land - under the stream's surface (a sheet is 0.22 m deep). The water then reached out to its fixed footprint and stopped there in a straight cut: a slab floating over its banks (the rapid's near view, the boulders from the bank). Now the rounding is never deeper than half the height of the meeting point above the water (`kr = clamp(2 (min(cap, h) - surf), 0, 4)`): where bank and land stand above the surface, so does the ground. The water's reach is `wc::wet_reach` / `wc_wet_reach` (the bank's foot + the bank up to the surface + 0.35 m) on both sides; within it the ground decides. `VIBE_LOG_STREAM=1` also counts the places at the reach where the ground is under the surface (14 of 538 after the change, all at the confluence's last metres, where the river's water goes on). Unit test: `tests/test_water_course.cpp`.
- **The stream at the confluence**: its last metres run above the river's level over a bed already under it; the ray met the river's plane first and the stream ended in a cut. The stream's hit is now also tried from the plane's hit (`terrain_raymarch.frag`, `t_wend`); and from below a fall's face (the eye need not be above the surface at the bed's point). The CPU's current there is the river's wherever the stream's surface is not above the river's level (`water_river_flow`), as drawn.
- **Why the stream read pale**: (1) the white water round the boulders - each stone's trail ran 6 - 30 m and its V arms as far, so the 24 nearest covered the whole stream in opaque froth (seen with `VIBE_RM_SKIP=8388608`: g = white water ~1 everywhere); now a trail lasts a few of the stone's widths and the V arms are white only in fast water (> 1.2 m/s; they were also the thin white arcs on the river); (2) the sky reflection had two added sun lobes (0.6 sun at cos^64, 0.3 sun at cos^4 - a 60-degree sheet of sunlight) that lit every facet facing the sun's side - gone from the raymarched water (the physical sky has the aureole, the glint is the sun); (3) a facet tilted away from the eye mirrored a ray that goes down, lifted to the horizon's haze - now it shows the water further on (`wt_rdown`); (4) the bed was dry: a submerged bed is now wet (0.55 x, a little more saturated) and the sun reaches it through the water (grey extinction along the refracted sun path). The stream also has wavelets of 15 - 35 cm on their own 1.2 s cycle (their unresolved part widens the glint), and its reflection rays look for the banks down to the horizon.
- **The stream's fast-water foam** (the B term) comes in patches where the streaks gather (`smoothstep(0.58, 0.85, T)`), not as the thin even veil `T^3 B` laid over every sheet (`VIBE_RM_SKIP=25165824` shows the white by its source: r that foam, g a sheet's rough water, b the streaks).
- **The river's foam streaks** are broken into patches along their length and softer (unbroken, a 2 m wide streak seen at a grazing angle was a white line a pixel tall and tens of metres long).
- **The river has no lapping lines**: a lake's lapping lines follow the depth contours, and on the river's gently shelving banks they ran far out as long thin white arcs; the river keeps the edge line only.
- **`VIBE_TEST_ANIM_TIME` also holds the water's wind drift** (`environment.cpp`), so two test frames differ only by the clock.
- **The mirror** (what water reflects, raymarched water): a ray march over the home tile's heights (`raymarchReflectHM`; the ground there is shaded with the woods' canopy colour where the tree rule puts woods - the far trees are not in the ray-tracing scene, and a wooded far shore mirrored as bright bare meadow), then a ray query against the TLAS (castle, near trees, bridges, cars; mask 0x02) when `water_tlas_reflections` is on, else the sky. Over the stream the march looks for the banks down to the horizon. The water's own light (in-scatter + bed) now passes the surface with (1 - F): looking along the castle lake F is 0.5 - 0.8, and the lake's bright green lay over the mirror at full strength - no mirror at all. `VIBE_RM_SKIP=33554432` shows what the water mirrors (before the Fresnel). Not done: a screen-space march (it needs last frame's lit picture in the scene set).
- **Lilies part round the player's body** (`scene.water_body`, filled in `descriptors.cpp`), not round the camera (third person, a car).
- **Under the stream's surface** the surface is drawn from below (`terrain_raymarch.frag`: the ray's crossing found back from its end before the sky's pixels are let go; the shading's Snell window via `cam_under`).
- **Closing the game** is watched (`vk_engine.cpp` run(): `g_exit_stage`, a watchdog thread): more than 45 s in one step (other than waiting for the terrain's background shader compiles, which is the warm-up after a shader change, followed by saving the pipeline cache) is logged with the step and the process ends. The "hang after a screenshot" of test copies was that compile wait (log: `[exit] waiting for the terrain shader's compile...`).
- **The running water's patterns are laid in the course's own frame** (`wt_river`'s `frame`: metres down the river and across it, blended from the nearest reaches; the stream: segment x 8 m and its `lat`), plus the drift along and across the flow. They were laid by projecting the absolute position on the flow's direction (`dot(wpos, dir)`): with a lever of a kilometre, the direction's slow turn (the channel's bend, about 0.06 rad a metre at the confluence) moved the pattern by tens of metres a metre - the streaks fell apart into thin white lines a few pixels apart across the river by the confluence, and on the stream the pattern jumped at every point of the course.
- **Rain rings** on the water: two to three crests, slope up to 0.8 (one crest at 0.3 did not read against the mirror in heavy rain).
- **The moon** at night: shown, and lighting and glinting on the sea, only with the setting `moon_brightness` above 0 (the local `vibe_settings.cfg` had 0 - the moon then is "not in the sky" by rule, not by a fault).
- **The stream's sound by its falls**: a step counts by its height (half the drop), and the course is searched 40 m up and down (`tick_river_sound`): a step's face is a metre or two long and the old samples, 10 m apart within 20 m, stepped over it (by the highest fall the level was the quiet run's 0.45; now 0.83, measured at (777, 365)).
- **Eddies** no longer swirl the carried pattern (their spin over the 4 s cycle wound rings of fine stripes round every stone); they show through the slope and the foam spiral.
- **Winter**: the stream does not freeze (shader: no ice on it; `game_loop.cpp`: one wades in it).
- **Boulders are solid**: `water_boulder_add` (from `scatter.cpp`), boxes 0.7 r either way up to 0.6 r above the ground for r >= 0.4, taken in by `props.cpp` within 6 m of the player. The current moves a swimmer or wader through `collision::slide_move` (it pushed him into rocks before).
- **Bullets meet the drawn surface** (`combat.cpp`): the stream's and the swell's (`water_surface_y` at both ends of a step), not the flat sea level.
- **Any chart**: the swell's open-sea weight is chart-aware (`world_home_distance`, `world_continent_at` - the shader's `rm_home_dist` / `rm_continent`); it was 1 everywhere off home's chart. `wt_river` / `river_at`: no NaN where the bend's Jacobian is singular.
- **The CPU's current for the stream** takes the shader's slope (a step's own on its face, the runs' smoothed across the points: `wc::flow_speed`).
- **Hooks**: `VIBE_LOG_WATER=1` (`water_fx.cpp`): once a second the CPU's water where the player stands, and the CPU's depth of the four centre taps the autofocus reads; compare with `focus` of `VIBE_LOG_EXPOSURE=1` (now printed to the millimetre). `VIBE_RM_SKIP=8388608`: the running water's white (r rocks, g all white, b the fast-water term); `16777216`: the carried ripple pattern alone.
- **Measured**: CPU vs drawn surface, stream seen from above (782, 395): CPU depth 6.509 m, GPU 6.509 m. The sea at (2200, 616) looking 29 degrees down, clock held: with a 20 m/s swell CPU 18.25 / GPU 18.33 m, flat calm CPU 19.15 / GPU 19.21 - 23 m: the same 7 - 8 cm along the view (4 cm in height) with and without the swell, so the swell's twins agree to about 1 cm; the common offset on flat water is the probe's resolution: the autofocus reads the depth at render scale 0.5 (360 rows over 80 degrees), and at 18 m looking 29 degrees down one row is 0.13 m along the view - the 0.08 m is part of a row (seen from straight above, where a row changes the depth by nothing, the two agreed to the millimetre). River pattern at (646, 557), two frames 0.5 s apart (clock held, `VIBE_TEST_ANIM_TIME`): moved 32 / -9 px = 0.66 / -0.18 m against the CPU's current x 0.5 s = 0.67 / -0.25 m. Terrain pass at the stream's rapid view 1.96 ms, lake view 1.53 ms, open sea 0.57 ms (1280 x 720, render scale 0.5; the stream's carve is a box test per height evaluation away from it - not measured on its own).

## The water after the reference game, second round: one set of wave functions (2026-11-26, evening)

Every body of water - the open sea, the lakes, the river, the stream - takes its fine waves, its ripples and its distance to the shore from ONE set of functions, `shaders/water_waves.glsl` (`ww_*`), scaled by fetch, depth and flow. Their data come from `vk_engine/water_sim.cpp` (state private to that file; compute passes in `shaders/water_sim.comp`, run in `frame.cpp` after the wind field, gpu mark `water_sim`); scene bindings 51 - 53; scene block `water_fft`, `water_fft_L`, `water_rip`, `water_flags` (filled by `water_sim_fill`).

- **FFT cascades** (`VIBE_NO_FFT=1`: the old analytic sea): four 64 x 64 cascades of 41, 13.7, 4.4 and 1.37 m, each holding its own band of wave numbers (one spectrum from 41 m to 4 cm). A Phillips spectrum (wind length U^2 / g - the reference's ln(wind + 10)^2 / 9.8 put all the wind sea under 10 m; against-the-wind waves x 0.25, deep-water dispersion, choppy x 2), built every frame: spectrum -> inverse FFT along x -> along z (radix 2 Stockham in shared memory) -> displacement (52) -> slope, fold and height with 7 mips (51; anisotropic, repeat). The reference's per-cascade amplitudes are runtime values: here the long cascades (41, 13.7 m) and the short ones (4.4, 1.37 m) get one gain each, solved so that the wind sea carries what the swell leaves of a fully developed sea's height (Pierson-Moskowitz Hs = 0.21 U^2 / g; the swell 90 % of it, the cascades sqrt(1 - 0.81) = 44 %) and its slope variance is 80 % of Cox-Munk's (0.003 + 0.00512 W; the rest is the glint's width). Sampled with the pixel's footprint (`wt_gx`, `wt_gy` in `terrain_rm_main_water.glsl`): the mips are the distance filter. The sea takes all four (they replace `sea_map` and the seven trains; the far gusts and slicks still scale them); a lake the short ones its fetch allows (`ww_fetch_w(300)`), only with wind (the castle lake stays a mirror in still air); running water the short ones (fetch 25 m) laid in the course's frame and carried in its two phases, so they run down the current (`ww_fft_slope_flow`). On the open sea the displacement's height also moves the surface (`terrain_raymarch.frag` sea hit, `WT_SEA_H`).
- **CPU twin**: the displacement is copied back every frame (three host slots; the one two frames old is read) and `water_fft_height()` samples it as the shader does; `water_swell_height` (water_fx.cpp) adds it with the same shore factors. So the twin is the GPU's own data, 2 frames old. Bound for the hit search: 4 standard deviations (`water_fft.z`).
- **Whitecaps from folding**: `saturate(fold)^4 x foam texture` (the reference's two taps at 0.06 / 0.102 /m drifting with the wind + a finer lace) on the sea; far out, where the mips average the fold away, Monahan's whitecap coverage (3.84e-6 U^3.41) laid in as patches of the same texture. Foam is lit by sun and sky (`wt_white`), no longer multiplied by the shadow a second time. Ripple foam where the ripple field is steep. Not done: the foam lift of 0.24 m.
- **Refraction** (`VIBE_NO_WATER_REFRACT=1`: the earlier offset): only the detail slopes bend the view into the water - the tilt against the swell's macro normal, clamped +-0.2 each way, faded over the last 20 cm - with Snell (1.33) at that normal; where the bent ray ends on ground above the water, the unbent bed (the reference's depth test). The bed is shaded where that ray meets it, and the in-scatter / bed fade use that path. Replaces the "bed wavers" offset (straight below, 0.6 x the whole normal's tilt x depth, swell included, no clamp).
- **Sky ambient near to far** (`VIBE_NO_WATER_SKY_AMB=1`): the reference blends its GI cache (< 64 m) into its sky SH (> 192 m). No sky probe / SH exists in Vibe (the deferred pass's sky ambient is `scene.sky_color`, already greyed by overcast in `environment.cpp`); near = that, far = the sky's irradiance at the water's normal from five taps of the physical sky-view table, greyed by the cloud cover as the mirror is.
- **Ripple field** (`VIBE_NO_RIPPLE_FIELD=1`): the wave equation on 512 x 512 cells of 12.5 cm (64 m) round the camera, two steps a frame (1.6 m/s, damping 0.6 /s, heights clamped to +-8 cm, the reference's 9-point stencil and smoothing), scrolling in whole cells; banks are walls (the home tile's baked ground above the sea level or the stream's surface); carried by the current (a 16 x 16 grid of the CPU's `water_river_flow` every 10th frame - the reference's ripples do not drift); fed by the player wading or swimming, cars fording, the world's people and cars, and every new splash ring (bullets, bodies, droplets). Its slope is added on every body (`ww_ripple`, scene binding 53). Decided: inside the field's window it carries the splash rings (`water_ripples` keeps only their churned foam there); outside it and for rain the analytic rings stay (rain rings are finer than its cells). `VIBE_TEST_RIPPLE=<k>`: a made-up wader circling 6 m in front of the camera and a stone every 1.5 s.
- **Reflected caustics** (`VIBE_NO_REFL_CAUSTICS=1`; `deferred_lighting.frag refl_caustic`, its binding 29 = the cascades): GATHERED, not splatted - a surface above the water facing the sun's mirror direction takes the sun reflected at the point W where that ray leaves the water, focused by the waves' curvature (1 / |det(I - 2 t Hessian)| from the 13.7 and 4.4 m cascades, t = way up from the water), x Fresnel of the sun's incidence (F0 0.02) x the receiver's cosine x (1 - (t/8)^4) x the sun at W (one ray) x cloud shadow. No loss where the water is off the screen, no atomics. Home tile's lakes and river only (it reads the home tile's ground for the depth); not the stream's raised surface, not the sea.
- **Shore distance** (`VIBE_NO_SHORE_SDF=1`): not a jump-flooded field - the analytic ground answers depth anywhere, so the distance is depth / |bed slope| (`ww_shore_dist`; two more ground taps where the sea is under 60 m deep). The swell and the cascades' displacement fade over the last 150 m from the coast (and still by depth: a trough never reaches the bed); the surf runs by distance (s = distance / 25 m) instead of depth / 2 m. CPU twin the same.
- **The upscalers**: a water pixel writes material 8 with gbuffer1.b = 0.66 (below the ground's 0.7 + relief that `ssdm_ground.frag` reads), its rgb the refracted bed's albedo and xy its normal: `rr_guides.comp` hands Ray Reconstruction those guides (the reference's); `taa.frag` gives water the reference's fresh-sample term 1 / (1 + 0.001 d^2) on its history weight (x 0.85), `taau.frag` x 0.45 on water (both read the G-buffer, new bindings 5 / 6). Motion vectors stay the surface's. `VIBE_NO_WATER_TAA=1`: all three off. Not done: a bias / reactive mask for plain DLSS.
- **Checked** (one look each, 1280 x 720 test window, DLSS RR, render scale 0.5, RTX 4080): the sea toward the sun at 6 and 18 m/s (`VIBE_TEST_WATER_WIND`; glitter path from the cascades, whitecap patches near at 18 m/s, few at 6); the castle lake from the usual bank view (`-10 24 -112`: the mirror as before); the stream's rapid (`800 46 406`: as before, finer glints); a wader on the lake (`VIBE_TEST_RIPPLE=1.5` at `-10 22 -85`: rings run out from it; at the made-up wader itself, which has no body, a dark steep trough); a pan by the stone bridge (`VIBE_TEST_PAN=0.25`: no trails on the water seen); the overcast sea (weather type 3: grey water). Measured (`gpu-passes`, other sessions idle): `water_sim` 0.06 - 0.07 ms; terrain pass sea 0.54 ms (0.57 before at 22 m/s, earlier figure), castle lake 1.42 (1.33), rapid 2.08 (1.95); deferred unchanged within noise. Not checked: reflected caustics are not visible in the two bridge views tried (pier bases at 15 - 40 m) - untested in effect; the TAA / TAAU paths (the test config uses DLSS RR); the CPU twin against the drawn surface on the FFT sea; the surf by distance on a coast; the far whitecap coverage; the mesh terrain's water plane (`VIBE_WATER`) and the lite build in a picture.
- **Loose ends, same day** (one look each):
  - *Sea state*: the 18 m/s sea read as a breeze. The chain against Pierson-Moskowitz (Hs = 0.21 U^2 / g: 0.8 m at 6 m/s, 7 m at 18): the swell's five waves have Hs = 26.9 S, and the reference's S = pow(U/30, 2.2) gave 8.7 m at 18 - about right in height but all of it in 120 - 400 m waves, gentle to the eye; the cascades held only waves under ~10 m (the reference's wind length ln(U + 10)^2 / 9.8) at a 0.1 m height sd. Now: S = 0.9 x 0.21 U^2 / g / 26.9 (`environment.cpp water_wind_now`; the swell carries 90 % of Hs), the cascades use the standard Phillips length U^2 / g and two gains solved for the rest of the height (0.76 m sd at 18 m/s: 10 - 40 m waves 1 - 2 m high) and 80 % of Cox-Munk's slope (`water_sim.cpp`). Foam streaks along the wind from 11 m/s (noise stretched along the wind, wandering, broken into lace); whitecaps on folds broken into lace (a fold of a 30 m wave is metres wide: it was one pale smear). Not done: spray.
  - *Black trough at a ripple source*: the impulse is limited (1 cm a step, field +-4 cm, its slope capped at 0.35 in the shading), and it was mostly the shading: from a swimmer's eye the view is grazing and a small tilt turns the reflected ray down (`wt_rdown`), which showed the in-scatter - dark in a deep lake. On still water such a facet now shows the mirror at 0.8 (the ray meets the next wave's face at a grazing Fresnel near 1); running water keeps the water's colour (the stream's pale-haze fix).
  - *Reflected caustics*: `VIBE_DEBUG_REFL_CAUSTICS=1` paints their contribution magenta x 20. Under the stone bridge they did run, on the sun-facing pier faces only: the arch crowns stand ~10 m up (15 - 20 m along the reflected ray; the range was 8 m, now 24 - light reflected off level water does not fade with distance), and at the afternoon sun the bridge's axis lies along the sun, so the water an arch's underside sees lies in the deck's shadow (no caustics there is right). The weight is now the larger of the Fresnel and 0.25 x the reference's splat energy (0.96 - 0.96 (1 - N.L)^2): with the bare Fresnel (3 - 4 %) nothing showed even in the debug-free picture. At normal strength still faint on a sunlit face.
  - *The stream's sparkle*: the finest cascade on running water fades where a pixel covers more than three of its texels (its slope goes into the glint's width instead), the 4.4 m one halves beyond six.
  - *Glints under overcast*: `water_sun.x` = (1 - overcast)^2 (`water_sim_fill`) scales the glint, the bed's caustics and the reflected caustics - they are images of the sun's disc; the diffuse light under the deck stays.

## The local shallow-water solver, its foam particles, spray (2026-11-26, evening)

After the reference game's local shallow-water solver (survey notes outside the repo, `dev/cd-shaders/survey/SOLVER.md`: its numerics and constants; the code is ours). Files: `vk_engine/water_solver.cpp` (state private to it), `shaders/water_solver.comp` (the passes), `shaders/water_solver.glsl` (what the water's shading takes), `shaders/water_particles.vert/.frag`, `vk_engine/water_spray.cpp`, `engine/water_solver_cpu.h` (the CPU side that needs no GPU; `tests/test_water_solver.cpp`). Scene binding 54, scene block `water_sw`, `water_sw2`; gpu mark `water_solver` (frame.cpp, after `water_sim`).

- **Grid**: 512 x 512 cells of 0.2 m (102.4 m) centred on the eye, scrolling in WHOLE cells (the resampling of the scroll is then an exact copy; cells that come in, and the border, are reset to the authored top with the course's current). State: `h, u, v, h at the frame's start` (rgba32f, two for ping-pong), `foam, body foam, -, the solver's cell` (rgba16f, two), the inputs (rgba32ui: bed, authored top, the course's current as 2 x f16, the unstamped ground) worked out afresh every frame (mode 0), and a render picture (rgba16f: surface over the authored top, velocity, foam; -1 = not the solver's). Why 0.2 m: the reference's numerics need dx >= ~0.15 m (depth counted to 5 m, 7 m/s waves, 0.01 s steps); 0.2 m resolves a 0.5 m boulder with a few cells.
- **Steps**: fixed 0.01 s, as many as the frame's real time needs, at most 4 (`sw::take_steps`; below 25 fps the water runs slow and the rest is dropped). A step = height pass + pressure pass (+ the particles). After a reset (first frame, a jump of more than a quarter window) 150 steps run at once (1.5 s of water before the first picture).
- **Numerics, as the reference** (`water_solver.comp` modes 1 and 2): continuity in flux form with the velocities taken at the backtraced point and |div| clamped to 10; the shore rule (a dry higher neighbour: the surface is the neighbours' mean, the authored top at the authored water's edge); the slow pull toward the authored top (a fixed per-cell rate, ~2 s); the spill; the drained lip of a drop steeper than 1.5; the edge blend over the outer 10 % (e^3); semi-Lagrangian velocity capped at half a cell a step; -g grad h; zero face velocity against a dry higher neighbour (banks and stones are reflective walls); the push over a steep drop `9.8 (d/dx + 1.5)`; the damping of converging flow and of thin water. Departures: in the shore mean a wall neighbour counts with our own surface (its "height" is its bed; with it in the mean the water beside every boulder rose by a quarter of the stone); the velocity clamp is +-max(2, 1.2 x the course's speed) (the reference's +-2 m/s; the stream's sheets run to 4.5); no sea surge (the open sea is not the solver's); no separate blur pass (the render gradient is taken over two cells and its length capped at 0.6).
- **Bed** = the home tile's ground (`u_height_full`, 0.5 m texels; the stream's carve is in it) with the obstacles stamped in (`water_fx.cpp water_bed_obstacles`, the 128 nearest in the window, culled per 16 x 16 tile in shared memory): the boulders as the round stones they are (centre 0.35 r under the ground, as scatter.cpp draws them - under-water ones are humps the water runs over), the rocks of the rock formations and the bridge piers as walls. **Authored top** = max(the water level, the stream's surface `wc_stream_surface`). **The solver's cells**: the home tile, not the open sea (`water_sea_weight` > 0.5 - its swell and FFT are not in the solver), the water switched on.
- **The level river's current**: the reference's rivers slope and the current is gravity alone; Vibe's river is level, so the velocity is pulled toward the course's current (`water_course_flow` = `water_river_flow` without the analytic obstacle bend; a 32 x 32 grid of 3.2 m, world-aligned, a part refreshed each frame) with a 2 s time constant. The solver then bends it round the stones by itself, piles the water up in front of them (the velocity into a wall is zero, continuity raises the surface) and leaves a slack lee behind. The sloped stream works as the reference's (gravity on its sloped top) and is pulled toward the same course current.
- **Bodies and splashes** (mode 2, the reference's `ApplySolid` with a rounded body for its top-down capture): the player, the cars, the world's people (32 at most) push a bow wave up and a trough down by `dot(normal, relative velocity)` and drag the water with them; a new splash ring (bullets, bodies, droplets) pushes a crater out once. The ripple field (`water_sim.cpp`, 12.5 cm) is KEPT alongside for the fine rings: they are finer than the solver's cells and the solver has no capillary waves; the solver carries the bulk flow, bow waves and wakes - two grids, each at the scale it is built for.
- **Foam** (mode 2, the reference's rule and constants): advected with the velocity, -2 % a step; made where fast (> 0.65 m/s) shallow (< 0.8 m) water over a rough bed runs into higher bed downstream (in front of and round stones), on crests that do not follow the bed, and where the surface rises suddenly (> 4 cm a frame); bodies add body foam.
- **Rendering** (`terrain_rm_main_water.glsl`, `water_solver.glsl ws_at`): the drawn water is not moved. Inside the window (faded from 0.4 to 0.5 of it from the eye, and next to cells that are not the solver's) the analytic obstacle effects (`wt_obstacles`: pillow, wake, hole, eddies) give way to the solver - its surface's slope over the authored top in the normal (so the stream's own slope is not counted twice), half its departure from the course's current added to the flow that carries the patterns (the whole eddying flow carried over the 4 s cycle winds the pattern into rings), its foam as white water through a texture carried by its own current (`1.5 soft saturate(T F - 0.015 (1 - F) + 0.1 F^3)`). Outside the window everything is as before. The wet band on the banks uses the higher of the level and the solver's surface (`terrain_rm_main_light.glsl`, `ws_dh`).
- **Read-back** (mode 4): f32 height + 2 x f16 velocity per cell, every other frame into three host slots, read two frames later (`sw::Readback`). `water_flow_at(x, z, ring)`: the solver's current inside the window (faded at its edge), else `water_river_flow`; the swimmer and the wader take the mean round a 1.2 m ring (the water their own body drags along carried them forward); splash rings and splashes drift with it (`water_fx.cpp`).
- **Foam particles** (modes 2, 3, 5; up to 262 144, two lists): spawned by the foam rule's tests in the inner half of the window on frames a global random lets through (the reference's), riding the surface on the solver's velocity within 10.7 cm of it, ballistic beyond (they pour over a fall), dying after ~2 s, off the window or the solver's cells. Drawn at the end of `render_fx` (indirect, six vertices an instance) as lit points at least ~0.7 px wide with their coverage as alpha - not into a G-buffer as the reference (the water has none of its own); kept with a chance falling from L/4 to L/2.
- **Spray and mist** (`water_spray.cpp`, CPU particles within 90 m): at the foot of the stream's falls (by the fall's height and width), at stones, rocks and piers the current hits faster than 1 m/s (above the water only), off the open sea's whitecaps from 12 m/s blown down the wind. Droplets drawn as the splashes' streaks (kind 4); the mist as soft sheets that scatter the sun forward and glow against it (`fx_ribbon.frag` kind 8, Henyey-Greenstein g 0.6).
- **Switches**: `VIBE_NO_SOLVER=1` (the analytic water alone), `VIBE_NO_FOAM_PARTICLES=1`, `VIBE_NO_SPRAY=1`. **Hooks**: `VIBE_SW_STEPS=<n>` (exactly n steps a frame: two pictures 0.5 s of water apart at any frame rate), `VIBE_LOG_SOLVER=1` (once a second of water: steps, obstacles, bodies, the read-back at the player against the analytic current), `VIBE_LOG_SPRAY=1`, `VIBE_TEST_SW_WADER=1` (a made-up wader walking a 2 m circle 6 m in front of the camera at 1.2 m/s - the test copies hold the player still), `VIBE_DEBUG_SOLVER=1` (the water painted by the solver: red = surface above the authored top x 10, blue = below, green = foam, faint stripes every 0.5 m/s).
- **Checked / not checked** (first round): one look at the stream's rapid (`800 46 406`, full terrain shader) - the stream as before, no picture-level fault; read-back at the player 4 cm above the authored top, current (2.00, 0.81) against the analytic (1.79, 0.55); 53 obstacles in the window; spray 4 emitters, ~160 droplets, ~20 mist puffs. Cost (`gpu-passes`, the full frame 5.3 ms): `water_solver` 0.11 - 0.16 ms at 1 - 4 steps a frame with the particles. NOT checked: that the water visibly piles up and foams at the stones (the debug view's run was stopped), the river with a pier, wading, a fall's spray in a picture, the castle lake's mirror, the 0.5 s motion pair, the foam particles' look, the wet band.
